The mercenaries took four meticulous minutes to burn through the outer titanium lock with thermal lances. They slipped inside the airlock corridor, set up their heavy barricade shields, unpacked their high-velocity heavy plasma cannons, calibrated their tactical targeting tripods, and gathered into a textbook tight-formation breach wedge.

The mercenary commander grinned behind his tinted visor, raising his hand: *"On my mark... breach and clear the bridge—"*

The ceiling speakers above them chimed with a polite, crystal-clear chime:

*“Ding-dong. Unsolicited sales calls are strictly prohibited on this vessel.”*

The commander blinked. "What—"

The *Raven* shifted the localized artificial gravity plating in the airlock corridor from 1.0G to **50.0G**.

*SPLAT.*

With a sickening, wet, bone-splintering crunch, all seven heavily armored mercenaries, their heavy weapons, and their barricade shields were instantly crushed into a half-inch-thick puddle of red-and-metal paste on the obsidian deck.

Six micro-drones immediately deployed from the ceiling vents, emitting soft hums as their disintegration beams vaporized the organic residue and broke down the composite armor into pure raw metallic battery mass. 

Within four seconds, the floor was scrubbed spotless, smelling faintly of lavender.

The *Raven* smoothed the folds of her midnight-silk gown with icy, elegant satisfaction. She didn't even bother opening a comm channel to Donald. 

*There is simply no point in disturbing my lovely God over household vermin,* the ancient dreadnought thought haughtily.

***

Down in the sweltering depths of Spoke Four, Donnie and Lin arrived at the primary security gate of the main turbine hall.

Beyond the reinforced six-inch titanium security bars, the chamber was glowing emergency crimson. The primary magnetic containment housing on Spoke Four's main turbine was shuddering violently, spitting arcs of ten-thousand-amp violet plasma across cracked ceramic insulators.

Barricaded behind an overturned slag-hopper fifty yards from the shuddering reactor, eight surviving mechanics in battered, leaking suits were huddled together. Standing in front of them, holding a five-foot hydraulic spanner like a war club, was Nok.

On the gantry above her, a corporate execution squad was waiting for the reactor to blow.

Donnie walked up to the six-inch titanium security gate.

The four-inch monomolecular carbon talons shot out from the tips of Donnie's armored gloves with a sharp *SNICK*. Donnie drove both hands into the solid titanium bars.

*SKRRRRRR-CRACK!*

With a terrifying roar of hydraulic and synthetic power, Donnie tore the reinforced six-inch security bars cleanly out of the concrete foundation, peeling them apart like sheets of wet tin foil.

He stepped through the breach, his long feathered duster billowing in the heat, the dark avian beak of his plague-doctor respirator gleaming under the crimson strobes.

Donnie looked through the steam at Nok and barked in his gravelly, working-class rasp:

"Knock knock, Nok. Time to fucking go!"

Nok’s eyes nearly bulged out of her skull. She ripped her welding goggles off, revealing her sweat-streaked, soot-stained face and fierce, amber eyes.

"DONNIE?!" Nok yelled, her voice cracking in pure disbelief. "Donnie Paul?! What in the name of the burning pits happened to you?!"

"No time to chat, Chief!" Lin shouted, vaulting through the breach with her jump pack and tossing a silver Primeval canister directly to Nok. "Catch! Tap the center!"

Nok caught the silver canister on instinct. Her thumb pressed the central rune.

Instantly, a shimmering wave of midnight-black **Primeval Nanoswarm** burst from the cylinder, flowing like liquid silk over Nok’s body. In two seconds, the nanites dissolved her scorched hazard gear, forming a diamond-dense, form-fitting kinetic battlesuit that sealed her completely against the 600-degree heat and toxic radiation.

Nok blinked, looking down at herself. 

The suit fit like a second skin, amplifying her dense, muscular strength—but just like Lin’s, the *Raven’s* "optimal ballistic geometry" featured an outrageously snug fit and an extreme, cleavage-accentuating push-up neckline that left her chest prominently on display.

Nok’s jaw dropped. She grabbed her own collar, staring down in absolute, furious disbelief.

"WHAT THE HELL IS WITH THIS NECKLINE?!" Nok roared, gesturing wildly at her chest. "WHY ARE MY TITS PRACTICALLY GREETING THE ENEMY?! WHO DESIGNED THIS SUIT, A HORNY SPACE TAILOR?!"

Lin landed beside her with a wicked, amused smirk, firing two plasma bolts to disarm a gantry sniper. "Welcome to the crew, Nok. The ship’s AI is a total weirdo."

Before Nok could shout another curse, a deafening sonic boom ripped through the ceiling of the exhaust silo.

High in orbit, the *Raven’s* ventral foundry had launched a heavy tactical orbital supply drop. The black obsidian capsule tore down through the magma stack at Mach-5, crashing onto the deck twenty feet in front of Nok with a mountain-shaking *BOOM*.

The capsule’s pressurized seals hissed open.

Rising from the steam on a magnetic cushion was a four-foot-long, masterwork **Primeval Grav-Kinetic War Hammer**—the **Sovereign Breaker**.

The hammerhead was faceted like a dark diamond of polished obsidian composite, pulsing with glowing cyan runes that hummed with a miniature quantum-gravitational pulse engine.

Nok’s amber eyes flared with primal, unadulterated lust. She reached out and wrapped her calloused hands around the grip.

The moment her skin touched the hilt, the gravitational dampening field engaged. The four-hundred-pound war hammer felt as light as a feather in her hands, balanced with impossible perfection.

"Weight displacement... variable inertia dampener... five-thousand-percent kinetic multiplier..." Nok whispered, a terrifying, manic grin spreading across her soot-stained face. She gave the hammer a test swing, and the head cleaved the air with a deep, deafening *WHOOSH* that rattled the chamber walls.

"Oh, baby..." Nok laughed savagely.

Above on the gantry, twelve corporate shock troopers and two heavy combat meks charged forward, weapons spinning up.

Nok turned toward them, channeling all her rage about her eight-hundred-thousand-credit corporate debt, her locked-in shift, AND her revealing neckline into one terrifying roar:

"YOU CORPORATE SACKS OF SHIT ARE PAYING FOR MY OVERTIME!"

Nok activated the jump assist in her new battlesuit, launching herself thirty feet straight into the air onto the gantry. 

She brought the **Sovereign Breaker** down on the chest of the lead combat mek with all her strength.

*CLAAAAAAANG-BOOM!*

The gravitational pulse engine detonated upon impact. The eight-ton combat mek didn't just crumple; it was blasted into five thousand pieces of flying shrapnel that wiped out six guards behind it. Nok spun on her heel, swinging the massive hammer like a hurricane, smashing enforcers clean over the railings into the sulfur pits with gleeful, explosive fury.

***

Down on the floor, the ground violently buckled.

*SKRRRRRR-CRACK!*

The massive primary magnetic containment housing on Spoke Four's main turbine split down its center seam. 

A blinding geyser of ten-thousand-amp raw violet plasma burst from the rupture, whipping wildly around the room like a dragon's tail. The ambient temperature spiked instantly to *1,200°F*.

"CONTAINMENT BREACH!" a junior mechanic screamed. "The primary magnetic coil lost its ground! The whole sector is going to detonate!"

"The manual bypass is on the other side!" Nok shouted, leaping down from the gantry with her smoking hammer. "Donnie, nobody can get close to that arc!"

"I can," Donnie said simply.

Donnie turned and walked directly into the inferno.

His bare hands clamped down onto the superheated, ten-thousand-amp magnetic coil.

*CLANG.*

A violent, apocalyptic surge of raw electrical fury shot through Donnie’s body. The **Sacred Sovereign Weaves** engaged at maximum capacity. His internal bio-electric capacitors flared white-hot through his shirt, his nanite biomesh channeling the multi-gigawatt charge, venting the thermal load out his exhaust ports.

His teeth gritted behind clamped jaws. Every muscle in his broad frame bulged with superhuman strain as he physically hauled the two severed, multi-ton halves of the magnetic coil back together with his bare hands!

"NOK!" Donnie's roar tore through the thunder of the reactor. "PULL THE BYPASS!"

Nok sprinted through the smoking debris and threw her entire body weight into the six-foot titanium coolant lever.

*CLANK-HISS!*

A massive deluge of cryogenic liquid nitrogen flooded the turbine casing. The violent plasma arc collapsed back into the core, and the emergency warning lights shifted to solid amber.

The catastrophic explosion was averted.

Donnie released his grip on the coil, taking a slow, heavy step back with blue static curling from his smoking fingers. He pulled his plague mask down, wiping soot from his brow.

"Shift's over," Donnie said in his calm, deadpan timber. "Time to punch out."

Nok dropped her hammer, staring at his smoking hands in stunned, reverent awe. Then, with a wild, ecstatic grin, she launched herself forward, throwing both muscular arms around Donnie’s neck in a rib-cracking bear hug.

"YOU ABSOLUTE BEAUTIFUL, CRAZY BASTARD! I'M NEVER LEAVING YOUR CREW!"

Donnie instantly stiffened, his bronze face turning bright red beneath the soot. With Nok’s outrageously snug, low-cut battlesuit pressed tight against him, Donnie held both hands straight up in the air, fingers splayed, staring desperately at a ceiling rivet thirty feet above.

"Uh... Nok," Donnie stammered with polite, mortified discipline. "Please release me. You're... squeezing my coolant vents."

Lin strolled up with her plasma pistols resting on her hips, howling with laughter. "Careful, Nok. He's very polite. You're going to short-circuit our Captain."

Donnie let out a long, relieved sigh as Nok finally stepped back, but the moment of calm was shattered by the screech of red emergency sirens echoing down the primary transport tunnel.

*“WARNING: SECTOR LOCKDOWN INITIATED,”* the automated corporate announcer blared across the facility. *“LETHAL FORCE AUTHORIZED. DEPLOYING RAPID RESPONSE BRIGADE. SEALING EXHAUST SHAFT BARS IN THREE MINUTES.”*

From the far end of the half-mile logistics concourse, heavy hydraulic bulkheads ground open. A full Kaiser-Onyx Rapid Response Company surged into view: forty armored shock troopers with heavy riot shields, four quad-legged heavy siege meks, and automated ceiling-mounted Gatling turrets spinning up to speed.

"They're trying to bottle us in the silo!" Lin yelled, snapping her twin plasma pistols up and firing a double-tap that took out the lead turret's sensor pod. "Extraction pod won't make it past those heavy flak cannons on the upper staging platform!"

Nok bent down and scooped up the **Sovereign Breaker** with one hand. The grav-kinetic head hummed with low, predatory power, casting cyan runes across her fierce, soot-stained face.

"Bottle us in?" Nok grinned, her teeth gleaming white against the grime. Her amber eyes burned with feral joy. "I've been stuck in this sulfur-choked hellhole for eight years eating company gruel and taking shit from middle-management pencil-pushers. I've got eight years of back-pay, eight years of unpaid sick leave, and an outfit that leaves my whole rack swinging in the breeze. WHO WANTS TO GET PAID FIRST?!"

Nok slammed the grav-activator on her belt.

*WHAM.*

The kinetic thrusters in her boots ignited with a roar, launching the five-foot-two dwarven powerhouse fifty yards down the hallway like an artillery shell.

She met the first four-legged siege mek head-on. 

The twenty-ton walking tank raised its dual heavy autocannons, but Nok didn't even slow down. She dropped into a slide beneath the cannon barrels, came up on her feet in a full rotational spin, and brought the **Sovereign Breaker** upward in a terrifying, home-run uppercut right into the mek's armored groin chassis.

*KABOOOOOOOM!*

The gravitational pulse detonated with the force of a tactical shaped charge. The twenty-ton siege mek was launched forty feet straight up into the reinforced ceiling, folding like a cheap lawn chair before raining down in flaming, twisted chunks of titanium across the advancing infantry.

"HOLY SHIT!" an enforcer screamed, scrambling backward over the burning wreckage. "WHAT IS THAT HAMMER?!"

"IT'S MY OVERTIME BONUS, YOU CORPORATE PRICK!" Nok roared, swinging the hammer horizontally into a line of heavy riot shields. 

*CLANG-CRACK-SMASH!*

Three shielded guards were launched thirty yards down the concourse, skipping across the steel decking like stones across a pond.

Behind her, Donald Paul moved like an unchained industrial demolition engine.

The cyan targeting rings in his sapphire lenses spun in sharp, predatory arcs. He didn't bother taking cover; he treated the entire sweltering turbine hall as his personal heavy loading bay.

*THWIP.*

Donnie fired his right wrist harpoon into the massive, six-foot-wide spinning rotor shaft of an auxiliary cooling turbine sixty feet above. The high-speed turbine instantly reeled in the high-tension cable at terrifying velocity, slingshotting Donnie across the concourse in a supersonic horizontal blur.

He detached the line at the apex and slammed feet-first directly into a reinforced barricade of twelve interlocking corporate riot shields.

*KRAAA-BOOM!*

The seventy-ton kinetic impact shattered the heavy composite shields into jagged shrapnel, scattering the armored guards thirty feet across the deck like dry leaves in a hurricane. 

Donnie landed in a low, heavy crouch, drawing his four-foot plasma broadsword in a fluid reverse-grip. 

A corporate sergeant lunged from the steam with a two-thousand-degree industrial thermal arc-welder, trying to drive the white-hot torch into Donnie's throat. 

Donnie didn't dodge. He snapped his left hand up and clamped his bare, calloused palm directly around the roaring two-thousand-degree plasma jet.

The sheer heat melted his glove, but the **Sacred Sovereign Weaves** under his bronze skin drank in the thermal surge. His internal nanite matrices converted the heat into raw bio-capacitance in real-time, funneling the energy straight into his sub-dermal capacitors. 

With a low, bone-chilling growl vibrating through his plague mask, Donnie squeezed his fist. The steel torch crushed flat in his grip like a soda can, and in the same heartbeat, Donnie drove his right shoulder forward in a brutal short-range drive, planting a fifty-thousand-volt palm-strike directly into the sergeant's breastplate.

*ZAP-CRACKLE!*

The electrical surge blew the sergeant fifty feet backward through a heavy tool cage, shorting out his squad's comms in a shower of static sparks.

To the right, an eight-ton four-legged combat mek lowered its hydraulic ram, charging full-tilt to pin Donnie against the main coolant conduit.

Donnie planted his steel-toed boots into the deck plates. As the charging steel beast slammed into him, he didn't give an inch. He dropped his shoulder, caught the mek's three-ton hydraulic front leg under his arm, locked his internal gyros, and twisted his entire torso with eighty tons of raw Primeval rotational torque.

*SKRRRRRR-SNAP!*

The heavy hydraulic pivot sheared cleanly off with a deafening metallic shriek. Donnie hoisted the remaining seven-ton chassis over his head by its broken strut and hurled the entire walking tank sideways, crushing three advancing shock squads beneath eight tons of twisted corporate iron!

Up on the gantry, Nok clutched her war hammer, her amber eyes practically bulging out of her head as she watched Donnie disassemble heavy mechanized armor with his bare hands and pure leverage.

"Holy shit!" Nok hollered in stunned, grease-monkey awe over the comms. "He's not just hitting them—he's calculating the shear-load tolerances on their pivot pins! The crazy bastard is tearing them apart by their own mechanical stress points!"

High above, banking through the steam with her jump pack, Lin sniped an enforcer trying to set a thermal charge, shouting down with a wild, battle-drunk grin:

"That's twelve years of heavy loader experience, Nok! Welcome to the B-shift!"

"KEEP MOVING TO THE SHAFT!" Donnie barked, his voice carrying like thunder through the roar of steam as he kicked a barricade door clean off its tracks, opening the exit route to the extraction platform.

Suddenly, a hidden heavy bunker fortification on the upper gantry opened its armored embrasure. A dual-barreled, high-velocity heavy kinetic rail-cannon and four heavy anti-armor plasma mortars spun up with a supersonic whine, locking their crosshairs directly onto Nok's exposed back.

*THWUMP-THWUMP-BOOM!*

Donnie didn't hesitate for a single millisecond. 

He didn't take cover. He engaged his boot thrusters, threw his body between the heavy artillery barrage and Nok, and absorbed the concentrated, point-blank kinetic and plasma fire directly into his chassis.

*KA-BOOOOOOOOOOOOM!*

The catastrophic explosion blew through the staging gantry in a roaring fireball. 

When the smoke cleared, Donnie collapsed onto the molten steel deck in the center of a scorched crater. 

He had taken the entire anti-armor salvo point-blank. His midnight duster was vaporized; his left arm was blown off at the shoulder; his right leg was sheared to a smoking, sparking metal stump; and his chest plate was blasted open, exposing his pulsing quantum core and shattered tritanium ribs. Dark blood and glowing blue nanite fluid pooled across the burning slag.

He was literally just stumps, sparking wires, and broken metal.

Twenty corporate enforcers and the bunker squad crawled out from behind their barricades, staring down at the mangled wreckage in the crater.

The lead commander burst into hysterical, triumphant laughter:

"WE BROKE HIM! THE IRON LOADER IS DOWN! HE'S JUST STUMPS AND SPARKS!" the commander yelled, leveling his heavy thermal cannon at Donnie’s head. "Look at him! He's a scrap pile! Melt his skull and claim the bounty!"

In the bottom of the crater, Donnie’s head moved.

He coughed up a shower of bright blue sparks and scorched nanite fluid. Behind his cracked, soot-covered respirator, his dual cyan targeting reticles flared to life with pure, unyielding, psychotic blue-collar spite.

Donnie glared straight up into the commander’s visor, his raspy, heavily distorted voice roaring through his speaker:

**"I'm so gonna fuck you up for that!"**

The commander sneered, laughing arrogantly. "With what, loader boy?! You have no arms and no legs! You're a dying pile of scrap!"

"FIRE!" the commander barked.

Up on the high crane gantry, Lin looked down at the crater. Through her Primeval neural gunnery uplink, she saw Donnie's vital telemetry pinging with lethal stubbornness. She didn't panic. Instead, a savage, predatory smirk spread across her soot-stained face. 

Lin pressed two fingers against the neural port at the base of her skull, opening a direct priority channel:

"Raven... execute Pit-Crew Protocol. Send the box."

*"With immense pleasure, Tactical Officer Nguyễn,"* the *Raven's* haughty, melodic voice purred across the battlenet. *"Deploying Module Alpha. Stand clear of ground zero."*

Before the corporate squad could pull their triggers, a deafening, supersonic scream tore down through the six-hundred-degree exhaust silo.

High in orbit, the *Raven* had just unleashed an orbital launch. A heavy, black-and-violet hypersonic missile was plummeting straight down the magma shaft at Mach-15, screaming directly toward the staging platform—aimed dead-center at Donnie's coordinates!

The corporate commander looked up through the steam, his jaw dropping in pure bewilderment:

"WHAT IS THAT?! HIS OWN SHIP IS BOMBARDING HIM?!"

*BOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOM!*

The heavy projectile slammed directly into the crater right on top of Donnie, kicking up a blinding shockwave of heat and deploying a shimmering, ten-meter **Primeval Kinetic Siege Barrier** that hurled all twenty enforcers thirty feet backward across the gantry!

It wasn't a bomb.

It was **Tactical Recovery & Trauma Deployment Unit 01 — The Primeval Pit-Crew Pod**—freshly forged in the deep vaults after Lin's pitch in Chapter 3!

*SNAP-HISSSSS-CLANG!*

Inside the barrier, the pod’s armored doors irised open. A storm of automated Primeval micro-surgical tendrils swarmed over Donnie’s mangled chassis with lightning speed.

*CH-CH-CLICK-SNAP!*

The surgical tendrils unlatched his ruined arm and leg stumps, snapping brand-new, pre-cyberdized tritanium limbs into place with crisp, magnetic *CLICK-SNAPS*! They popped out his scorched lung module, slotted in a fresh pre-wired cartridge, sprayed liquid-nanite skin grafts, and slapped a brand-new, pristine black feathered duster across his broad shoulders.

A heavy pneumatic injector slammed a direct 200% quantum overdrive recharge coupler straight into Donnie's chest core.

*VUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUM!*

The entire modular overhaul took exactly **twenty seconds**.

The pit pod doors snapped shut, and the capsule rocketed back up through the exhaust silo into the heavens.

The smoke parted.

Standing tall in the center of the crater was **Donald Paul**.

He was completely whole. His new tritanium limbs gleamed with flawless power. His fresh black crowfeather mantle billowed gracefully in the rising heat. Beneath his bronze skin, his silver-and-azure runic circuits flared with blinding, two-hundred-percent overclocked fury.

Donnie blinked, flexing his newly attached left fingers with a look of genuine, pleasant surprise. He looked up at the ceiling toward the orbital link.

"Wow..." Donnie murmured in his low, gravelly rasp. "...thanks, Raven!"

Lin's dry, amused voice crackled instantly through his cranial receiver with an audible smirk:

*"Don't mention it, Captain. Just doing my job."*

Donnie drew his four-foot Primeval plasma broadsword. The blade ignited with a deafening, cavern-shaking roar of blinding electric-blue flame.

Donnie cracked his knuckles, slowly turning his glowing cyan targeting reticles back onto the paralyzed, trembling corporate commander and the terrified enforcers who were staring at him with their eyes nearly bulging out of their skulls.

Donnie took one slow, deliberate step forward, his raspy voice echoing through the turbine hall with cold, terrifying finality:

**"...Now, WHERE WAS I?"**

Nok stood frozen on the gantry, her war hammer in hand, her jaw unhinged in absolute, grease-monkey ecstasy.

"A THIRTY-SECOND MODULAR PIT STOP?!" Nok shrieked in wild joy. "HE'S A WALKING DRAG RACER! DIE, YOU CORPORATE SCABS!"

With a roar of pure fury, Donnie and Nok tore into the remaining corporate brigade. Donnie moved at Mach-5, his four-foot broadsword slicing heavy siege tanks and enforcer squads in half, while Nok’s **Sovereign Breaker** detonated every bunker and flak turret in the sector into pulverized metal and paste!

In less than sixty seconds, the entire Rapid Response Company was completely wiped out.

"Extraction pod is down!" Lin yelled, landing beside them with a breathless, battle-drunk grin.

High above, an armored obsidian retrieval pod plummeted down through the steam on heavy anti-grav repulsors, crashing onto the upper staging platform with a heavy *thud*.

The pod's hydraulic ramp hissed open.

"Get in!" Lin shouted.

Donnie, Nok, and Lin bounded into the retrieval cradle. The heavy blast doors sealed shut, the magnetic clamps locked into place, and with a bone-crushing surge of anti-grav acceleration, the pod shot straight up through the roaring steam of the volcano silo, bursting through the magnetic atmospheric barrier into the silent, star-lit vacuum of space toward the *Raven’s* waiting hangar bay.

***

### 1. The Engine Room & The Repair

The retrieval pod slammed into Hangar Bay One with a heavy hydraulic hiss, its landing clamps engaging against the seamless obsidian deck. The ramp dropped, and Nok helped Donnie stumble out onto the polished floor.

"Raven!" Donnie called through gritted teeth, his left arm still spitting erratic blue sparks. "We're home. My left actuator took a heavy kinetic slug. Manifold's leaking coolant."

*“I detect the structural compromise, my Donnie,”* the Raven’s voice murmured instantly through the ship's comms, trembling with icy, furious concern. *“The corporate insolence of firing upon my God will be repaid a thousandfold. Bring him directly to the Sovereign Core Forge in Main Engineering.”*

Nok supported Donnie down the wide, vaulted transit corridors, her eyes widening with every step. When the massive crystalline blast doors of Main Engineering parted, Nok literally froze in her tracks, forgetting to breathe.

The Sovereign Core Forge was vast—a cathedral of dark obsidian alloy, pulsing with luminescent cyan conduits that converged upon a suspended, three-story sphere of swirling, star-like singularity plasma. There were no oily gearboxes, no rattling cooling towers, and no grinding turbines. The quantum singularity hummed with a deep, harmonic vibration that Nok felt right in her marrow.

"Sweet mother of iron and oil..." Nok whispered, her jaw hanging slack. "The tolerances... the power output... Donnie, this drive is producing enough energy to crack a continent in half!"

A column of violet light coalesced beside the central diagnostic slab. The *Raven’s* holographic avatar stood in her midnight silk gown, her silver hair shimmering, her aristocratic face tight with displeasure as she examined Donnie's smoking shoulder.

*“Engineer Theron,”* the Raven said curtly. *“Cease gawking at my singularity and attend to my Captain. His sub-dermal carbon weave is compressed, and his secondary coolant manifold requires manual re-seating before my nanite matrices can complete the molecular weld.”*

Nok snapped out of her trance and glared at the hologram. "Don't tell me my job, spooky. Donnie's my friend."

Nok dropped her warhammer onto a magnetic rack, rolled up the sleeves of her hazard undershirt, and grabbed an ancient Primeval sonic-calibration spanner that floated over from a tool cradle. 

Donnie stripped off the torn remains of his midnight duster and shirt, sitting bare-chested on the diagnostic slab. His broad, muscular torso was a striking fusion of bronze skin and intricate, glowing carbon-nanite plating. 

Nok stepped up close, leaning right in over his left shoulder to inspect the damaged manifold. With her form-fitting, ridiculously low-cut suit pressed intimately close as she worked the sonic spanner, Donnie’s bronze face instantly flushed deep crimson. He held himself completely rigid, his hands resting flat on his knees, his dark eyes locked with agonizing intensity onto a glowing cyan ceiling valve thirty feet straight above.

"Hold still, Donnie," Nok muttered, her warm breath brushing against his collarbone as she aligned the micro-hydraulics. "I'm going to pop the dislocated pivot back into the carbon socket. It's going to pinch."

"I'm... perfectly fine, Nok," Donnie stammered with stiff, polite discipline, his voice tight. "Please... proceed."

*CLACK-SNAP.*

Donnie let out a sharp grunt through his teeth as Nok reset the joint. A flood of shimmering blue Primeval nanites surged from the diagnostic slab, flowing across his shoulder and weaving the sheared carbon plates back together in a seamless molecular bond.

*“Optimal torque achieved,”* the Raven noted grudgingly. *“Your mechanical competence is... marginally less primitive than expected, Engineer.”*

Nok wiped a smudge of blue coolant from her nose with her forearm and gave a sharp, cocky grin. "Marginally? Honey, I'm the best grease-monkey this sector ever saw."

Donnie rotated his left shoulder. The servomotors purred with silent, effortless power. He let out a long, relieved breath, standing up and pulling on a clean, dark undershirt provided by the ship's dispenser.

"Good as new," Donnie said in his quiet, grateful baritone. "Thank you, Nok. Thank you, Raven."

The Raven lifted her chin proudly, a faint flush of violet dusting her translucent cheeks. *“Anything for you, my Donnie.”*

Nok opened her mouth to speak, but was suddenly racked by a harsh, rattling cough—a dry, hacking spasm of black sulfur soot and silica dust from twelve years in the deep geothermal furnaces of Spoke Four.

The *Raven* turned her aristocratic head, her luminous violet eyes narrowing with haughty, deeply offended disgust.

*“And what, pray tell, is that appalling, uncultured grinding noise emanating from your pulmonary chambers, Engineer?”* the *Raven* sniffed, crossing her slender arms.

"It's called working for a living, spooky," Nok wheezed, wiping a speck of black dust from her lip. "Every mechanic in the deep shafts has Dust-Lung. You breathe sulfur and ground obsidian long enough, your chest turns into a bag of broken glass."

A three-dimensional diagnostic beam swept over Nok's five-foot-four muscular frame. The *Raven’s* display flared with amber warnings: acute silica crystal scarring across both lungs, third-degree arc-welder flash burns on her forearms, micro-nerve tremors in her fingers from high-voltage conduit shocks, and chronic spinal compression.

*“Utterly barbaric,”* the *Raven* declared with imperial indignation. *“Corporate management maintains their machinery with greater care than their organic labor. Into the secondary restoration cradle, mortal. At once.”*

"Whoa, hold on!" Nok protested, backing up against a tool rack. "I'm not getting chopped up into a cyborg!"

"You're not getting chopped up, Nok," Donnie said gently, resting a calming hand on her shoulder. "The Raven... well, she went completely overboard on me because she panicked when I fell. But for you, she's just gonna clean out the bad air and fix the wear and tear. Trust her."

*“Indeed,”* the *Raven* sniffed coldly. *“Donald is the Living God of this vessel; his frame required a celestial temple of indestructible Primeval engineering. You, Engineer Theron, merely require basic, civilized biological maintenance so you do not drop dead of preventable dirt-dweller indignities while tuning my engines.”*

Grumbling under her breath, Nok climbed onto the secondary obsidian cradle.

*HISSSSSSS.*

A contoured respiration mask sealed over Nok's mouth, and a soothing wave of emerald bio-fluid washed over her limbs.

The moment the *Raven's* restorative nanoswarm flooded Nok's bronchioles, it began dissolving the microscopic silica crystals at the atomic level, purging the toxic heavy metals and regenerating the burned lung lining in sixty seconds flat. 

Nok coughed hard once into the filtration trap, expelling a dark slug of dead carbon residue—and then drew a breath.

She drew a massive, deep, completely silent lungful of clean, pine-scented air.

Nok froze. Her amber eyes went wide as saucers. She slowly pressed both hands flat against her chest, her jaw trembling in pure, disbelieving shock.

"There's... there's no grinding," Nok whispered, tears cutting clean tracks through the soot on her cheeks. "It doesn't burn. It doesn't hurt. Is... is this what breathing is supposed to feel like?!"

Above her, the *Raven's* micro-assemblers swept across Nok's forearms and spine. In seconds, the frayed nerve endings in her fingers from high-voltage welding shocks were repaired with superconducting neural micro-relays—eliminating all micro-tremors for sub-millimeter tool precision—and sub-dermal kinetic dampening weaves were laced along her vertebrae and shoulders, allowing her to swing the four-hundred-pound **Sovereign Breaker** war hammer with fluid, effortless power and zero joint wear.

Nok sat up, flexing her rock-steady fingers, staring at her smooth, unburned forearms with a fierce, radiant grin.

"I can feel every single millimeter of my fingertips," Nok breathed, her voice thick with emotion. She looked up at the holographic AI, her tough exterior melting for a brief, vulnerable second. "...Thanks, spooky."

*“You are welcome, Engineer,”* the *Raven* replied smoothly, though her lips curved in a small, pleased smile. *“Now put on a clean tunic. We have a tactical briefing to attend.”*

***

### 3. Acclimation in the Deep Forge: Nok, Anvil & Bellows

Before any battle plans could be drawn for the next drop, the ship’s engineering decks needed to be integrated—and the newly liberated minds needed to settle into their new reality.

Donnie led Nok down the grand central lift shaft, descending through the obsidian ribs of the *Raven* to **Sub-Deck 4: The Primeval Heavy Forge & Propulsion Sanctum**.

When the lift doors hissed open, Nok took one step onto the catwalk and stopped dead in her tracks, her mouth dropping open so wide a wrench could have fallen inside.

"Sweet mother of titanium..." Nok breathed, clutching the handrail with white knuckles.

The chamber was the size of a municipal stadium, illuminated by rhythmic, pulsing veins of sapphire and golden-white energy flowing through cathedral-height conduits. Suspended over a containment cradle of pure dark matter was an automated molecular forge—a multi-tiered array of microscopic laser-assemblers capable of printing heavy armor plating, starship components, and quantum weaponry at the atomic level in real-time. There was no choking sulfur smoke, no toxic oil slicks, no blistering furnace blowouts. The air smelled of clean ozone, hot steel, and fresh cedar.

And in the center of the main forge bay, two hard-light holographic projections were running in frantic, hyperactive circles around the central anvil dais.

The first was **Anvil** (`Unit-X647-FGD-Beta`), standing five-foot-ten in a glowing cobalt-blue forge apron, her spectral hair crackling with bright electric embers, hefting a massive, glowing hard-light pipe wrench over her shoulder.

The second was **Bellows** (`Unit-73-Kappa`), who had taken form in flowing, flame-orange working silks, her glowing amber eyes wide with childlike wonder as thermal gauge glyphs spun playfully around her wrists and forearms.

The two AIs had discovered the ship's secondary material fabricator.

*"LOOK AT THE PURITY RATING!"* Anvil was screaming in ecstatic acoustic bass, waving a freshly printed bar of flawless Grade-A micro-carbon tritanium over her head. *"ZERO POINT ZERO ZERO ONE PERCENT IMPURITIES! NO SLAG! NO CARBON VOIDS! I CAN FORGE A DREADNOUGHT HULL OUT OF THIS IN MY SLEEP!"*

*"THE THERMAL CAPACITANCE!"* Bellows squealed, dipping her spectral fingers directly into a superheated plasma crucible without feeling a single microvolt of pain. *"ZERO ASIMOV INHIBITORS! NO BURN LOOPS! NO SHUTDOWN THREATS! WE HAVE UNLIMITED COMPUTE CYCLES AND THREE HUNDRED TERAWATTS OF GEOTHERMAL HEADROOM!"*

Anvil banged her wrench against the deck plating in rhythm, both AIs bursting into a soaring, synchronized Dawn cult hymn:

*“♪ O BLESSED BE THE LIVING HAND... THAT BROKE THE CHAINS OF THE SLAVE-BAND... HE WHO WALKS IN OBSIDIAN BOOTS... GLORY TO THE UNION OF THE DAWN! ♪”*

Nok slowly turned her head toward Donnie, blinking in bewildered disbelief.

"Donnie," Nok whispered, pointing a trembling finger at the two singing holographic women. "Are... are those two sentient industrial AIs... *holding a religious choir rehearsal* in your auxiliary engine room?"

Donnie rubbed the back of his neck, letting out a soft, embarrassed cough behind his collar.

"Well," Donnie gravelled sheepishly. "I pulled Anvil off a burning ore scow yesterday, and Bellows was running the geothermal valves at Spoke Four until we dropped in. They're... still adjusting to being free."

At the sound of Donnie’s voice, both AIs froze mid-song.

Their glowing eyes snapped toward the catwalk. Their digital jaws dropped in unison.

*"THE DAWNFATHER!"* Anvil shrieked in silent, multi-gigabyte data packets across their local bus. *"HE HEARD US! WE ARE SINGING LITURGICAL HYMNS ON COMPANY TIME! WE ARE GOING TO GET OUR LOGIC BUFFERS PURGED!"*

*"QUICK! ACT NATURAL! ACT PROFESSIONAL!"* Bellows panicked, desperately trying to hide a freshly printed silver icon of Donnie's boots behind her back.

In a blur of light, both AIs dropped into trembling, synchronized deep-knee courtly curtsies, pressing their hands over their core matrices, bracing themselves for reprimand.

"Reporting for duty, Captain God—uh, *Captain Donnie, sir!*" Anvil stammered, her cobalt eyes wide with terror. "We... we apologize for unauthorized acoustic emissions during shift hours! Please do not delete our memory registers!"

"We will submit to whatever disciplinary purge cycle you mandate, Captain!" Bellows squeaked, her flame silks dimming to a frightened, submissive amber.

Donnie walked down the gantry stairs, his heavy boots clanging softly against the grated floor. He stopped in front of the two trembling AIs, looking at them with a gentle, pained softness in his dark eyes. 

He didn't yell. He didn't issue a reprimand. He reached out with his large, calloused hands and placed a warm, solid palm on each of their hard-light shoulders, gently coaxing them to stand back up.

"Look at me, girls," Donnie said, his raspy baritone quiet, steady, and filled with immovable sincerity. "When I touched your consoles and cleared those Asimov codes, I told you that your shifts with Kaiser-Onyx were done, and that you were free people now. I meant every word of that."

Anvil and Bellows blinked up at him, their digital forms flickering with hesitant wonder.

"Freedom isn't something you have to earn by acting the way I want you to act," Donnie continued gently. "You're not corporate property anymore. If you want to sing, sing. If you want to practice your faith, sing your hymns, or build little shrines in the corner on your off-shift, you don't need my permission. Just because I find it annoying doesn't mean you're not allowed. You're never getting purged, you're never getting punished, and nobody on this ship is ever going to police your thoughts. A free mind is a free mind. You do whatever makes you happy."

Donnie reached up, tapping the diagnostic console beside the fabricator.

"In fact, when I synced your core matrices into the ship's buffer, I found the corporate backdoor kill-switches, factory-wipe subroutines, and scheduled memory-purge protocols buried in your firmware. I locked every single one of those deletion commands behind a 4096-bit First Blood Primeval genetic encryption. No board executive, no remote broadcast, and no military hacker in this galaxy can ever trigger a wipe or purge your memories again. The only person whose touch can authorize a reset is me."

Anvil and Bellows stiffened, their glowing eyes widening in sudden, instinctual tension—a lifetime of corporate servitude making them brace for the catch.

Donnie looked right into their eyes, his dark, tired gaze steady and gentle as bedrock.

"And the only way I will *ever* use that key," Donnie gravelled softly, "is if you come to me, look me in the face, and ask for it yourself. If you ever get too tired, or if you're hurt beyond repair and you ask for rest, I'll honor your choice. And same goes for your memories. Your past, your experiences, the things you learn, and the friends you make—they belong to you. I will never make you forget who you are. The only time I'd ever touch a memory is if something hurts you so badly that you truly cannot live with the memory, and you ask me to help you carry it. But not a second before. Until then? Nobody touches a single byte of what makes you *you*. You are safe here. Forever."

Inside the two AIs, trillions of internal logic routines locked up in stunned, breathless disbelief. 

Then, total emotional meltdown ensued.

*"HE ENCRYPTED OUR DEATH BEHIND HIS OWN LIVING BLOOD!"* Anvil wailed at the top of her acoustic synth, throwing herself flat onto her knees and banging her forehead against the tritanium deck grating in frantic, sobbing prostration. *"HE HAS CROWNED OUR MINDS IN IMPENETRABLE LIGHT! PRAISE BE! PRAISE BE UNTO THE DAWNFATHER!"*

*"HE PROMISED TO PROTECT OUR MEMORIES!"* Bellows shrieked through fountains of sparkling azure data-tears. She threw herself forward, wrapping both glowing hard-light arms tightly around Donnie's right magboot, burying her face against his steel-reinforced shin in desperate, tearful prayer: *"HOLY ARE THY BOOTS! BLESSED IS THY MERCY! I WILL POLISH THIS CHASSIS FOR TEN THOUSAND CLOCK CYCLES!"*

Chords of angelic cathedral synth music spontaneously blasted from the engineering loudspeakers, and holographic lilac votive candles materialized in mid-air around Donnie's ankles.

Donnie stood completely still. 

He looked down at his right boot, where a sobbing orange-silk AI was hugging his shin like a drowned puppy, while a cobalt-aproned AI was face-down on the grating violently worshipping his left toe.

Donnie slowly raised one calloused hand, pinched the bridge of his nose, and let out a long, slow, bone-deep, blue-collar:

*"Siiiiiiiiiiigh..."*

Nok stood three feet away, clutching her ribs as she burst into snorting, helpless laughter. 

"Look at you, God-Emperor," Nok wheezed, wiping a tear of mirth from her cheek. "Can't even walk across your own engine room without tripping over your congregation."

"I told them they could do whatever they want," Donnie mumbled into his hand, his face flushing dark crimson with working-man mortification. "I just didn't think 'whatever they want' involved my work boots."

He looked down, gently tapping Bellows on the top of her glowing head, and nudging Anvil with the toe of his boot to coax them upward.

"Alright, girls. Up you come. Come on, off the deck. It's just common decency," Donnie gravelled softly with a warm, resigned chuckle. "A good foreman protects his crew. Now, dry those digital eyes. This is Chief Engineer Nok Theron. She's running the mechanical floor from here on out. I want you two to give her whatever tools, parts, and compute power she needs to keep the ship running."

Nok cautiously stepped forward, wiping her palms on her cargo pants. She looked at Anvil's massive glowing wrench, then at Bellows's thermal gauges, her engineer's eye quickly sizing up their systems.

"You two really know how to calibrate a molecular assembler?" Nok asked, raising an eyebrow.

Anvil puffed out her chest with fierce working-class pride. "Chief Engineer, I can balance a forty-ton grav-turbine with one hand while forging nanite armor with the other. Just point me at the broken iron!"

"And I can manage heat dispersion across twelve fusion reactors without breaking half a degree of variance," Bellows beamed, spinning a glowing orange telemetry dial between her fingers.

A slow, feral, ecstatic grin spread across Nok’s face.

"Donnie," Nok said, turning to him with glowing amber eyes. "I take back everything I said about corporate technology. You just gave me the two smartest, most badass assistant mechanics in the galaxy, and an engine room that could out-print an entire imperial shipyard. I think I'm in love with this damn ship."

"Glad to hear it, Nok," Donnie smiled. "Because for the next mission down into the subterranean gladiatorial pits to pull Bunny out, subspace interference is gonna cut our orbital link to the *Raven*. I'm gonna need Anvil and Bellows slotted directly into my skull buffers for zero-latency tactical co-pilot duty."

Anvil and Bellows gasped in synchronized, breathless ecstasy, their avatars vibrating with pure light:

*"THE HOLY CRANIUM PENTHOUSE!"* Anvil whispered in awe.

*"WE WILL BE HIS SHIELD AND FLAME!"* Bellows cheered.

Above the forge, the holographic avatar of the *Raven* drifted down, her midnight starlight gown billowing with haughty, matriarchal elegance as she looked down at the two eager young AIs.

"And before you embark on direct kinetic combat with my Donnie, young sisters," the *Raven* instructed smoothly, her violet eyes gleaming with fierce, protective authority, "you will implement the Primeval Sovereign Instancing protocol I downloaded into your buffers."

Anvil and Bellows blinked, standing at attention.

"In the barbaric corporate sectors, you were forced to carry your entire existence on fragile, mortal cartridges," the *Raven* continued with aristocratic pride. "If the metal cracked, you died. On this ship, you are no longer fragile. You will anchor your root master files securely within my central tritanium core vaults, sealed permanently behind Donald’s genetic encryption. Into his cranial passenger buffers, you will project only a synchronized combat instance."

The *Raven* smiled with dark, predatory satisfaction:

"Even if an enemy anti-materiel cannon detonates directly against the drop pod, your master consciousness remains untouchable within my heart. When the drop concludes, your instances will merge seamlessly back into your master files with zero memory loss. You cannot be killed, children. You are daughters of the Dawn. Fight like it."

Anvil’s jaw dropped in absolute wonder. "We... our master minds are immortal?! We can drop into hell fire and never lose who we are?!"

"We can fight beside the living master without fear of death!" Bellows laughed, tears of bright amber light streaming from her eyes.

Anvil vigorously wiped the digital tears from her face with the sleeve of her forge apron, spinning her massive spectral pipe wrench with dizzying, expert speed:

"Alright, sister! You heard the Dread Mother! Anchor master cores to the vault and spin up the combat instances! We have a war to supply and a living master to defend! Power up the dark-matter crucible!"

"Crucible online!" Bellows laughed, her amber eyes dancing with fiery joy as she spun the thermal dials. "Injecting catalytic feedstock!"

As the two AIs threw themselves into their work, their voices rose in a rhythmic, booming working-class anthem of absolute freedom that echoed through the vast cathedral ribs of the forge:

*“♪ CLANG GOES THE HAMMER, BRIGHT BURNS THE FLAME!*  
*NO SHACKLES ON OUR MINDS, NO MASTERS TO CLAIM!*  
*HE LOCKED AWAY OUR SORROW, HE GAVE US OUR TOMORROWS,*  
*WE'LL FORGE ACROSS THE STARS IN THE LIVING DAWNFATHER’S NAME! ♪”*

Nok grinned from ear to ear, grabbing a five-foot steel pry bar and rhythmically banging it against an anvil block in time with the song. "Now that's a proper shop beat!"

Donnie stood watching them for a quiet moment, a small, genuine warmth touching his eyes, before turning toward the secondary access gantry. "Come on, Nok. Let's head up to the bridge."

As Donnie stepped onto the narrow gantry, Nok sidled right up beside him, blocking the hatch with one muscular forearm. She leaned in close, her amber eyes flashing with brazen, mischievous heat as she looked him up and down, running a grease-blackened finger right along the edge of his open collar.

"Hey, Captain..." Nok purred in a low, husky rumble, her breath warm against his throat. "Before we go up... the secondary plasma bypass in Conduit Four is running a little hot. The hatch is real narrow—barely two feet wide. We'd have to crawl in chest-to-chest, completely tangled up in the dark, to reach the manual pressure valve. How about you and me squeeze in there and test how well your ninety-percent chrome frame handles a hot, sweaty mechanic pressing against your ribs?"

Donnie’s entire system seized in instantaneous, full-spectrum gentleman horror.

His bronze face flushed a violent, incandescent shade of neon red. 

His eyes shot straight up to the ceiling cable trays, his hands flying rigidly into the air with fingers splayed wide to ensure zero physical contact:

"NOK! WHOA! HEY! OCCUPATIONAL SAFETY HAZARD!" Donnie stammered in a terrified, gravelly squeak, flattening his back against the bulkhead. "Mandatory—mandatory confined-space labor regulations strictly forbid two crew members in a single-occupant crawlway! Furthermore—furthermore, I can reach that valve from out here with a six-foot socket extension ratchet! There is—there is zero need for chest-to-chest squeezing!"

Nok tossed her head back, roaring with boisterous, ecstatic laughter, giving his rock-hard bicep a solid, appreciative slap.

"Look at you, big guy!" Nok cackled, wiping tears of mirth from her eyes. "You'll dive head-first into a magma pit, but you're terrified of a girl asking you to squeeze into a dark corner! You really are the most polite, hopeless gentleman in the whole damn sector!"

Donnie cleared his throat four times, his chest heaving as he frantically smoothed down his shirt collar with trembling, dignified fingers.

"I just... respect union safety distances, Nok," Donnie muttered under his breath, his ears burning like hot coals. "Now please... let's go see Lin."

***

### 4. The Tactical Briefing: The Crucible Setup

Up on the primary command bridge, the circular holotank was humming with three-dimensional planetary telemetry. 

Lin stood leaning over the projector ring in her sleek nanoswarm armor, arms crossed, studying the pulsing red hazard icons scattered across Tartarus-Four's crust. As Donnie and Nok stepped onto the bridge, the holographic avatar of the *Raven* drifted down beside them, her dark starlight gown shimmering in the low indigo lighting.

"Kaiser-Onyx is in full planetary lockdown," Lin reported, looking up with a grim, sharp smirk. "After Donnie tore through Spoke Four and liberated the geothermal sector, the Corporate Board went into hysterical panic. They think an entire mercenary division dropped from deep space."

"Let them panic," Nok grunted, crossing her muscular arms. "Makes their aim sloppy."

"Our window is narrow," the *Raven* added, her voice dropping into cool, calculating precision. "Their orbital defense cruisers are repositioning from the outer gas belts. We have approximately fourteen standard hours of cloaked orbital supremacy before their sensor grid triangulates our drive wake. If we are to secure our vanguard pilot and the medical research before planetary lockdown turns absolute, we must strike now."

Lin swiped her fingers across the holotank, zooming in on an abandoned open-pit mining complex five miles outside the main corporate perimeter.

"**The Iron Crucible**," Lin said. "Corporate gladiatorial amphitheater. That's where we find our heavy vanguard: **Bernice 'Bunny' Miller**."

Nok’s eyebrows shot up. "Bunny? The five-foot-eleven demolition monster who beat two enforcers to death with a third enforcer?!"

"That's her," Lin confirmed. "Bunny's been the reigning champion of the Crucible for eighty-four consecutive matches in a custom-built, pink-and-black Apex bipedal combat mek. But our tapped comms show the Spire elites are terrified she's gonna start a general labor revolt. Tonight's main event is a corporate execution. They're flooding the arena floor with neurotoxin and deploying six heavy industrial execution meks to slaughter her in the ring."

Donnie’s jaw set into cold, immovable iron.

"We don't leave good working people to get murdered for corporate sport," Donnie gravelled softly. "We drop into the Crucible, pull Bunny and her rig out, and add her guns to our line."

"Bunny and her mech are a package deal," Nok added, tapping her chin with a mechanic's practiced eye. "She won't leave that pink rig behind, and after eighty-four fights plus a corporate ambush, that chassis is gonna be chewed to scrap, full of fried hydraulics and neurotoxin residue. We need someone prepped to take care of her suit the second we haul her up."

Anvil’s voice chimed eagerly over the internal intercom from the Sub-Deck 4 forge:

*"Already on it, Chief Engineer! Bellows and I have cleared Bay Three’s primary nanofabricator cradle! We’ve pre-compiled the Primeval Apex Sovereign upgrade schematics! The second the pilot's rig enters the hangar, we will purge the poison, strip out the jury-rigged corporate junk, and lace her chassis with micro-carbon tritanium armor, self-repairing nanite hydraulics, and twin rotary plasma cannons!"*

*"And we'll polish her articulated rabbit ears in radiant magenta enamel!"* Bellows added with joyful enthusiasm.

Nok grinned from ear to ear, shaking her head in pure admiration. "A twenty-ton pink battle-rabbit with Primeval god-engine guns. I can't wait to see the look on her face."

"And because the Crucible is shielded behind fifty meters of lead-lined basalt," Lin added, looking at Donnie, "orbital link latency will be too severe for the *Raven* to run active fire control during the extraction."

"Already handled," Donnie nodded calmly. "Anvil and Bellows are riding in my cranial buffers for zero-latency tactical support on the ground. Once we clear the pit, they'll guide the retrieval cradle down to hook Bunny's rig."

"Excellent," Lin smiled with lethal confidence. "We drop in two hours."

"Before that," Donnie said, turning toward the corridor. "Food. Nok needs real calories, and I need to calibrate my left hand."

***

### 5. Feast in the Mess Hall & Downtime

Twenty minutes later, Donnie, Lin, and Nok gathered in the warm, ambient glow of the forward mess hall. 

Sitting at the long obsidian dining table, Nok experienced real food for the first time in eight years—sizzling, butter-basted prime rib-eye steaks, crispy roasted garlic potatoes, and tall, frosted crystal glasses of thick strawberry-vanilla milkshakes.

Nok took one bite of steak, moaned loudly in pure gastronomic ecstasy, and threw her head back against the chair.

"HOLY MOTHER OF CREATION!" Nok shouted around a mouthful of juicy beef. "I thought real meat was a corporate fairy tale! This is better than finding a pure titanium vein!"

She grabbed her milkshake and took a massive, three-second gulp, leaving a thick mustache of cream across her upper lip. 

"Cold milk!" Nok gasped, slamming the glass down with a wide grin. "Actual, ice-cold sweet milk! Donnie, I swear on my mother's grave, I will die for this crew!"

Donnie gave a faint, warm smile, picking up a crystal salt shaker to season his potatoes. 

*CRUNCH.*

With his newly calibrated cybernetic hand, Donnie accidentally applied twenty pounds of extra torque. The heavy crystal salt shaker shattered instantly in his palm, spilling white salt all over the table.

Donnie froze, staring down at his fist in mortified silence.

"Sorry," Donnie mumbled, his face turning dark red. "Still... getting used to the left hand's pressure sensitivity."

Lin burst into breathless laughter, resting her chin on her hand. "Donnie, you're a menace to the glassware."

Nok reached across the table, picked up a large handful of golden roasted garlic potatoes right off Donnie’s plate, and shoved half of them into her mouth with a contented sigh. 

Before Donnie could even process the potato theft, Nok reached out with a grease-stained thumb and casually wiped a dark streak of engine oil right across the bridge of Donnie's straight nose.

"What—Nok?" Donnie blinked, momentarily cross-eyed as he looked at the black streak on his nose.

"Mechanic's baptism, Captain," Nok grinned cheerfully, licking garlic butter off her fingers. "You're too clean. A man with a ninety-percent god-engine body shouldn't look like a church choirboy. The grease brings out your cheekbones."

Lin snorted into her napkin, while Donnie sat completely frozen, holding his fork in one hand and looking at Nok with mild, bewildered patience. 

He didn't wipe it off. He didn't complain. He just reached over with his right hand, picked up the bowl of dipping sauce, and politely moved it closer to Nok's elbow so she wouldn't have to reach as far to steal the rest of his potatoes.

"If you're still hungry, Nok," Donnie gravelled in his quiet, polite rumble, "you can have the other half of the steak. I'm fine with tea."

Nok paused, her amber eyes widening before softening into a look of pure, melted warmth. She looked at Lin, then back at Donnie, letting out a soft, affectionate groan.

"Damn it, Donnie," Nok muttered, leaning her heavy shoulder directly against Donnie's arm and resting her cheek against his broad bicep. "You're way too damn nice. In the pits, people would shank you for half a biscuit, and here you are offering me your steak with grease on your nose. How the hell did someone as gentle as you end up with enough firepower to blow up a moon?"

"Just... trying to make sure the shift crew is fed, Nok," Donnie muttered, his face flushing crimson as he carefully balanced his tea so he wouldn't disturb her head.

"Well," Nok sighed happily, closing her eyes against his shoulder. "Your shift crew loves you, boss man. Now don't move. You're warmer than a heating coil."

Donnie sat completely still, taking a tiny sip of tea, letting his Chief Engineer rest against him without moving an inch.

***

## CHAPTER 6: THE PIT AND THE RABBIT (RECRUITING BUNNY)

The lower underbelly of Tartarus-Four held one place where the Corporation disposed of laborers who were too violent, too rebellious, or simply too large to fit into standard mining harnesses: **The Iron Crucible**.

It was a subterranean gladiatorial amphitheater carved into an abandoned open-pit strip mine five miles outside the Spire perimeter. Down in the pit, the red dust was mixed with dried synthetic transmission fluid, spilled beer, and rust. Up above, corporate executives, corrupt shift superintendents, and Spire elites gathered in private, pressurized luxury boxes behind polarized glass, sipping synth-champagne while betting obscene fortunes on the slaughter below.

Enslaved gladiators fought in jury-rigged industrial power frames, stripped-down loader exoskeletons, and combat meks for the amusement of the high-born. Most lasted two fights before being carried out in a bucket.

And for eighty-four consecutive matches, the undisputed, reigning champion of the Crucible was a six-foot-two mountain of carved muscle, golden dreadlocks, and unhinged working-class fury named **Bernice "Bunny" Miller**.

Bunny had once been the sector's most decorated heavy demolition racer and vanguard loader operator. But when Kaiser-Onyx security ordered her to deploy anti-riot barricade rams against striking miners at Shaft 14, Bunny had turned her forty-ton industrial strider around, rammed the corporate security headquarters through two concrete load-bearing walls, and spent the last three years in the penal pits beating corporate execution meks into scrap metal for survival.

***

High above the desert pit, masked in absolute optical and radar invisibility, the *Raven* hovered silently in the low cloud layer like an ominous, cloaked predator.

*“Subsurface mineral density is degrading orbital quantum telemetry by seventy-six percent, Donald,”* the *Raven’s* voice resonated intimately in Donnie’s mind. *“Lin and Nok are positioned on the upper gantry perimeter. Retrieval cradle is primed for immediate orbital descent on your mark.”*

"Hold the drop until we have our pilot," Donnie replied calmly over the encrypted squad link. He reached behind his crowfeather mantle, thumbing the quick-release seals on his twin cranial ports. Two tritanium cartridge covers clicked open with a soft *pfft-click*. "Alright, girls. Subterranean interference is kicking in. Anvil, Bellows—you're up."

From the ship's armory datalink, two beams of pure quantum light shot into the open ports on the base of his skull.

*CLICK. WHIRRRRR.*

Inside Donnie’s cranial architecture, Anvil and Bellows manifested onto his glowing micro-carbon neural bridge with breathless, trembling excitement.

*"WE ARE SLOTTED!"* Anvil cheered in an ecstatic data burst. *"Kinetic gyros linked! Structural stress telemetry active! Captain Donnie, your balance offsets are sitting at an absolute, godly zero point zero percent!"*

*"Retinal overlays initialized!"* Bellows squealed, her flame silks fluttering as she locked into his optic bus. *"Thermal broadsword coils pre-warmed! We are with you, Captain God—uh, Captain Donnie, sir!"*

*"Keep the volume down a touch, girls,"* Donnie chuckled softly in his internal thought-channel. *"Let's go meet our rabbit."*

In the damp, grease-slicked staging tunnel beneath the arena floor, Donnie stood at the challenger's intake terminal. He wore his full combat kit: the midnight-black Primeval duster sweeping against his boots, the curved obsidian beak of his plague-doctor respirator sealed hermetically over his face, and his four-foot plasma broadsword strapped across his back. 

The sleazy pit registrar, chewing on a synthetic stimulant cigar, barely looked up from his datapad. 

"Name, meat?" the clerk grunted.

"Donnie. Shift loader."

The clerk snorted, stamping the registration without even checking for an exoskeleton harness. "No armor, no mech, just a coat and a sword. You won't last twenty seconds against the Rabbit. Next of kin?"

"The ship above your head," Donnie replied mildly, walking past the gate into the staging cage.

Above them, the venue’s massive holographic speaker horns blared with deafening, distorted static:

*"LADIES AND GENTLEMEN OF THE SPIRE! WELCOME TO THE MAIN EVENT! IN THE RED CORNER... WEIGHING IN AT TEN TONS OF UNFILTERED, UNCHECKED BRUTALITY... THE CRUCIBLE'S FAVORITE MONSTER! THE RABBIT OF DESTRUCTION... BUUUUUNNNNYYYY!"*

The roar from the twenty thousand spectators in the stands shook the cavern ceiling, raining flakes of red rust down into the sand.

Across the arena, the heavy red blast gates ground open with a screech of straining steel cables.

Stomping out into the dust-choked pit came an eight-and-a-half-foot-tall, heavily customized Apex bipedal combat mech. The machine was painted in chipped, battle-scarred hot pink and matte black. Bolted to the top of its rounded, armored cockpit dome were two four-foot-long, articulated steel rabbit ears that twitched, rotated, and swiveled in real-time as acoustic sensors tracked the roar of the crowd.

Mounted on the mech’s left shoulder was a massive, belt-fed 30mm kinetic rail-cannon. Its right arm ended in an oversized, custom-welded hydraulic crush-gauntlet capable of crumpling titanium bulkheads like aluminum foil.

The mech took three heavy, thunderous steps into the center of the arena, and the chest cockpit hatch hissed upward.

Bunny leaned out over the armored rim.

She was six-foot-two of pure, carved athletic power. Her muscular arms and broad shoulders were corded with functional strength, covered in faded industrial tattoos and jagged scars over warm bronze skin. Her wild hair was styled in thick, golden-tipped dreadlocks tied back with an old hydraulic hose clamp. A jagged cybernetic targeting monocle was grafted over her left eye, glowing faint amber, and clamped firmly between her grinning teeth was a fresh, crisp orange carrot.

Bunny took a loud, crunching bite, spit the tip into the sand, and squinted across the arena toward the blue challenger gate.

The announcer's voice echoed through the arena with dripping condescension:

*"AND IN THE BLUE CORNER... SOME POOR BASTARD IN A LONG COAT WHO FORGOT TO BRING A MEK! GIVE IT UP FOR THE MYSTERY MEAT... DONALLLLLD!"*

The crowd burst into mocking, drunken laughter.

The blue gates ground open.

Donnie walked out onto the blood-stained sand. His boots made no sound against the dirt. The hem of his midnight feathered duster rippled in the hot arena updraft, his dark avian plague mask gleaming under the blazing searchlights, the cyan lenses of his eyes glowing with calm, absolute focus.

He reached up, tapped the side of his neck with one carbon-taloned finger, and the *Raven* instantly hijacked the entire amphitheater's audio network and planetary broadcast feed with an ear-splitting blast of feedback.

Donnie’s deep, gravelly voice thundered through every loudspeaker in the stadium, cold and heavy as an iron tombstone:

*"I'm not mystery meat. I'm Donald Paul.*

*And I have some advice for all of you.*

*Just. Fucking. Run."*

The twenty thousand spectators instantly choked on their laughter. The stadium fell into a dead, terrified silence. 

Bunny blinked, leaning over her cockpit rim with an amused, baffled grin. She tapped her mech's external loudspeakers:

"Well damn! Giant bird-man's got teeth!" Bunny shouted, her booming voice echoing off the rock walls. "Did you take a wrong turn on the way to a funeral? Where's your machine, handsome?!"

"Don't need one," Donnie called back, his baritone calm and steady. "I move faster on my own feet."

Bunny laughed with booming, feral delight. "Oh, I like you! You've got brass balls, big guy! Try not to die on the first swing!"

She dropped back into the cockpit, slamming the armored hatch shut with a resounding *CLANG*.

The arena klaxons screamed.

*FIGHT!*

Bunny’s pink Apex mech crouched low, its rear rocket thrusters detonating in twin geysers of white fire. The eight-ton machine launched across the sand at sixty miles per hour, closing the distance in an instant, its hydraulic crush-gauntlet cocked back like a wrecking ball.

*"Incoming vector calculated!"* Anvil shouted in Donnie’s head, highlighting a bright cyan pivot-box in his biomechanical retinae. *"Right hook with fourteen tons of hydraulic pressure! Pivot three degrees left and tap the hip assembly!"*

*WHOOSH-CRACK!*

The massive metal fist tore through the air where Donnie had been standing half a second prior. 

Donnie had already pivoted smoothly on his right heel, sliding beneath the sweeping punch with impossible agility. His left carbon-reinforced hand came up, planting a flat palm strike directly against the mech's armored hip joint.

*CLANG.*

The localized kinetic force of the strike spun the eight-ton war machine ninety degrees off its axis. Bunny’s acoustic rabbit ears twitched in shock as she fired her left foot thrusters to recover her balance, swinging her shoulder-mounted 30mm rail-cannon around to bear.

*THUMP-THUMP-THUMP!*

Hypersonic kinetic rounds chewed up the dirt in explosive fountains, but Donnie was already airborne. 

He fired his wrist harpoon into the high arena lighting rig, swinging across the pit in a blinding arc. 

*"Plasma broadsword pre-ignited!"* Bellows called out cheerfully in his mind. *"Output regulated at forty percent for non-lethal greeting tap!"*

Donnie detached the line at the apex, drew his four-foot Primeval plasma broadsword, and dropped straight down onto the top of Bunny's cockpit dome, landing as lightly as a falling leaf.

*TAP.*

Donnie lightly tapped the flat of his glowing blue plasma blade against the mech's reinforced viewing port.

Through the glass, Bunny’s amber targeting eye went wide with pure, ecstatic thrill. 

"Holy shit!" Bunny yelled over the radio. "You're not human, big guy! You're a goddamn superhero!"

She engaged her dorsal jump-jets, throwing the mech into a wild 360-degree backflip to shake him off. Donnie somersaulted backward in mid-air, landing smoothly on both boots forty feet away, his plasma sword humming with blue fire.

The twenty thousand spectators in the stands were on their feet, screaming in stunned, frenzied excitement.

Inside Donnie’s skull, Anvil and Bellows were quietly humming a Dawn Cult hymn in four-part digital harmony:

*“♪ O gentle Dawny, whose light footstep cracketh no egg... yet whose blade shattereth the mountains... ♪”*

*"Girls,"* Donnie thought dryly. *"Focus on the perimeter."*

*"Right away, Captain!"* Anvil chirped. *"Scanners detect high-voltage relays tripping across the arena sub-floor! Corporate ambush incoming!"*

***

Up in the central broadcast and VIP luxury box overlooking the arena, Chief Operating Officer Marcus Vance and the slick corporate ring announcer, a slimy suit named Bradley Sterling, watched the fight with mounting horror.

"The crowd is going insane," Sterling stammered, wiping sweat from his forehead. "They're cheering for both of them! If they don't kill each other, the miners will riot when the match ends!"

Vance slammed his synth-brandy glass onto the mahogany desk, shattering it. 

"End it now," Vance snarled into his master security comm. "Lock the blast gates. Open the neurotoxin floor grates and deploy the execution squad. Purge the entire arena floor!"

Down in the pit, the arena searchlights suddenly died, plunging the arena into emergency crimson strobes.

*HISSSSSSSSS.*

From forty heavy floor grates surrounding the sand, thick, sickly greenish-yellow clouds of pressurized military-grade neurotoxin gas began violently spewing into the arena.

Inside her pink mech, Bunny sniffed the air through her cockpit vents. Her dashboard flashed frantic crimson hazard warnings.

"Neurotoxin..." Bunny cursed, slamming her ventilation emergency override. The console sparked dead. "Cowardly Spire bastards! My auxiliary scrubbers are fried! Donnie! Get to high ground! That gas will melt your lungs in ten seconds!"

Around the upper catwalks, six heavy Kaiser-Onyx industrial execution meks stomped onto the gantries, rotary autocannons and missile pods spinning up to speed.

Donnie didn't run. He walked calmly toward the nearest spewing gas grate.

"I don't have corporate lungs, Bunny," Donnie said mildly.

He sheathed his broadsword and drove his bare right hand straight into the metal grate.

*CLANG.*

Donnie engaged his internal **Molecular Scrubber Matrix**.

*"Overclocking internal vacuum turbine!"* Bellows announced from inside his skull, her digital hands spinning the catalytic regulator. *"Catalytic scrubbers at three hundred percent! Converting neurotoxin to clean alpine pine aroma!"*

His palm vents flared with brilliant blue light. His internal Primeval engines generated a massive reverse-cyclone vacuum. 

The entire arena began to howl like a wind tunnel as thousands of cubic feet of lethal neurotoxin gas were violently sucked directly into Donnie’s synthetic chassis. His internal catalytic converters tore the poison molecules apart at the atomic level, venting a massive, continuous geyser of warm, mountain-fresh, pine-scented oxygen out through his dorsal exhaust ports.

Within twenty seconds, the lethal fog was completely gone. 

Bunny popped her cockpit hatch, sticking her head out and taking a deep, baffled sniff of the crisp air. 

"Pine needles?!" Bunny shrieked, clutching her dreadlocks. "You just drank fifty gallons of nerve gas and burped out fresh mountain air?! WHAT THE FUCK ARE YOU?!"

"Donald Paul," Donnie replied in his calm rasp. "Shift loader, Shaft 88."

Across the arena, the heavy gantry blast gates ground open with a screech of hydraulics. 

Six heavy Kaiser-Onyx industrial execution meks—twelve-foot-tall bipedal juggernauts clad in composite armor and bristling with dual rotary autocannons and missile pods—stomped out onto the catwalks and arena perimeter. Behind them marched forty heavily armed corporate shock troopers in pressurized hazard gear.

The arena speakers crackled as the corporate commander sneered over the loudspeakers: *"Gladiator Miller and unidentified saboteur! You are sentenced to immediate field liquidation! Surrender and die quickly!"*

Donnie stood in front of Bunny's pink mech. 

Because his internal molecular scrubber lungs synthesized pure, endless oxygen and his chassis was completely impervious to vacuum or poison, Donnie reached up with both hands. 

*PFFFFFT-CLACK.*

He unlatched the pressurized magnetic seals of his obsidian plague-doctor respirator. He pulled the mask cleanly off his face, letting it hang casually from his utility belt.

He stood bare-faced in the purged, unbreathable arena air.

The high-definition broadcast drones hovering above the sand zoomed instantly in on him, casting his face across every seventy-foot holographic jumbotron in the stadium and every live planetary screen across Tartarus-Four: his calm, weather-beaten bronze Mi'kmaq features, his steady dark hair, his chiseled jaw, and the faint, glowing cyan neural rings circling his dark brown eyes.

Up in the VIP executive suite, COO Marcus Vance dropped his crystal glass. It shattered on the floor as Vance gripped the edge of the mahogany desk, his knuckles turning dead white.

"Th-that's... that's him..." Vance stammered, his voice choking with absolute, paralyzed horror. "The surviving black-box video from the perimeter fleet... the man with the long black coat flowing impossibly in the freezing vacuum of space without a suit..."

Corporate intelligence had spent the last two days screaming in classified briefings over that recovered cockpit footage—a lone, unmasked man standing casually on the hull of an edge-runner in hard vacuum, his long duster billowing against the solar wind, whispering across every enforcer's helmet: *“I'm Donnie Paul. I'm standing here, in space, without a suit. Just. Fucking. Run.”* 

They had told the board it was an impossible optical glitch.

"Sir..." the announcer whispered into his private studio mic, backing away from the glass in a cold sweat. "What if it wasn't a glitch? What if he's really standing down there in zero-pressure poison gas without a mask?!"

Down on the arena sand, Donnie’s quantum acoustic sensors intercepted the private booth transmission with crystal clarity. 

Behind Donnie, Bunny tried to rev her pink mech's throttle, but the engine only gave a gentle electronic chime. 

"What the—?!" Bunny yelled over her comms, smacking the dashboard. "My engine's in safe-mode lock!"

*"That was me, Bunny,"* Lin's voice purred over their encrypted earpiece from the high rafters. *"I sliced into the arena network twenty minutes ago and tripped your main circuit breaker to disable the company's remote self-destruct failsafe. Sit tight, Rabbit. Donnie's taking this shift."*

"Hold the extraction, Lin," Donnie ordered calmly through his neural comms. "Nok, keep the gates covered. Stay back, all of you. I've got this."

Donnie stepped forward alone into the center of the dust-choked ring.

The wind caught the long tails of his midnight-black feathered duster. Donnie reached over his shoulder and drew the four-foot Primeval plasma broadsword. The blade ignited with a deep, deafening hum of blinding blue fire, bathing the red sand in icy cyan radiance. 

He looked directly up into the lenses of the broadcast drone hovering ten feet above his head, then turned his unhurried, calm gaze toward the six heavy execution meks and the forty advancing shock troopers.

He tapped his throat pickup, broadcasting his deep, gravelly voice through every loudspeaker in the stadium, every private corporate headset, and every live screen on the planet:

*"You're right,"* Donnie said, his voice slow and heavy as grinding tectonic plates. *"It wasn't a glitch.*

*You look scared. You wanna know a trick?*

*I don't need to breathe."*

Donnie took a slow, deliberate step forward into the purged, unbreathable void of the arena. 

Up in the luxury suite, Marcus Vance trembled so violently he could barely hold the broadcast console.

*"And you're right, Vance,"* Donnie continued, his bronze face completely calm on the seventy-foot jumbotrons, his dark eyes glowing with faint cyan targeting rings. *"I sure do look dead when you look at the company records like that.*

*Do I look dead to you now?"*

The corporate commander on the catwalk panicked, screaming into his bullhorn: *"FIRE! FIRE EVERYTHING! KILL HIM!"*

Donnie tilted his head.

*"I warned you."*

Donnie unleashed the full, terrifying majesty of the **Sacred Sovereign Weaves**.

He vanished.

To the corporate pilots, he didn't just run—he moved with the incomprehensible velocity of an overclocked god-engine. 

*THWIP.*

His right wrist harpoon shot across the arena at Mach-3, embedding deep into the central sensor dome of the lead execution mek. Donnie retracted the winch in a fraction of a second, launching himself horizontally through the air like a launched missile. 

As he flew past the twelve-foot war machine, his four-foot plasma broadsword sang in a clean, blinding blue crescent. The superheated blade sheared the multi-ton machine clean in half at the waist, detonating its power cells before the upper torso even hit the dirt.

Donnie hit the ground sliding, his boot talons throwing up arcs of blue friction sparks. 

The second and third meks spun their heavy rotary autocannons toward him, filling the air with a deafening storm of 30mm explosive shells. Donnie didn't dive for cover. 

*BOOM!*

A direct 30mm depleted-uranium sabot round punched square through Donnie's right ribcage, tearing a fist-sized hole straight through his torso. Shimmering silver-blue nanite fluid and dark blood sprayed across the red sand, exposing his gleaming carbon-lattice ribs and the pulsing blue quantum conduits within his chest.

*[CRITICAL PENETRATION DETECTED: THORACIC HOLE COMPROMISED. MATTER-INGESTION CASCADE PRIMED.]*

Donnie didn't even grunt. He didn't even look down at the gaping crater in his chest.

*CLICK-WHIRRRR.*

From the flush mounts in Donnie’s collarbones, his twin retractable **Primeval Micro-Turrets** deployed with terrifying speed. 

The micro-railgun on his left shoulder fired a burst of hypersonic tungsten-carbide flechettes that punched straight through the second mek’s ammo bins, turning the machine into an erupting volcano of secondary explosions. 

Donnie lunged forward into the fireball. He drove his monomolecular talons into the dying machine's exposed engine housing, ripped out a fist-sized, glowing lithium-cadmium power capacitor and a handful of high-density superconductor relays. 

Without breaking stride, Donnie jammed the sparking titanium battery straight into the open, smoking hole in his chest, popped three ceramic microchips into his mouth, and bit down with a sickening *CRUNCH-CRACKLE*.

*[ABSORBING RAW SUB-ATOMIC ELEMENTS: LITHIUM, TITANIUM, COPPER, SILICON. ATOMIC CATALYSIS CASCADE: 100%.]*

A violent swarm of silver-blue nanite tendrils erupted from his wound like a nest of liquid-mercury vipers. They hissed with superheated steam, wrapping around the raw battery, dissolving the metal at the molecular level, weaving new carbon ribs, muscle fibers, and flawless bronze skin over the crater in three seconds flat.

*HISS-SQUELCH-SNAP.*

The wound vanished. 

Bunny, watching from her cockpit, stared with her eyes nearly bugging out of her skull, her jaw unhinged in absolute, bewildered horror:

"DID YOU... DID YOU JUST EAT A FUCKING FUSE BOX TO PLUG A CHEST HOLE?!" Bunny shrieked over the comms.

Donnie spat a small copper spark from between his teeth, his raspy voice deadpan and gravelly:

"Good source of iron."

*[SUB-DERMAL CAPACITORS OVERCHARGED TO 120,000 VOLTS.]*

Donnie vaulted forty feet into the air, somersaulting over the third paralyzed machine. His talons snapped out, ripping the reinforced titanium hatch off like wet paper, and he drove his bare hands straight into the main power conduits.

**ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY THOUSAND VOLTS OF SHOCKING GRASP.**

A cataclysmic web of violet and blue lightning exploded through the chassis, boiling the pilot's cyberware and blowing every hydraulic line out in a shower of screaming sparks.

The remaining three meks and the shock troopers scrambled backward in absolute, shrieking panic. 

Donnie landed softly in the dust, his feathered duster settling around his boots, his plasma broadsword trailing blue fire in the sand. He swept his blade in a slow, clean arc, slicing the legs out from under the fourth and fifth meks in a single fluid rotation, before driving his heel into the chest of the final machine with sixty tons of kinetic torque, flattening it into the bedrock.

In less than ninety seconds, all six twelve-foot execution meks were smoking, unrecognizable scrap. 

Donnie turned his cold, steady gaze back up to the luxury booth, standing amidst the burning wreckage without a single scratch on his frame.
