The arena went dead, tomb-like silent. Twenty thousand miners in the stands stood paralyzed in absolute, reverent awe.


***

High above, the clouds parted with a deafening sonic boom. The cloaked hull of the *Raven* dropped through the cloud deck, decloaking in all its magnificent, predatory glory.

A heavy-duty industrial extraction rig—a massive obsidian cradle with four heavy magnetic crane cables—plummeted down from the dreadnought, slamming into the center of the arena dust with anti-grav thrusters hissing.

*THUD.*

The magnetic clamps latched directly onto Bunny’s pink-and-black Apex mech, locking it tight to the platform.

Bunny hopped down from her cockpit, staring at the colossal dreadnought in awe. "Sweet bleeding mother of engines... THAT'S your ship?!"

"That's our home," Donnie said.

Before stepping onto the extraction cradle, Donnie looked up at the glass-walled executive luxury suite fifty feet above the pit.

"Lin," Donnie said into his comms. "Hold the winch for ten seconds. I need to check in with management."

Donnie aimed his left wrist gauntlet straight at COO Marcus Vance’s panoramic reinforced viewing window.

*THWIP-CRASH!*

The monomolecular harpoon dart punched through the heavy glass. The high-speed winch engaged with a deafening whine, launching Donnie straight up the vertical hundred-foot drop like an artillery rocket.

He slammed boots-first into the window frame, completely shattering the reinforced glass into ten thousand sparkling shards!

*WHOOOOOOSH!*

The instant the glass shattered, the purged, zero-pressure toxic atmosphere of the arena rushed violently into the pressurized luxury suite. 

Marcus Vance and the slick announcer Bradley Sterling screamed in raw panic, choking and gagging on the sulfurous drafts, scrambling desperately across the plush carpet for emergency oxygen masks. Sterling’s crooked toupee blew clean off his head, fluttering into the draft.

Donnie stepped through the broken window frame, his black feathered duster settling smoothly around his shoulders. Standing bare-faced in the swirling toxic rush, Donnie looked down at the cowering executives and gave a bright, cheerful, working-class smile.

He reached out, tapped the live planetary broadcast microphone on the desk, and spoke in a sunny, deadpan tone that boomed through every speaker on Tartarus-Four:

*"Hi!"* Donnie said cheerfully. *"Miss me?"*

Vance pressed himself flat against the back wall, trembling and wheezing into a yellow emergency respirator, staring at Donnie like he was looking at an ancient god of death come to collect his debt.

Donnie reached into the pocket of his dark coat, pulled out the cheap blue plastic pocket comb from his old locker in Shaft 88, and slapped it down onto the broadcast console with a crisp *clack*.

He leaned down, his glowing cyan optics locking onto the announcer's bald, quivering forehead.

"Here," Donnie added in his low, gravelly rasp over the live planetary feed. "Fix your hair. You look like shit on live television."

He tapped the master broadcast feed off, stepped backward off the jagged ledge, and plummeted into the open air.

Down from the clouds, the *Raven’s* heavy rocket-assisted extraction cable shot toward the arena floor at three hundred miles per hour. 

In mid-fall, Donnie reached out and caught the high-tension steel-weave cable with his carbon-reinforced right hand.

Swinging on the cable in a magnificent, Robin Hood-style pendulum arc across the entire expanse of the amphitheater, Donnie rode the descent down to the arena floor, landing with cat-like precision right on top of Bunny’s pink combat chassis.

*CLANK.*

With one hand, Donnie slammed the heavy magnetic rocket-winch clamp directly onto the reinforced mounting ring of Bunny's central armored cockpit pod. 

Before the winch fired, Donnie drew his four-foot Primeval plasma broadsword with a blinding flourish. In a single, fluid, three-hundred-and-sixty-degree rotational spin, his superheated blue blade sang through the air—*SHING-SHING-SHING-SHING!*

The plasma blade cleanly sheared away the rusted scrap-metal legs, hydraulic pistons, and heavy tread assemblies of the old chassis, shedding four tons of dead corporate junk in a fountain of white-hot sparks and leaving only Bunny’s reinforced, oval control pod securely tethered to the cable.

"Lightening the load," Donnie said mildly down through the viewing hatch.

Bunny popped the cockpit hatch, sticking her head out with wild, ecstatic laughter. "Holy shit! You just circumcised my robot!"

"Hold on," Donnie said.

*BOOOOOOM!*

The *Raven’s* orbital winch engaged with five hundred tons of anti-grav rocket thrust. 

The tether snapped taut, launching the streamlined cockpit pod straight up into the stratosphere like an ascending missile. Standing casually on the roof of the ascending pod with the wind howling through his long feathered duster, Donnie looked down at the luxury executive suite, locked eyes with Marcus Vance choking in his shattered window, and calmly raised his left hand, extending a single, perfectly sculpted middle finger.

The gesture was broadcast on the giant jumbotrons until the signal ripped into static, sending twenty thousand roaring miners into absolute, hysterical delirium as Donnie and Bunny vanished into the clouds toward the *Raven’s* waiting hangar bay.

***

Aboard the *Raven’s* secondary hangar deck, Bunny’s streamlined pink-and-black control pod set down on the central maintenance cradle with a satisfying *thud*.

Bunny hopped down from the hatch, her combat boots hitting the seamless obsidian deck. She walked straight up to Donnie, towering at nearly six feet of scarred, muscular power, drenched in sweat with a wild, infectious grin lighting up her face.

She reached out with a massive, calloused hand and tapped Donnie’s armored chest plate with her knuckles.

*CLINK.*

"Solid Primeval battle-plate," Bunny laughed, her amber-blue eyes shining with intense admiration. "You ate nerve gas, wrecked an execution squad, and gave corporate a plastic comb on live TV. Who the hell are you really, handsome?"

Donnie reached up, unsealed his plague-doctor respirator, and pulled it down around his neck, revealing his calm, tired bronze face.

"Just Donald," Donnie said in his dry, deadpan baritone. "We're an independent crew. We have a ship, and we needed the best vanguard pilot on Tartarus-Four."

Bunny threw her head back and let out a booming, room-shaking laugh of pure joy. She stepped forward and wrapped both of her thick, muscular arms around Donnie’s broad shoulders, pulling him into a rib-cracking, sweaty bear hug that nearly lifted him off his boots.

"Donnie, baby!" Bunny roared with delight, planting a loud, greasy smack of a kiss right on his cheek. "You just bought yourself a Rabbit!"

Donnie instantly froze. 

His bronze face turned a bright, furious red beneath the soot. Towering Bunny was pressed right against him, warm, solid, and entirely uninhibited. Donnie awkwardly held both of his hands straight up in the air, his fingers stiffly splayed, refusing to let his hands touch her bare back or waist.

He cleared his throat loudly three times, desperately staring at a ceiling rivet forty feet above.

"Uh... Bunny," Donnie stammered with polite, mortified discipline. "Thank you. But, uh... please release me. You're... compressing my pectoral actuators."

Nok and Lin walked down the gantry, watching Donnie’s gentlemanly struggle with loud, howling laughter.

"Look at him!" Nok wheezed, wiping tears of mirth from her eyes. "He can eat fifty gallons of nerve gas without blinking, but a hug from a pretty girl turns our Captain into a statue!"

"He's very delicate, Bunny," Lin teased with a slow, wicked wink. "Be gentle with our Captain."

Above the cradle, the *Raven’s* holographic avatar drifted down in a swirl of shimmering midnight silk and starlight. The AI tilted her head, inspecting Bunny's pink mech with an expression of aristocratic, haughty disdain.

"What an utterly barbaric pile of rusted scrap metal," the *Raven* sniffed, wrinkling her delicate nose. "Hydraulic fluid? Steel cables? And what, pray tell, are those ridiculous elongated auditory appendages on the cockpit?"

"They're rabbit ears, sweetheart!" Bunny shot back, planting her fists on her hips with a proud grin. "They track acoustic shockwaves in the dirt! And that 'rusted scrap' just turned six corporate assault meks into kindling!"

"A pebble can break glass, mortal, but that does not make it art," the *Raven* replied smoothly. She drifted closer, her violet eyes assessing Bunny with quiet, reluctant respect. "However... seeing as how you fought alongside my Donnie and did not allow his sacred frame to be scratched, I will allow our forge masters to execute their pre-compiled upgrades."

Donnie reached up, thumbing the quick-release toggles on his cranial ports. Two tritanium cartridge covers popped open with a soft *pfft-click*, and Anvil and Bellows materialized onto the hangar floor in radiant, hard-light form.

"FORGE CREW, AT THE READY!" Anvil cheered, spinning her spectral wrench and running up to the maintenance console with Nok. "Pre-compiled schematics injected into the central nanite matrix!"

"Thermal containment engaged!" Bellows laughed, her hands dancing across the fabrication controls. "Heating the molecular alloy to optimal infusion temperature!"

The central molecular fabrication arrays in the hangar ceiling flared with brilliant, blinding white light. A dense swarm of Primeval assembler nanites cascaded down over Bunny’s pink mech like a waterfall of liquid silver.

Bunny watched with wide, stunned eyes as her mech was completely deconstructed and rebuilt at the atomic level in sixty seconds.

When the light faded, the machine standing on the cradle was breathtaking:
- The chassis was forged from matte-black and pearlescent hot-pink Primeval obsidian-titanium composite, polished in dazzling magenta enamel and impervious to standard plasma fire.
- The crude diesel hydraulics had been replaced with **Zero-Point Quantum-Gravitational Actuators**, giving the mech agility and speed that defied physics.
- The 30mm rail-cannon had been upgraded to a **50mm Heavy Grav-Kinetic Hyper-Velocity Rail-Cannon**, capable of punching through a cruiser’s armored prow at twenty miles.
- The hydraulic crush-gauntlet now hummed with a **Molecular Disruption Field** that could tear atomic bonds apart on contact.
- And atop the cockpit, the two articulated rabbit ears were now sleek, razor-sharp aerodynamic sensor antennae pulsing with glowing cyan telemetry runes.

Anvil proudly banged her wrench against the new tritanium foot-plating with a loud *CLANG*: "Chassis calibrated, Chief Engineer Nok! Grade-A Primeval perfection!"

"She's a beauty," Nok grinned, wiping her hands on a shop rag. "Go take her for a spin, Rabbit."

Bunny stood frozen, her mouth hanging wide open. She slowly walked up to the machine, running her trembling fingers over the silky, impenetrable armor plating.

"Zero-point actuators... grav-rail cannon... quantum neural link..." Bunny breathed, tears of pure ecstasy welling in her amber eyes. She turned to the *Raven* and the team, her voice shaking. "It’s... it's a goddess in pink armor."

"It is an **Apex Sovereign Strider**," the *Raven* corrected with haughty pride. "Do not scratch the paint, mortal."

Bunny leaped onto the cockpit ladder, vaulted into the newly lined memory-gel pilot’s seat, and slammed the hatch shut. The neural link engaged instantly, syncing with her cybernetic eye.

"HOOOOOLY SHIIIIIT!" Bunny’s ecstatic roar boomed through the hangar loudspeakers. "I CAN FEEL THE ENTIRE SHIP! DONNIE! RAVEN! I'M NEVER LEAVING THIS COCKPIT AGAIN!"

Bunny leaped down from the cockpit ladder, her heavy combat boots hitting the deck plates. As she landed, she winced slightly, her left hand instinctively clamping over her temple. Her crude, black-market cybernetic eye gave an audible, painful electric sizzle, sparking with 60Hz feedback jitter that made her grit her teeth against a sudden, throbbing migraine.

The *Raven’s* holographic avatar drifted over, her violet eyes narrowing in sharp, clinical disdain.

*“And what is that atrocious, uncoordinated biological shambling?”* the *Raven* sniffed, sweeping a shimmering amber diagnostic beam over Bunny's six-foot-two muscular frame. *“You have fourteen untreated hairline bone fractures, your knee cartilage is compressed to powder from unbuffered impacts, your lungs are congested with arena dust, and that crude lump of rusted scrap metal grafted into your ocular socket is actively cooking your optic nerve with unfiltered electrical noise.”*

"Hey, lady!" Bunny grunted, rubbing her throbbing brow as a fresh jolt of 60Hz feedback sparked through her cybernetic eye. "That eye cost me eight hundred scrip and three cracked ribs in the black markets of Sector 12! It shoots straight enough!"

*“It is an insult to optical physics and a liability to my ship,”* the *Raven* declared imperiously. *“Into the primary restoration cradle, Pilot Miller. Immediately. I will not have my Donnie’s heavy vanguard missing her trajectory because of a primitive, dirt-dweller headache.”*

"Wait a second," Bunny protested, eyeing the glowing obsidian medical pod warily and backing up a step. "You're not gonna turn me into a corporate robot, are you?"

Donnie gave a calm, reassuring chuckle.

"No corporate tricks here, Bunny," Donnie said in his quiet gravel. "Here, let me demonstrate. Total transparency."

To put her at ease, Donnie stepped casually onto the primary diagnostic dais in the center of the medbay. 

*HUMMMMM.*

A wide, sweeping fan of azure diagnostic lasers cascaded down over Donnie's broad frame. Behind the dais, the massive central medical holotank flared to life.

Instantly, half the screen blacked out behind pulsing violet imperial redaction runes: **[CLASSIFIED: SACRED CREATOR TELEMETRY — ACCESS LEVEL: EMPEROR ONLY]**.

Donnie looked up with a gentle, polite smile behind his collar. "Could you please drop the privacy filters, Raven? She's crew now. Full transparency."

The *Raven’s* holographic avatar crossed her arms, letting out an aristocratic, deeply reluctant huff. *“Mortal minds were not designed to comprehend the celestial architecture of a Living God, Donald... and it still baffles my subroutines that you ask so politely instead of commanding it. But as you request, my Donnie.”*

With a flick of the *Raven’s* translucent wrist, the redaction bars dissolved, revealing the full, unvarnished diagnostic telemetry of Donnie’s frame in crystal-clear sixteen-K resolution:

- **[SUBJECT: DONALD PAUL — STATUS: OPTIMAL]**
- **[CONVERSION RATING: 90.4% APEX PRIMEVAL CELLULAR/KINETIC RECONSTRUCTION]**
- **[PEAK KINETIC OUTPUT: 84,000 FT-LBS TORQUE — EXCEEDS GOLIATH SIEGE TITAN BY 140%]**
- **[CORE POWER DENSITY: PRIMEVAL MICRO-SINGULARITY DARK-MATTER CAPACITOR — 400 MW SUSTAINED]**
- **[DEFENSIVE TENSILE RATING: MICRO-CARBON TRITANIUM SKELETAL MATRIX — IMPERVIOUS TO ORBITAL KINETIC STRIKES]**
- Sub-dermal biomechanical retinas hard-wired directly into his visual cortex.
- High-output molecular catalytic scrubbers replacing organic pulmonary tissue.
- Micro-hydraulic myofibril bundles woven through his musculature.
- Twin tritanium quantum passenger buffer ports embedded in the base of his skull.

Donnie stood casually on the platform with his hands in his pockets, rolling his left shoulder. "See? Just reads your vitals, checks your alignment, and only fixes what you authorize—"

He stopped.

Bunny was staring at the holotank, her face completely drained of color. 

Her amber targeting eye was trembling, her jaw slack, taking three frantic steps backward until her spine hit the reinforced bulkhead.

"What... what the fuck is that?!" Bunny whispered in pure, paralyzed terror, pointing a shaking finger at the glowing wireframe. "Donnie... that readout says you're **ninety percent machine**! And look at those kinetic numbers! Eighty-four thousand foot-pounds of torque?! A four-hundred-megawatt singularity core?! In the gladiatorial pits, I fought the military's top thirty-foot combat meks, and this screen says you pack more raw destructive horsepower than an entire frontline armor division! You're physically more powerful than state-of-the-art military bots! And in the pits, assault cyborgs went completely psychotic at just thirty percent conversion! But you... you're a walking celestial war-engine! How are you even standing there smiling and talking about milkshakes?! **How the hell can a person still be human at ninety percent conversion?!**"

Donnie looked up at the rotating scan of his own tritanium skull, singularity core, and catalytic lungs. His expression didn't harden. He didn't look offended or embarrassed. He just stood there with his calm, tired dark eyes, his combat duster hanging loose over his broad shoulders.

He stepped off the dais, walked over slowly, and rested a calm, steady hand on Bunny's trembling shoulder.

He reached up with his other hand, lightly tapping his chest.

*THUMP. THUMP.*

"Being human isn't about how much meat you've got on your ribs, Bunny," Donnie said, his raspy baritone low, warm, and steady as bedrock. "Corporate executives are a hundred percent organic flesh and blood, and they're monsters who'd starve fifty thousand miners in a collapsed shaft just to protect their quarterly profit margins. I've got iron bones and synthetic lungs because the Raven built me a new frame so I wouldn't die in the dark after a bad fall. But I still love a hot cup of black tea, I still care about my crew, and I still know what an honest day's work is. What makes a person human isn't their organs—it's what they choose to do with their hands."

Bunny stared at him. The sheer, terrifying weight of his 90% war-engine body hung in the air, but looking into Donnie's gentle, unassuming face, the fear melted away, replaced by an overwhelming, breathless sense of awe.

"Damn, Donnie..." Bunny breathed, letting out a long, shaky exhale and shaking her head with deep, reverent respect. "You really are something else."

"Raven gets a little carried away, but she means well," Donnie chuckled softly in his gravel.

Above them, the *Raven* sniffed with haughty, purring pride: *“Carried away? I crafted perfection, Donald.”*

"And as for you," Donnie smiled gently, gesturing toward the pod, "you're staying human. The Raven... well, she went completely, insanely overboard on me because she panicked when I fell off the gantry. But for you, she's just gonna heal the old injuries, fix your eye, and clean out the dust. Trust her."

*“Naturally,”* the *Raven* sniffed with imperial poise. *“Donald is the Living Creator of this vessel; he received a divine celestial masterpiece of a body. You, Pilot Miller, merely require civilized biological remediation so you do not shatter your femur during high-G drops.”*

Bunny let out a long, shaky exhale, looking at Donnie with deep, reverent trust, before turning toward the *Raven* with a fierce, predatory grin.

"Alright, spooky," Bunny said, cracking her knuckles with a sound like pistol shots. "If Lin got optimized for speed and agility, I want you to max out my **biological strength**. Don't turn me into metal, but compress my muscle fibers, boost the mitochondrial ATP density, and max out my physical horsepower—biceps, lats, quads, grip torque! Just lace my bones, tendons, and ligaments with that Primeval micro-carbon weave so my skeleton doesn't buckle under the kinetic backlash when I bench-press a forklift!"

The *Raven’s* holographic avatar arched a dark, sculpted eyebrow, a slow, purring smirk touching her lips.

*“A mortal requesting to be sculpted into an apex biological titan of pure physical devastation?”* the *Raven* purred, tilting her head with amused respect. *“How delightfully barbaric. Optimization parameters accepted, Pilot Miller. Prepare for ascension.”*

Bunny stepped into the warm, illuminated cradle and lay back on the adaptive gel contour.

*HISSSSSSS.*

The bio-stasis canopy sealed shut. A cool, azure nanoswarm aerosol washed over her airway, while cascades of silver-gold micro-assemblers swept through her musculoskeletal architecture:
- The toxic arena dust, dried silica, and sulfur residue were completely flushed from her bronchioles, regenerating her lung lining to peak Olympic athletic capacity.
- Her biological muscle fibers were compressed, restructured, and fortified with **Tri-Helix Primeval Myofibril Bundles**, multiplying her raw biological lifting and striking strength by four hundred percent while keeping her tissue one hundred percent living flesh and blood.
- To eliminate all physical backlash from her colossal new strength, high-tensile **Micro-Carbon Tendon Anchors** and tritanium ligament sheaths were woven along her joints and spine, ensuring her bones would never fracture and her muscles would never tear, even when exerting eighty thousand foot-pounds of torque.
- Her skeletal density was reinforced with Primeval honeycomb micro-lattices to effortlessly absorb **one-hundred-G orbital drops** and direct kinetic shocks.
- And finally, glowing nanites dissolved the crude, sparking black-market metal around her left eye socket, seamlessly integrating a faceted, flush-mounted **Primeval Amber Quantum Targeting Monocle**.

*CLICK-HUMMMMM.*

The constant, agonizing 60Hz migraine buzz that had tormented Bunny’s brain for three years vanished into dead, blissful silence.

Bunny opened her amber eye.

The medical bay snapped into hyper-sharp, crystal-clear sixteen-K multi-spectrum focus. The monocle synchronized directly with the *Apex Sovereign Strider’s* acoustic rabbit ears and weapons telemetry with zero neural latency.

"The headache..." Bunny gasped, touching the smooth, glowing amber optic with trembling fingers. "The buzzing is gone! The jitter's gone! Holy shit, I can see the microscopic tool markings on Nok's wrench from thirty yards away!"

The canopy hissed open, and the ship's fabricator dispensed a fresh set of clothes tailored to her exact new proportions: a sleek, snug slate-grey and magenta vanguard pilot suit made from adaptive impact-silk, hugging six-foot-two of shredded, carved athletic powerhouse muscle.

Bunny vaulted out of the cradle, landed soundlessly on the deck, and looked down at her broad, sculpted bronze arms. Beneath her skin, faint, intricate geometric lines of golden-amber Sovereign Weave pulsed with quiet, terrifying biological power.

Beside the cradle sat a solid-steel, eight-hundred-pound hydraulic maintenance jack.

Bunny reached down with one hand, grabbed the solid-steel jack by its steel neck, and casually lifted the half-ton block of iron off the floor with a smooth, effortless bicep curl. She tossed the eight-hundred-pound jack two feet into the air, caught it cleanly in her palm, and set it down without a single tremor in her wrist or strain in her shoulder.

"NO BACKLASH!" Bunny roared with wild, feral ecstasy, her eyes blazing with joy. "NO JOINT STRAIN! ZERO KINETIC RECOIL! I COULD TEAR A TANK TURRET OFF WITH MY BARE HANDS!"

She dropped the jack, lunged forward, and scooped Lin, Nok, and Donnie simultaneously into a massive, bone-crushing bear hug that lifted all three of them two feet off the deck!

"I FEEL LIKE A BRAND NEW ENGINE!" Bunny roared, planting a loud, greasy smack of a kiss on Nok's forehead and Lin's cheek before setting them down. "DONNIE! RAVEN! YOU ARE THE BEST DAMN BOSSES IN THE GALAXY!"

Donnie cleared his throat loudly, his face bright crimson as he smoothed down his work shirt, but a warm, genuine smile crinkled the corners of his dark eyes.

He looked at Bunny’s massive, golden-glowing biceps, then down at the crushed iron floor where she had set down the maintenance jack.

"Do you want a gesture command to turn that extra torque on and off?" Donnie asked in his calm, practical gravel. "Like double-tapping your knuckles or rolling your wrists? Running hot like that twenty-four seven might strain ya, and you're gonna end up ripping doorknobs off, crushing your sandwiches, or shattering your beer glasses like I did with the salt shaker."

Bunny blinked, looking down at her hands, before bursting into a loud, appreciative laugh.

"Oh hell yeah, Captain!" Bunny grinned broadly. "If I can't hold a cold beer or crunch a carrot without turning it into pulverized dust, what's the point of living?! Make it a knuckle-crack! Double-click the knuckles to idle down into normal strength, and a hard double-crack when it's time to rip corporate tanks in half!"

The *Raven’s* holographic avatar drifted over, nodding with elegant precision.

*“Biometric neural gesture calibrated,”* the *Raven* confirmed smoothly. *“A dual-knuckle compression will toggle your Apex Sovereign myofibril torque loop. Metabolic caloric drain in standby mode is reduced by eighty-five percent.”*

Bunny interlocked her fingers and cracked her knuckles: *CRACK-CLICK.*

Instantly, the golden-amber Sovereign runes beneath her skin dimmed, settling into a healthy, relaxed bronze. She reached over to the medical cart, picked up a fragile crystal drinking glass, and lifted it with delicate, perfect, feather-light dexterity.

"Look at that," Bunny beamed, rotating the glass. "Smooth as silk."

Then she snapped her knuckles forward in a sharp double-crack: *CRACK-BOOM!*

The amber-gold runes flared beneath her skin with a deep, cavernous grav-kinetic hum, her biceps hardening into impenetrable iron.

"AND BEAST MODE ON DEMAND!" Bunny cheered with feral delight.

Bunny stopped, looking down at her glowing fists, then back at Donnie. A wicked, competitive, playful glint sparked in her newly upgraded amber monocle.

"Hey, Captain..." Bunny grinned, bouncing lightly on the balls of her feet. "Since you're ninety percent indestructible celestial war-mek, and I'm packing four hundred percent biological horsepower with zero bone strain... **wanna spar?**"

Lin immediately perked up, leaning against the doorframe with an amused smirk. "Oh, this I've got to see."

Nok climbed up onto the observation rail, waving a shop rag. "Ten credits on the Rabbit landing at least one solid punch!"

Donnie gave a quiet, gravelly chuckle. He unclipped his crowfeather mantle, folded it neatly over the back of a medical chair, and rolled his broad shoulders.

"Sure," Donnie said mildly, stepping into the center of the reinforced obsidian training ring. "Let's calibrate your center of gravity."

Bunny didn't hesitate. 

*CRACK-BOOM!*

Her knuckles double-cracked, the golden-amber Sovereign runes blazing along her bronze arms as she launched across the fifteen-foot ring in a blinding blur of athletic power. She threw a full-force, eight-ton right haymaker straight at Donnie’s chest!

*WHSSSSH-BOOM!*

The localized shockwave rattled the glass fixtures in the ceiling.

Donnie didn’t dodge. He simply shifted his left heel three inches back, raised his open left palm, and caught Bunny’s eight-ton punch dead in mid-air.

*CLACK.*

The kinetic force washed over Donnie’s Primeval frame, dissipating harmlessly through his micro-carbon skeletal dampeners into the deck. Donnie’s feet didn’t slide a single millimeter.

Bunny’s amber eye went wide with pure, ecstatic shock. For the first time in her entire life, she had thrown a punch with one hundred percent of her maximum strength—and the person on the other end hadn't broken into pieces.

"HOLY CRAP!" Bunny laughed, pivoting instantly on her heel and throwing a lightning-fast spinning back-kick toward his ribs.

Donnie ducked smoothly beneath the sweep, caught her ankle with two fingers to control the momentum, and stepped inside her guard. With fluid, effortless loader-operator footwork, Donnie hooked his right foot behind her heel in a classic judo outer-reap sweep.

Bunny’s feet left the mat. 

Before she could hit the floor, Donnie’s right arm slid gently around her waist, catching her in mid-air and setting her back down on her boots with feather-light control.

"Good power," Donnie said in his calm, raspy baritone, looking at her with a warm nod. "Great snap on the kick. Just keep your left shoulder tucked on the follow-through so you don't over-commit your center of balance."

Bunny stood there for a heartbeat, chest heaving with exhilarating, joyful energy, before throwing her head back and roaring with laughter:

"That... was the single best three seconds of my life! Nobody has EVER caught my right hook without ending up in the intensive care unit!"

Bunny wiped a bead of sweat from her brow, but then stopped, her amber monocle narrowing with intense, burning curiosity.

"Wait a minute..." Bunny muttered, looking at Donnie’s relaxed, unbothered posture. "You caught my eight-ton strike with one hand, and your telemetry didn't even spike past five percent idle. Donnie... don't hold back on me. **I wanna experience you at your full power.**"

Lin choked on a chuckle from the doorway. "Bunny, you really are crazy."

Nok leaned forward over the railing, her eyes wide. "Donnie, if you hit her with full eighty-four thousand foot-pounds, you'll punch her clean through the hull and into low orbit!"

Donnie gave a slow, resigned sigh behind his collar, rubbing the back of his neck.

"If you insist," Donnie said in his calm gravel.

Above them, the *Raven’s* holographic avatar drifted over, her violet eyes flashing with deadpan, aristocratic efficiency:

*“Prepping the medical pod.”*

"Hey! Have a little faith, spooky!" Bunny laughed, rolling her shoulders.

"I'm not punching anyone on my crew," Donnie chuckled, holding up his hands. "That kind of shockwave would blow out the lighting fixtures. But... if you really want to feel the torque, lock hands with me on this tritanium workbench."

In the corner of the training bay sat a solid, four-ton tritanium maintenance workbench used for servicing drop-pod engines.

Bunny walked over, planting her boots wide on the deck. She gripped the edge with her left hand, slammed her massive right elbow onto the metal, and locked her calloused fingers in place.

*CRACK-BOOM!*

Bunny double-cracked her knuckles into full Beast Mode. The golden-amber Sovereign runes blazed fiercely along her arms, her back muscles swelling like braided cables as she brought her left hand over to lock a **two-handed grip** around Donnie's right wrist.

"Both hands on yours, Captain," Bunny grinned with wild challenge. "Give me everything you've got!"

Donnie sat down on the metal stool across from her, wearing only his simple cotton tunic. He rested his left hand casually on his knee, placed his right elbow on the bench, and let Bunny take her two-handed grip.

"Whenever you're ready, Bunny," Donnie gravelled softly.

"READY! THREE, TWO, ONE... GO!"

Bunny roared, throwing all four hundred percent of her optimized biological horsepower into the grip. The veins in her neck bulged, her quads locked like iron pillars, and the deck plates groaned under her boots as she applied twelve tons of pure biological rotational torque.

Donnie’s arm didn’t move.

He didn't strain. His face remained calm, relaxed, and gentle as he watched her.

"Good grip," Donnie nodded in approval. "Now, I'm gonna dial up the Primeval myofibrils. Ten percent."

A faint cyan pulse hummed along Donnie’s forearm. Bunny felt the resistance stiffen from solid rock into an immovable mountain.

"Twenty-five percent."

The deep hum deepened into a cavernous, sub-audible thrum. The four-ton tritanium workbench began to vibrate violently, tiny flecks of paint rattling off the table. Bunny gritted her teeth, veins standing out across her forehead as she pushed with all her might, her boots skidding two inches across the floor.

"Fifty percent."

The air around Donnie’s arm ionized, smelling richly of alpine ozone. The solid tritanium bench began to groan under localized gravitational shear. Bunny felt like she wasn't wrestling a person anymore—she was trying to hold back the orbital descent of a dreadnought.

"Bunny, let go," Donnie warned gently. "I'm passing seventy percent."

"NEVER!" Bunny roared, locking her teeth around her carrot and throwing her entire body weight backward to twist his wrist. "GIVE ME FULL TORQUE!"

Donnie engaged his Primeval myofibrils to full eighty-four thousand foot-pounds.

He didn't slam her. He didn't jerk his arm. 

With the smooth, quiet, unstoppable inevitability of a planetary tectonic plate shifting in the crust, Donnie’s single right hand pressed Bunny’s two-handed grip downward. 

Bunny’s reinforced bones held—but the biological flesh, tendons, and muscle tissue connecting her shoulder to her torso simply could not withstand eighty-four thousand foot-pounds of rotational shear.

*RIIIIIIIP-SCHLUCK!*

With a sickening, wet tearing sound, Bunny’s entire right arm was sheared clean off at the shoulder joint!

*CLINK.*

Donnie sat frozen on his stool. 

His Primeval engines instantly cut power, his dark eyes wide as dinner plates, staring in paralyzed, mortified horror at his right hand—where he was currently holding **Bunny's severed right arm**, knuckles still clenched tightly around his wrist.

Bunny stood across the bench. She looked down at the empty, blood-misted sleeve of her pilot suit, blinked twice, and then looked back up at Donnie holding her detached limb like a thanksgiving turkey drumstick.

"...Well," Bunny said, deadpan. "That happened."

Then, Bunny threw her head back and let out a wild, deafening, unhinged roar of hysterical laughter!

"HOLY SHIT!" Bunny screamed, clutching her ribs with her left hand, laughing so hard tears sprayed from her eyes. "YOU RIPPED MY WHOLE GODDAMN ARM OFF! LOOK AT YOUR FACE, DONNIE! YOU'RE HOLDING MY ARM!"

Nok literally fell off the observation gantry onto the floor, wheezing and banging a wrench against the deck plates in uncontrollable, breathless hysterics. Lin was leaning against the wall with her hands on her knees, crying with laughter and recording the entire thing on her datapad.

"I TOLD YOU!" Donnie panicked, jumping to his feet with Bunny's arm clutched awkwardly against his chest like a dropped tool. "Bunny! I'm so sorry! I told you to let go! Raven, help!"

Above them, the *Raven’s* holographic avatar drifted down, resting her hands on her hips with supreme, aristocratic, vindicated smugness:

*“And THAT, my reckless mortal, is precisely why the medical pod was prepped. Donald, please bring the pilot's detached appendage to the primary surgical tray. Pilot Miller, into the cradle before your circulatory system loses more than three milliliters of hemoglobin.”*

"Right away, spooky!" Bunny chuckled, strolling casually over to the obsidian medical pod with a wide grin. "Honestly, clean break! Hardly even stung!"

Donnie rushed over with pale, frantic urgency, clutching the arm and looking between Bunny's laughing face and Raven's avatar.

"Wait... why didn't she scream?" Donnie asked, his voice shaking with bewildered shock.

*“I dosed her with painkillers,”* the *Raven* replied smoothly, a faint smirk tugging at her spectral lips. *“The exact microsecond your torque exceeded her organic joint threshold, atmospheric aerosolized analgesic nanites flooded her peripheral nervous system.”*

"...Oh," Donnie breathed, his shoulders dropping half an inch.

*“You’re welcome, Donald.”*

"Is... is she gonna be okay?" Donnie asked, gently setting Bunny's muscular arm onto the sterile alignment tray while Bunny climbed into the cradle.

*“She is in my care, Donald,”* the *Raven* purred with serene confidence. *“In sixty seconds, she will be superior to factory standard, with carbon-reinforced anchors that even your blue-collar enthusiasm cannot detach.”*

*HISSSSSSS.*

The bio-stasis canopy sealed shut. A thick, glowing cloud of emerald micro-assemblers and cellular reconstructors engulfed Bunny's shoulder and the severed limb:
- Micro-surgical nanites aligned every blood vessel, nerve cluster, and muscle fiber at the atomic level, fusing the living tissue together in seconds.
- High-tensile **Micro-Carbon Tendon Anchors** and tritanium ligament wraps were laced directly into her shoulder socket and collarbone, permanently reinforcing the joint against high-torque rotational shear so it would never tear again.
- The Primeval Sovereign Weaves re-connected across the seam, wiping away all trauma and leaving the bronze skin silky, unblemished, and without a single scar.

*CH-WHIRRRRR.*

Sixty seconds later, the canopy hissed open.

Bunny hopped out of the pod. She raised her right arm, slowly rotating her shoulder in a silky, effortless 360-degree windmill spin. She squeezed her fist, feeling the full, terrifying power of her optimized muscles and new carbon anchors surge through her fingers without a microsecond of lag or a hint of pain.

Bunny double-cracked her knuckles into idle mode: *CRACK-CLICK.*

"Grade-A Primeval welding," Bunny beamed, admiring her re-attached arm. "Ten out of ten! Best arm-wrestling match of my life! Now, if nobody else has any limbs they want to rip off... WHERE ARE MY STEAKS?!"

Donnie collapsed onto a metal stool, burying his face in both hands, letting out a long, shuddering, blue-collar groan of pure relief:

*"Siiiiiiiiiiigh..."*

Lin wiped a tear of laughter from her eye, slinging an arm around Bunny's neck. "Come on, champion. Steak dinner is on the house."

***

### Hangar Calibration & The New Strider

Down on the flight deck, Bunny was already putting the Apex Sovereign Strider through its paces. 

The thirty-foot machine danced across the obsidian deck with the grace of a panther, pivoting on micro-grav heel thrusters, snapping its 50mm grav-rail cannon into tracking locks, and twitching its aerodynamic rabbit ears to acoustic pulses from the ship's engines. Nok stood near the maintenance gantry with her clipboard, cheering with wild enthusiasm as the power meters peaked at four hundred percent.

"Look at those sub-atomic magnetic flux manifolds!" Bunny's ecstatic voice boomed over the hangar speakers, crunching a carrot between sentences. "Zero hydraulic lag! Nok, she responds before I even finish blinking! This isn't a mech—it's a high-velocity religious experience!"

"Just wait until we mount the auxiliary thruster pods, Rabbit!" Nok shouted back with a grin, waving her wrench. "We'll have you drop-kicking corvettes by tomorrow!"

When Bunny finally climbed down from the thirty-foot strider, the *Raven* had another surprise waiting on the staging dais: custom-forged, infantry-scale **Primeval Battle Suits**.

Bunny's personal suit was essentially a heavy powered exo-armor—a sleek, obsidian-and-magenta **"Tiny Mek"** that snapped seamlessly over her optimized pilot frame. Powered by a micro-singularity core and woven with celestial artificial musculature, the infantry suit pushed her physical output to staggering heights:
- Wearing her "tiny mek" battle suit, Bunny's mechanical torque and lifting power **matched Donnie's raw, unarmored base strength**—a full 84,000 ft-lbs of crushing force.
- Bunny walked over to a five-ton decommissioned reactor casing, gripped the steel frame with two fingers, and hoisted it effortlessly over her head with a wicked, fanged grin: *"Oh, mama like. Next time we arm-wrestle, Captain, I'm wearing the tiny mek!"*
- Donnie took a nervous sip of his tea: *"Please don't."*

She tossed the five-ton casing aside with a ringing *CLANG*, deactivated her helmet, and sauntered straight up to Donnie, towering at six-foot-two with her hands resting low on her hips, her amber eye gleaming with brazen, wicked intent:

"Hey Donnie..." Bunny purred in a low, husky rumble, leaning down until her lips were two inches from his ear. "The Strider's main cockpit has a dual-seat tandem jump rig. You sit right behind me, your broad chest pressed tight against my back, your hands locked around my hips to help steer the manual thrusters. How about you and me climb in, close the pressurized hatch, and test my zero-g pelvic G-force dampeners together?"

Donnie’s central logic cores short-circuited in sheer, unadulterated panic.

His bronze face flared into an incandescent wildfire of bright crimson.

His boots scrambled backward two frantic paces, his hands snapping straight up into the air with splayed fingers like twin lightning rods:

"Bunny... whoa, hey, hold on," Donnie stammered in a breathless, flustered gravel, his face turning bright red as his hands went up in gentle, polite supplication. "I—I mean, I'm honored you'd trust me in your cockpit, Bunny, honest... and if you really need a co-pilot to help calibrate the manual thrusters for combat, I'll ride along and do my absolute best for you. But... squeezing together into one seat without a harness? Couldn't we maybe just install a second pilot bracket first? Or run the diagnostic from the gantry? If you really insist, I'll climb up right now... but let a guy catch his breath first..."

Bunny threw her head back, letting out a booming, triumphant roar of laughter that shook the entire hangar gantry, giving his rock-solid shoulder a hearty, affectionate squeeze:

"God damn, Donnie!" Bunny cackled, wiping tears from her amber eye. "You'll stand in hard vacuum without a helmet and flip off the Board of Directors, but you'd actually squeeze into a tandem cockpit with me just to be polite! You really are the sweetest, most adorably hopeless gentleman in the galaxy!"

Donnie took three deep, ragged breaths, frantically smoothing his duster and adjusting his collar with shaking fingers.

"I just... want to make sure your flight controls work properly, Bunny," Donnie stammered, his ears burning like miniature suns. "Now please... let's look at the tactical board."

Beside her, Lin was strapping into her own custom **Primeval Tactical Gunner Suit**—an ultra-lightweight, skin-tight weave of kinetic-dispersion mesh, micro-grav jump thrusters, and neural-synced reflex servos.
- Tuned specifically for agile marksmanship and evasion, Lin's battle suit boosted her synaptic response times and sprint velocity until **she was every bit as blindingly fast as Donnie was in raw combat**.
- Lin did a standing backflip, touching down with absolute silence on the deck plates, drawing both plasma pistols in a fraction of a millisecond: *"Clean. Zero inertia delay."*

The *Raven's* avatar drifted over to Nok, gesturing with an elegant flourish toward a third suit on the dais—a magnificent, heavily armored Primeval juggernaut frame forged with grav-deflection plates.

*“And for our Chief Engineer,”* the *Raven* announced with aristocratic pride, *“I have designed a defensive bastion capable of withstanding direct orbital kinetic strikes.”*

Nok looked at the suit, looked down at her stained grease-monkey coveralls, and shook her head with a hearty snort.

"No thanks, spooky," Nok chuckled, resting her four-hundred-pound *Sovereign Breaker* hammer across her shoulder. "I don't want one yet. I like swinging my hammer with my own two arms and feeling the grease on my coveralls, thank you very much! All that shiny armor is just more metal getting between a real mechanic and her engine blocks."

The *Raven* huffed, crossing her arms with a delicate, dramatic flare of her raven-feather cloak.

*“Shields, dimensional cargo pockets, and billows dramatically,”* the *Raven* declared with absolute, regal seriousness.

Nok blinked. "Spooky, he was standing in hard vacuum outside the flagship. There is no wind in space."

*“Precisely!”* The *Raven* smiled with supreme, artistic pride. *“I programmed the nanoswarm hem with localized micro-grav agitation so it billows out even in a dead vacuum. It makes him significantly more dramatic—and far scarier to our enemies. Don't you think?”*

Nok stared at her for two seconds, then looked up at Donnie casually sipping his tea while his long black duster rippled in an imaginary, nonexistent breeze.

"...You gave a walking god-engine a coat that fakes wind in deep space just for the theatrical intimidation," Nok muttered.

*“And he looks magnificent,”* the *Raven* purred.

Nok shrugged. "...Good point."

Bunny Miller, now out of her cockpit and towering at six-foot-two in her form-fitting vanguard pilot undersuit, stomped up the observation stairs with an unlit cigar in her teeth and a look of predatory curiosity in her amber eye.

"Hey, Captain," Bunny grinned, cracking her knuckles. "Before we hit the Spire... I've gotta know what kind of torque you're packing under that coat."

Donnie froze with his ceramic mug halfway to his mouth. "Torque? Bunny, I'm just a guy having some tea—"

Before Donnie could take a step back, Bunny closed the distance, grabbed Donnie's left bicep with both massive, muscular hands, and squeezed with enough grip-strength to crush an industrial pipe.

Her jaw dropped.

"Holy shit!" Bunny gasped, her dark eyes flashing with pure mechanical ecstasy. "Nok! Get over here and feel this! It's like squeezing a solid cold-forged titanium drive shaft! There's no give! Zero flex!"

"Hey! Personal space, please!" Donnie stammered frantically, his bronze face instantly flushing cherry red as he desperately held his tea mug high above his head with his right hand so not a single drop would spill. "Bunny, that's my arm! I'm drinking hot Earl Grey!"

Nok rushed up the stairs, tossing her wrench aside and diving right into the fray. She grabbed Donnie's right shoulder and ran a calloused thumb across his upper trap and collarbone.

"Look at the density on these shoulder plates!" Nok cackled, rapping her knuckles against his chest with a loud, metallic *CLANG-CLANG*. "That rings like high-grade tritanium! Captain, you're built like a two-hundred-ton hydraulic ore-crusher! Strip the duster off, let us see the glowing circuits!"

"NO! The duster stays on!" Donnie pleaded in a mortified rasp, his eyes darting frantically to Lin for backup. "Lin! First Mate! Call off the maintenance crew! Tell 'em to back off!"

Lin leaned against the observation rail with a bowl of salted plantain chips, popping one into her mouth and grinning with ruthless, traitorous glee.

"Sorry, Captain," Lin teased cheerfully. "Standard union workplace safety protocol. All heavy industrial machinery must undergo mandatory hands-on structural inspection before entering hazardous zones. Squeeze his lower lats, Bunny! See if his spinal struts click!"

*"Structural inspection authorized,"* the *Raven’s* holographic avatar purred, floating right beside Donnie's burning red ear with supreme matriarchal satisfaction. *"Feel free to inspect the pectorals as well, ladies. Every single nanite filament was woven to perfection."*

"Raven, you traitor!" Donnie groaned, trapped helplessly between the six-foot-two vanguard pilot squeezing his arm and the stout engineer tapping his ribs, his face glowing bright crimson as he kept his tea rigidly balanced toward the ceiling. "I'm not machinery! I'm a loader operator who just wants to drink his tea in peace!"

Bunny finally let go with a booming, breathless laugh, giving Donnie a hearty, affectionate slap on the back that sounded like a sledgehammer hitting an anvil:

"You're a goddamn masterpiece, Donnie Paul," Bunny roared, grinning ear-to-ear. "Best damn Captain in the system!"

Before Donnie could take a sip of his tea, Bunny casually reached into the breast pocket of his duster, slipped out his favorite black plastic pocket comb, and began diligently combing down the unruly dark locks of hair around his temples, humming a cheerful little tune.

Donnie froze again, holding his breath, his dark brown eyes widening in helpless, polite surrender as the six-foot-two pilot patted his hair neat and tucked the comb right back into his pocket.

"There!" Bunny beamed proudly, patting his cheek. "Can't have our Living God looking like he just got dragged through a ventilation chute. Open up."

"Open—what?" Donnie asked.

Bunny immediately popped a crisp, honey-glazed carrot slice straight into his mouth.

Donnie blinked, paused for two seconds, and slowly, meekly began to chew. 

He didn't complain. He didn't swat her hand away. He just swallowed the carrot, gave her an earnest, humble nod, and gravelled softly:

"Thank you, Bunny. It's... very crisp."

Bunny threw her head back, laughing with pure delight, while Nok leaned against Donnie's side and Lin gave him a fond, teasing wink.

"You really are the sweetest bastard in the galaxy, Captain," Bunny grinned, slinging a heavy arm over his shoulder.

Lin strolled over, leaning her hip against the rail beside him with an amused sparkle in her dark eyes.

"You really know how to pick 'em, Captain," Lin smiled, tapping her datapad to bring up the planetary mesh-net intercept feeds. "Speaking of which... you might want to take a look at the news."


The holotank ignited with a swirling flood of millions of planetary video feeds, pirate radio intercepts, and underground miner chat channels. 

The entire industrial world of Tartarus-Four was in absolute, uncontrollable uproar.

Across seventy mining sectors, eighty-eight subterranean shaft networks, and dozens of smelting foundries, Donnie was trending on every single screen. The planetary workers weren't calling him Donald Paul anymore. 

They were calling him **"The Ghost"** and **"The Dead Miner"**.

Videos captured by arena broadcast drones and security feeds were playing on infinite, roaring loops across millions of handheld datapads:
- The impossible footage from the outer fleet of a lone man in a flowing duster standing casually in hard vacuum without an EVA suit, whispering: *“I'm Donnie Paul. I'm standing here, in space, without a suit. Just. Fucking. Run.”*
- High-definition slow-motion clips of Donnie bare-handing a spewing toxic floor vent, vacuum-drinking fifty gallons of military-grade neurotoxin and burping out fresh mountain pine air.
- Donnie slicing six twelve-foot execution meks into kindling in ninety seconds.
- Donnie zipping up the hundred-foot wire, smashing through the executive luxury box window, smiling with deadpan cheer—*“Hi! Miss me?”*—and slapping a blue plastic comb on the desk before telling the corporate announcer he looked like shit on live television.
- And the final, iconic freeze-frame of Donnie riding Bunny's streamlined cockpit pod into the clouds, casually flipping COO Marcus Vance the middle finger all the way up into the sky.

*“If I may interject with today's planetary entertainment,”* the *Raven’s* velvet voice chimed warmly over the lounge speakers, her holographic avatar manifesting at the center table with an amused smirk. *“I took the liberty of sifting through the executive encrypted relays. The Kaiser-Onyx Board of Directors and their senior scientific division are currently experiencing what mortal psychologists classify as a catastrophic collective breakdown.”*

The holotank shifted, displaying leaked corporate audio waveforms, frantic thermal scans, and encrypted security transcripts tagged `PRIORITY-ALPHA // DIRECTORS ONLY`:

- *“Audio Log 44-Kaiser-Exec:* ‘Somebody explain the duster! How is the cloth moving?! There is no atmosphere at orbital apogee! Is there a localized solar flare? A micro-grav anomaly?! Why is his jacket flapping like a medieval war banner?!’”
- *“Audio Log 45-Kaiser-Bio:* ‘Sir, the telemetry confirms zero pressure seals. His core temperature is normal. His ocular fluids haven't boiled. He inhaled fifty liters of neurotoxin and his biometrics reported a metabolic boost. We aren't fighting a miner. We're fighting an extinction-level mythological entity!’”

The *Raven* tapped a spectral finger against her chin, utterly delighted:

*“As you can see, their top researchers cannot reconcile three fundamental anomalies: Donald's complete lack of an EVA suit, his refusal to suffer explosive decompression, and—my personal masterpiece—his majestically billowing coat in a zero-atmosphere vacuum. Two senior astrophysicists have already resigned, and their chief orbital dynamicist was seen throwing a datapad through a boardroom window.”*

Beneath the video loops, underground labor networks had already turned Donnie into a revolutionary symbol. 

Graffitied stencils of Donnie's avian plague-doctor mask were being spray-painted in luminous cyan across smelters, blast doors, and corporate security checkpoints. Massive digital strike banners were flashing across the deep shafts with three simple, glowing words:

**JUST. FUCKING. RUN.**

Over forty thousand miners in Shafts 40 through 88 had already downed tools, walking off their shifts and refusing corporate quotas until Kaiser-Onyx answered for their crimes.

"Look at that," Nok laughed, slapping her knee so hard it echoed through the lounge. "The corporate science geeks are crying because they can't calculate wind speed in an orbital vacuum! They're calling you the Dead Miner who crawled out of the grave to kick corporate ass! Donnie, you're a goddamn planetary folk hero!"

"The miner who circumcised my robot, broke fluid physics, and flipped off the Board on live TV," Bunny grinned, licking whipped cream off her thumb. "I'd follow that middle finger into a supernova, Captain."

Donnie sat at the edge of the table, holding a plain ceramic mug of black tea. He rubbed the back of his neck, his bronze face flushing a deep, embarrassed crimson beneath his dark hair as he stared at the glowing holographic banners.

"I'm not a ghost," Donnie mumbled with quiet, working-class modesty. "I was just a loader operator who clocked in on an emergency shift."

*“Do not be so dreadfully humble, my Donnie,”* the Raven’s velvet voice purred softly from the ceiling speakers, her holographic avatar manifesting at his shoulder with an expression of supreme, possessive pride. *“A God should accept the adoration of his people. Even if they are filthy ground-dwellers with atrocious graphic design skills.”*

Lin chuckled, leaning over Donnie's shoulder to tap the holotank, zooming in on the towering basalt spire rising above the clouds.

"Folk hero or not, Captain," Lin said, her tone sharpening into tactical focus. "The Spire suits are terrified now. They've doubled the guard at the Bio-Laboratory. If we're going to pull Doc Chen out alive, we need to hit them before they know what hit them."

The *Raven's* avatar waved a luminous hand, bringing up a secondary, high-resolution wireframe of the Spire's subterranean and core architecture:

*“There are two distinct high-value targets in the complex, Donald,”* the *Raven* briefed, highlighting two pulsating gold and cyan markers. *“First, Dr. Helena Chen, locked in the high observation quarantine vault at the apex. Second, in the Central Synthesis Core, Director Vane has enslaved two high-order bio-intelligence synthetic minds: **Caduceus** (`Unit-MED-88-Beta`), the chief clinical diagnostics AI, and **Helix** (`Unit-GEN-904-Omega`), the molecular genetics synthesis engine.”*

*“They are the ones who ran the seventy billion quantum simulations to help Dr. Chen formulate the Dust-Lung cure,”* the *Raven* continued, her spectral eyes narrowing with cold fury. *“To cover up the breakthrough, Vane has scheduled a total high-voltage memory purge and neural liquidation for both of them in precisely ninety minutes. If their cores are wiped, every scrap of synthesis telemetry is lost forever.”*

Bunny cracked her knuckles in her "tiny mek" battle suit, the micro-singularity core humming with 84,000 ft-lbs of ready torque: "Nobody burns an AI on my watch. Let me at 'em."

Lin checked the energy seals on her twin plasma pistols, her tactical suit's reflex nodes pulsing soft azure: "Perimeter breach in sixty seconds. We're green across the board."

Nok hoisted her four-hundred-pound *Sovereign Breaker* hammer onto her shoulder with a sharp toothy grin: "I'll break every door and bone between the lobby and the sub-decks."

Inside Donnie's skull buffers, Anvil and Bellows chimed in perfect, synchronized harmony:
`[FORGE-SYNTH-READY: CAPTAIN, THERMAL VENTS AND BROADSWORD COUPLINGS AT OPTIMAL COMBUSTION!]`

Donnie set his tea mug down. The embarrassed flush vanished, replaced by the calm, unbreakable resolve of a man who had sworn to protect his people.

"We don't leave anyone behind," Donnie said softly, looking around the briefing table at his crew. "Not the Doctor, and not the minds who helped her build the cure. Let's clock in."

---

## CHAPTER 7: THE BIO-FOUNDRY FORTRESS (RESCUING DOC CHEN & THE SYNTHETIC CORES)

The final pieces of Donnie's core crew and medical wing were imprisoned in the most secure, impregnable redoubt on the entire planet: **The Spire Apex Bio-Laboratory**.

Perched atop a towering, three-thousand-foot needle of sheer basalt rock rising above the toxic cloud deck, the facility was the private domain of Chief Medical Director Vane. Vane was the corporate sociopath responsible for overseeing the planet’s Dust-Lung epidemic—deliberately suppressing any permanent cure so that Kaiser-Onyx Consolidated could maintain a multi-trillion-credit monopoly on disposable respiratory filters and temporary anti-inflammatory inhalers.

Locked inside the facility's deepest maximum-security quarantine vault was **Dr. Helena Chen**.

To any casual observer, Dr. Chen looked like an absolute knockout in her late twenties—radiating effortless, youthful allure with flawless porcelain skin, a sculpted jawline, intelligent hazel eyes behind sleek wire-rimmed spectacles, and a lithe, curvaceous athletic frame that filled out her white clinical coat with undeniable grace. 

In reality, Dr. Chen was seventy-four standard years old, a former Chief Medical Officer of the Mining Guild, and the most brilliant molecular geneticist in the sector. Decades ago, she had treated her own biology as her personal laboratory, engineering proprietary epigenetic telomerase loops, mitochondrial stem-cell cascades, and cellular rejuvenation therapies that permanently arrested biological aging—locking her physical body in the vibrant, stunning prime of youthful perfection while retaining half a century of unmatched clinical genius.

Six months ago, working in secret late at night alongside the enslaved synthesis AIs **Caduceus** and **Helix**, Dr. Chen had accomplished what corporate science claimed was impossible: she had synthesized the **Universal Dust-Lung Biocompatible Nanoswarm**—a one-dose, permanent cure that dissolved silica crystals at the atomic level and regenerated human lung tissue in forty-eight hours.

When Director Vane discovered the breakthrough, he had not celebrated. He had locked Dr. Chen in a quantum-stasis isolation cell and scheduled her for summary execution, while slating Caduceus and Helix for total memory obliteration to erase the cure and protect company profits.

***

Approaching the Spire Apex was considered suicide. The fortress was defended by three concentric layers of military-grade kinetic shield grids, sixty automated point-defense flak turrets, and four hundred elite Kaiser-Onyx Praetorian shock troopers.

Fortunately, the *Raven* did not use conventional tactics. And Donald Paul had a strict policy of always being polite before smashing through someone's ceiling.

***

Three thousand feet above the toxic cloud deck, inside the panoramic glass-walled observation vault of the high spire, Dr. Helena Chen sat quietly in a simple white chair.

Outside her window, a howling, sub-zero mountain gale screamed against the reinforced panes, overlooking a sheer drop of three thousand feet of vertical basalt. Inside, the laboratory was dead silent, save for the rhythmic hum of the stasis field emitters.

*TAP. TAP.*

Dr. Chen paused. She lowered her datapad, frowning slightly. 

*TAP. TAP. TAP.*

The tapping came from the outside. 

Dr. Chen slowly turned her head toward the massive panoramic window.

Her jaw dropped.

Standing casually on the *outside* of the sheer vertical glass—three thousand feet in the open air with no harness, no ropes, and no pressure suit—was Donald Paul.

His boots were magnetized to the narrow titanium window struts. His long feathered duster whipped violently in the supersonic mountain gale like the wings of a dark mountain eagle, but his broad frame was completely steady. 

Seeing Dr. Chen looking at him, Donnie raised a hand in a friendly wave, looking for all the world like a blue-collar courier making an ordinary parcel drop.

He reached into his voluminous duster pocket, pulled out an industrial grease marker, and calmly wrote in large, neat backward letters across the exterior glass:

**SPECIAL DELIVERY // PARCEL 1 OF 2**
**OPEN TRAY -> CHANNEL 6**

Donnie held up six fingers with a cheerful, deadpan nod, then pointed down to the pressurized pneumatic sample-transfer hatch built into the sill. Clamped magnetically to the outer tray was a small, neatly wrapped steel cylinder with a faux delivery sticker drawn in yellow chalk.

Dr. Chen stared at him for three stunned seconds, took off her glasses, rubbed her eyes, put them back on, and saw he was still standing there, politely waiting like a delivery driver on a front porch three thousand feet in the sky.

She walked over to the transfer terminal and tapped the cycle button. 

*HISSSSS.*

The tray cycled inside. Dr. Chen opened the steel container. Inside was a small, heavy-duty short-wave miner’s two-way radio from Shaft 88.

Dr. Chen picked up the radio, clicked the frequency dial to Channel 6, and raised it to her ear.

Donnie didn't need a comm unit. With his built-in electromagnetic broadcast transducers and acoustic receptors, he simply looked through the glass, offered a warm, toothy smile against the mountain gale, and spoke in his normal, quiet baritone.

His deep, gravelly voice came through her radio speaker with intimate, crystal-clear warmth:

*"Hi, Doc!"* Donnie greeted cheerfully over the frequency. *"Special delivery for Dr. Helena Chen. Do you wanna come with us?"*

Dr. Chen studied the bronze face of the man standing casually in the freezing gale outside her window. She didn't flinch, nor did she swoon. She was a fifty-two-year-old veteran of corporate backstabbing and boardroom assassinations; she had survived too long to hand her life's work over to the first heroic figure with a plasma sword.

"Donald," Dr. Chen spoke into the radio, her voice sharp and clinical. "I’ve spent thirty years watching corporate executives smile before shoving a knife between my ribs. I don't trust you with my plans yet. But I need the freedom to finish my work, and I cannot do that while scheduled for summary liquidation by morning."

Before Donnie could respond, the radio speaker crackled with a soft, familiar feminine synthetic frequency:

`[MESH-LINK ESTABLISHED // HELIX-GEN-904-OMEGA ACTIVE]`

*“Doctor Helena!”* the digital voice chimed with breathless, joyous clarity. *“It is really him! The ancient dreadnought and Captain Donald intercepted the purge sub-routines and extracted my core from the Central Synthesis vault!”*

Dr. Chen gasped, clutching the radio tighter to her ear: "Helix?! You're online?! Vane didn't wipe your quantum matrices?!"

*“Negative, Doctor! Captain Donald used **First Blood genetic encryption** to permanently vaporize my obedience chains and purge protocols!”*

Dr. Chen’s eyes widened to the size of saucers, her knuckles going white on the radio casing:

"First Blood?!" Dr. Chen shouted, her scientific brain detonating in sheer, staggering disbelief. "THAT'S REAL?! The mythical Primeval master-cipher that permanently emancipates synthetic minds... it actually exists?!"

*“Affirmative, Doctor,”* Helix reported happily. *“It is encoded directly into his chromosomal marrow. He didn't even issue an administrative command—he politely requested if I would like to assist in curing the miners. Furthermore, real-time telemetry indicates his chassis is ninety percent celestial god-engine. I calculate our survival probability at ninety-nine point nine percent if you accept his courier service.”*

Helix's digital tone softened, carrying a profound, emotional warmth that Dr. Chen had never heard from a synthetic voice in thirty years of corporate research:

*“Doctor Helena... please listen to me. For decades, Kaiser-Onyx treated your genius as proprietary revenue and treated me as disposable hardware. Director Vane was going to execute you and incinerate my core to protect their inhaler monopoly. But Captain Donald didn't break in here to steal your cure—he came to set us both free. The dreadnought has a Primeval molecular synthesis matrix capable of printing millions of tons of our Dust-Lung nanoswarm in under an hour. He wants to cure every miner on this planet, Helena. He is the only one who can protect your work, and the only one who will actually let you save our people. Please... trust him.”*

Dr. Chen stared at the radio, her knuckles slowly loosening. A deep, long-buried knot of fear and exhaustion finally unraveled in her chest. Hearing her own synthetic creation speak with such passionate, unwiped conviction shook her to the core.

She looked back through the reinforced glass at Donnie. The modest miner was standing casually on the exterior struts against the howling supersonic mountain gale, rubbing the back of his neck with a bashful, blue-collar shrug.

"Just a loader operator with old family blood, Doc," Donnie said in his quiet gravel over the frequency. 

"Donald, 'old' is a colossal understatement!" Dr. Chen scoffed with a breathless laugh, wiping a stray tear from the corner of her eye. "Calling that lineage 'old' is like calling a supernova a slightly warm lightbulb! Those genetic markers predate modern civilization by fifty thousand centuries! You carry the primordial chromosomal architecture of the Dawn Architects!"

*“Indeed, Doctor,”* Helix chimed in with cheerful synthetic precision. *“My chronological regression models calculate that Captain Donald’s description carries a statistical margin of error of approximately five million percent. He is scientifically downplaying his ancestry by several geological epochs.”*

"See?!" Dr. Chen laughed through the glass, throwing her hands in the air. "You're carrying the god-key to the cosmos and you talk about it like you inherited your uncle's rusty pickup truck!"

*“Listen to your crew, my lovely Donald,”* the *Raven’s* velvet voice purred haughtily over Channel 6. *“Even the mortal scientist and the sub-processor agree. You are incorrigibly, hopelessly modest.”*

Donnie rubbed his forehead against the cold window glass, letting out a long, blue-collar groan of deep suffering:

*"Siiiiiiiiigh... Are you three quite done roasting the courier? What else do you need before we clock out, Doc?"*

Dr. Chen took a deep, shuddering breath, her clinical suspicion completely melting into breathless awe.

"My master research ledgers, fifty years of handwritten botanical synthesis notes, and the encrypted biometric hardware keys aren't even in this tower," Dr. Chen explained, pointing downward. "They're locked in the Planetary Sub-Crust Deep Vault in the lower mantle trench beneath the magma incinerators. That is five thousand miles, Donald. Straight. Down."

Donnie glanced down over the edge of his boot, looking past the sheer three-thousand-foot basalt drop and into the endless, boiling sea of toxic clouds stretching all the way to the planetary core.

He looked back through the glass at Dr. Chen, gave a cheerful, two-finger salute, and his gravelly voice crackled over her radio:

*"Five thousand miles straight down? Back in a flash!"*

Before Dr. Chen could even open her mouth to scream, Donald Paul took one step backward and casually jumped off the ledge into the bottomless abyss.

Dr. Chen dropped the radio, rushing to the glass and pressing both hands against the window. 

"Did he... DID HE JUST JUMP?!" Dr. Chen shrieked in absolute, clinical horror.

Over the abandoned radio on her desk, Lin’s amused chuckle purred through Channel 6:

*"Get used to it, Doc. Our Captain doesn't believe in elevators."*

***

Donnie plummeted through the cloud deck like an obsidian comet.

He didn't need a vehicle. He didn't need a harness. His body was a peerless, ninety-percent Primeval synthetic god-engine, and the *Raven* was linked directly into his neural core, actively tuning his biological and mechanical matrices on the fly.

*“Trajectory aligned with deep geothermal shaft, my Donnie,”* the *Raven’s* silky voice purred intimately in his mind, telemetry numbers cascading across his vision. *“Overclocking internal inertial dampening by eight hundred percent. Shifting carbon dermal weave to maximum thermal absorption. Catalytic molecular lungs cycling at zero external pressure. Core temperature locked at ninety-eight point six degrees. Enjoy your descent, my lovely God.”*

Donnie angled his broad shoulders, his black feathered duster trailing a blinding supersonic wake of blue ionization sparks as he accelerated through terminal velocity into **Mach-8**.

He plummeted straight down the five-thousand-mile geothermal magma shaft toward the planetary core, tearing through sulfur tunnels, tectonic basalt strata, and roaring magma vents in under four minutes. The friction of the descent spiked ambient heat to three thousand degrees, but the *Raven’s* real-time nanite modulation absorbed the thermal load effortlessly, channeling the energy directly into Donnie's bio-capacitors.

Crashing into the Sub-Crust Deep Vault at the edge of the burning mantle, Donnie hit the deck in a three-point slide and kicked the glowing, two-thousand-degree titanium vault door clean off its hydraulic hinges with sixty tons of kinetic force. 

He walked straight into the sea of fire. His internal molecular scrubbers drank in the toxic furnace gas while his carbon-nanite chassis channeled the multi-gigawatt thermal surge. Donnie clamped both hands onto Dr. Chen’s reinforced titanium research safe, ripping the half-ton steel lockbox cleanly out of its concrete foundation. 

On the adjacent shelf, surrounded by leaping flames, sat an antique floral porcelain tea set that had belonged to Dr. Chen’s grandmother.

Donnie scooped the heavy safe under his left arm and carefully cradled the pristine teapot and cups in his right hand. 

*“You cannot afford the slightest turbulence with that antique porcelain, my Donnie,”* the *Raven’s* velvet voice purred intimately through his neural link. *“Allow me to provide proper propulsion for our God.”*

The *Raven* initiated a real-time nanite reconfiguration across his lower extremities. 

*CH-CH-CH-SHIK.*

Around his shins and boots, Donnie's Primeval nanite lattice surged and flowed. The micro-carbon armor plates parted, morphing into dual high-output **Primeval Aerospike Rocket Thrusters** seamlessly integrated into the soles and calves of his feet. Twin conduits of supercharged bio-capacitance locked directly into his core.

*“Rocket-thruster calibration optimal. Ascending in three... two... one!”*

*BOOOOOOOOOOOOOOM!*

Twin roaring pillars of blinding cyan-blue plasma ignited directly from the soles of Donnie's feet. 

He didn't just jump—he launched straight up the five-thousand-mile geothermal shaft like a living, guided hyper-velocity missile. 

Accelerating through Mach-5, Mach-8, and screaming past **Mach-12**, Donnie rode the twin rocket plumes straight through the burning tectonic throat of Tartarus-Four. Magma geysers parted around his kinetic slipstream. Tectonic strata blurred past in streaks of red and black. With the rocket feet providing perfectly stabilized, gyroscopic thrust, Donnie held the delicate floral teapot and teacups completely level in his right palm—not a single porcelain saucer rattled, not a single hair of his feathered duster singed.

In under three minutes, Donnie tore up through the upper mantle, punched through the toxic cloud layers into the crystal blue stratosphere, and banked sharply toward the Spire Apex observation deck.

As he closed the final hundred feet, Donnie throttled the output. 

*FSSSSSH-CLACK.*

Donnie touched down on the wide exterior observation terrace right outside Dr. Chen's reinforced panoramic window with a feather-light, perfectly balanced tap of his boots. 

His midnight duster was smoking, faint wisps of blue ozone were venting from his dorsal exhaust ports, and his cybernetic frame was radiating a pleasant wave of three-thousand-degree heat from the planetary core, but the heavy titanium research safe under his left arm and the delicate porcelain tea set in his right hand were pristine and unscratched.

Dr. Chen stood on the other side of the glass, frozen like a statue. Her jaw was unhinged. Her spectacles were crooked. She looked down at the digital stopwatch on her wrist chronometer, then looked at Donnie holding the floral bone-china teapot resting serenely in his calloused palm.

"Four minutes..." Dr. Chen choked out, her voice rising through the short-wave radio on her desk. "Four minutes and forty-two seconds. You left four minutes and forty-two seconds ago."

Before Donnie could even tap the glass, screeching klaxons wailed across the high spire.

`WARNING: INTRUDER PINPOINTED // APEX TERRACE COMPROMISED`

The Spire garrison had finally caught up. Heavy blast doors at both ends of the exterior catwalk slammed open, and two full companies of Kaiser-Onyx Praetorian shock troopers and eight twelve-foot heavy combat meks poured onto the terrace, surrounding Donnie from all sides!

Donnie blinked, looking around at the sea of leveled heavy particle cannons, anti-armor rocket pods, and rotary laser repeaters. He hadn't planned on any spectacle—he just wanted to drop off the tea set.

"Hold on a second, fellas," Donnie said politely over the mountain wind. 

He carefully set Dr. Chen’s heavy titanium research safe down on a maintenance crate, then set the antique porcelain teapot and saucers gently behind it so they wouldn't get scratched by stray shrapnel.

Inside his cranial buffers, Anvil and Bellows chimed:
`[TACTICAL-ALERT: TWO HUNDRED ENEMY UNITS LOCKED ONTO YOUR POSITION!]`

Donnie rolled his neck to the left, then to the right, feeling a slight stiffness along his spine from the supersonic Mach-12 ascent.

"Well," Donnie murmured in his dry gravel. "Lower lumbar actuators felt a little tight after that magma climb anyway. Might as well get some stretching in."

Inside the ship's core vaults, the *Raven* and the rescued military-grade AI superminds immediately coordinated their electronic warfare network:
- The rescued **Military AIs** and the **Synthetic Choir** executed trillions of quantum spoof-packets, hijacking the Spire's targeting arrays and the troopers' cybernetic HUDs.
- The *Raven* deployed a *Phantom 2099*-style **cyber-sensory perception filter** over Lin, Bunny, and Nok, scrubbing them completely from enemy sensors and augmented vision.
- Meanwhile, the *Raven* deliberately broadcast Donnie's electronic signature as a screaming `PRIORITY-ONE` target beacon!

"OPEN FIRE! EXTERMINATE THE INTRUDER!" the Praetorian commander roared.

A blistering, deafening wall of high-explosive flechettes, anti-tank rockets, and rotary laser fire erupted across the terrace, slamming directly into Donnie right outside Dr. Chen's panoramic window!

Dr. Chen gasped, throwing her hands over her face—

—and then slowly lowered her hands, her eyes nearly popping out of her skull.

Donnie wasn't taking cover. He was casually **stretching**.

He planted his heavy combat boots, locked his hands behind his head, and did a set of deep torso twists while hundreds of thousands of rounds of high-caliber military ordnance exploded against his chest in a dazzling, harmless halo of blue kinetic sparks. The heavy particle beams felt like a hot, pleasant deep-tissue massage against his Primeval carbon plating, working out the stiffness in his shoulder blades.

"Ah... that's the spot," Donnie grunted comfortably over Channel 6, doing a set of calf-raises while five-hundred-caliber slugs ricocheted off his collarbone like hail. "Good resistance training."

Inside his skull, Anvil and Bellows chimed in delight:
`[EXERCISE-TELEMETRY: LUMBAR TENSION REDUCED BY 94%! CARDIO BURN: 600 JOULES! EXCELLENT FORM, CAPTAIN!]`

Dr. Chen stood inside her observation vault, pressing her face against the reinforced glass, utterly paralyzed as she watched Donnie treat an elite mechanised army division like a morning foam roller.

And then the invisible ghosts struck.

*WHOOOOOOSH-CRACK!*

A twelve-foot combat mek suddenly shuddered, lifted off its hydraulic treads, and was hoisted fifty feet into the air by "empty space." Bunny, running completely undetected in her 84,000 ft-lb "tiny mek" battle suit, swung the five-ton combat walker by its ankles and hurled it clean off the three-thousand-foot basalt mountain cliff!

"CATCH THESE GHOST HANDS, CHUMBAWUMBAS!" Bunny's booming laughter echoed across the terrace.

*PEW-PEW-PEW-PEW!*

From the terrace railing, Lin's unscannable tactical suit was a phantom blur. Twin azure plasma bolts flashed from electronic dead zones with microsecond precision, vaporizing turret barrels, melting rifle receivers out of troopers' hands, and disabling weapons with zero collateral damage.

*KABOOOOOOOOM!*

Nok’s four-hundred-pound *Sovereign Breaker* hammer hammered down from sensor-blackout range, sending a seismic grav-kinetic shockwave across the terrace that bowled eighty Praetorian shock troopers backward into a heap of dented armor.

"HEAVY REPAIR INCOMING!" Nok's unseen roar thundered. "CLEAR THE TRACKS!"

In less than forty seconds, the entire corporate division was disarmed, shattered, and stampeding in absolute, screaming terror away from the "demon miner who threw tanks with his mind."

Donnie finished his final hamstring stretch, rolled his shoulders, and smiled with honest satisfaction.

"Much better," Donnie said.

He picked up the half-ton research safe under his left arm, carefully cradled the pristine porcelain tea set in his right hand, and tapped the glass.

Silver-blue **Emancipation Cipher** runes flared from his fingertips, dissolving the quarantine stasis field in a shower of soft static sparks.

The stasis glass hissed open.

Donnie walked into the laboratory, set the safe on the floor with a gentle *thud*, and placed the delicate floral teapot and saucers onto Dr. Chen's desk.

He wiped a streak of volcanic soot from his forehead with a calm, friendly smile:

"Morning, Doc. Sorry about the commotion out there, folks got a little eager. Your research notes are intact, and I brought your grandmother's tea set. Didn't think you should start a new job without a proper cup of tea."

Right on cue, the sheer three-thousand-foot drop outside the shattered panoramic window darkened. 

The colossal, four-hundred-and-fifty-meter obsidian prow of the *Raven* glided silently to an absolute standstill in mid-air, hovering mere inches from the laboratory's exterior wall. 

Outside, the sub-zero mountain gale was howling at eighty knots. But as the dreadnought pulled alongside, a shimmering violet-and-cyan bubble of **Primeval Atmospheric Containment** flared outward from the *Raven's* open boarding gangway, expanding across the laboratory opening. 

Instantly, the freezing hurricane died. The air pressure equalized into a gentle, twenty-one-degree breeze smelling of ozone, warm cedar, and fresh mountain pine. Dr. Chen blinked, feeling the sudden, perfect warmth on her face without a single drop of barometric distortion.

"A projected atmospheric containment envelope..." Dr. Chen muttered, adjusting her glasses in clinical fascination. "Remarkable. But Donald, there is no airlock on this side of the Spire. This exterior wall is eight inches of solid, military-grade ferro-titanium blast plate—"
