He was a red-blooded man who adored her with every fiber of his being, but he was far too polite, too modest, and too honorable to ever dare make a bold move or lay a hand on her curves on his own.

*Well,* Lin thought with pure, affectionate mischief, *let's help the gentleman out.*

Still straddling his lap, Lin reached down, took both of Donnie’s massive tritanium hands, and guided them smoothly down to her sides—placing his broad, warm palms firmly right over the curve of her hips and the snug, sculpted rear of her cargo pants, curling his fingers gently against her shape. 

The moment she released his wrists, Donnie's nanite lattice locked the pose solid.

Donnie’s eyes nearly burst from their sockets. 

His calm tranquility vanished in an instantaneous, thermonuclear modesty meltdown. His hands were literally grasping her backside and hips, resting firmly against her curves, held in place by his own frozen nanotech. He couldn't pull his hands back. He couldn't open his palms. He was actively holding his First Mate's rear, feeling the warmth of her body through the fabric.

Steam practically whistled from his collar seams. His dark eyes flickered frantically down at his own hands, then up at Lin, vibrating in horrified, bashful shock.

*LIN!* his brain screamed in five-alarm union panic. *MY HANDS! GUILD DIRECTIVE NINE! REPUTATIONAL PROTOCOLS!*

Lin leaned forward until her nose was an inch from his, resting her forearms over his shoulders and looking down at him with a sultry, thoroughly amused smirk.

"You've wanted to put your hands there since our second year in the shipyard, Donnie," Lin whispered against his lips, her eyes glittering. "I'm just saving you another fifteen years of stalling."

*“MMMMMMMM-NNNNNNGH!”*

A deep, muffled, sub-harmonic groan vibrated out from Donnie's chest and through his locked throat. It was an extraordinary acoustic phenomenon: a single, desperate sound that somehow contained thirty-three percent gentlemanly despair, thirty-three percent five-alarm panic, and thirty-four percent pure, unadulterated, ecstatic male joy. The sheer resonance of the groan rattled his breastplate and sent little vibrations right through Lin's ribs.

Lin laughed softly against his collarbone, then tilted her head up to look right into his wide, helpless dark eyes with a dangerous, delighted smirk.

"Well, well..." Lin purred, lightly tapping the tip of his nose. "That groan didn't sound mad at all, Captain."

Donnie’s eyes crossed in sheer, helpless mortification, steam hissing faintly from his collar seams.

Floating directly above the couch in her shimmering midnight gown, the **Raven was laughing her head off at him**. 

The ancient, omnipotent dreadnought tossed her holographic head back in bright, musical peals of aristocratic laughter, clutching her ribs in pure, unbridled synthetic joy. She projected glowing, pulsing heart-rate icons and flashing caution signs over Donnie's forehead, pointing down at him and teasing him without a shred of mercy:

*"Oh, look at him, Lin! Look at the mighty conqueror!"* the *Raven* chimed between fits of melodic laughter. *"Three minutes ago he was a legendary ancient war-machine... and now a single pair of cargo pants has completely disabled his primary cognitive matrix! His nanites are practically boiling!"*

Donnie's dark eyes darted frantically toward the *Raven*, begging in silent, desperate morse code: *YOU. ARE. MY. SHIP. HAVE. MERCY.*

The *Raven* only giggled harder, completely ignoring his pleas.

Meanwhile, across the bridge, the rest of the crew hadn't noticed Donnie's domestic hostage crisis in the slightest—they were completely, utterly absorbed in the holoscreen, cheering over his brutal battle feeds:
- **Bunny** was hollering with laughter, repeatedly hitting the replay button on the Gladiator toss: *"LOOK AT THAT TORQUE! He ripped that hydraulic shoulder clean off like a chicken wing!"*
- **Nok** was cheering with a mouth full of spicy pork: *"Clean shear! Look at that forty-five-degree plasma cut! That's Master Guild craftsmanship!"*
- **Vesper** was leaning over her console with a dangerous grin, studying the telemetry angles.
- And **Dr. Helena Chen** was standing completely motionless, her wire-rimmed glasses slightly askew, staring at the screen in stunned, flushed, smoldering clinical awe.

***

The holoscreen finally faded back to the tactical map of Jackson's Basin.

The bridge fell into a brief, breathless silence.

Dr. Chen slowly took off her glasses, rubbed the bridge of her nose, put them back on, and turned around to stare at Donald Paul.

Her chest rose and fell in a deep, unsteady breath. 

"Clinically speaking..." Helena murmured, her voice a mix of breathless shock and pure, simmering heat. "He's not committing suicide. He's committing aggravated assault on an entire planetary defense garrison."

Dr. Helena Chen leaned back against the console, crossing her arms beneath her bust. A slow, predatory, intensely appreciative smile spread across her stunning lips. Her dark eyes ran down the length of Donnie's broad shoulders, his thick chest, his hands firmly grasping Lin's hips, and his frozen, glowing-red face with unmistakable, smoldering admiration.

"Donald Paul... in seventy-four years of corporate research and back-alley survival, that is without a doubt the most unhinged, magnificent, terrifyingly attractive display of masculine authority I have ever witnessed."

Donnie’s eyes widened in sheer, catastrophic modesty overload. Completely locked in place, his hands still firmly planted on Lin's rear, steam began hissing from his ears in frantic, silent desperation.

Lin didn't move an inch. Still straddling his thighs with Donnie's broad hands clamped to her hips, she casually glanced over her bare shoulder at Dr. Chen, a slow, smug, possessively amused smirk playing across her lips.

"He can't reply yet, Doc," Lin purred with casual authority, lightly patting Donnie's rock-hard chest. "And... frankly, I'm not done."

Donnie flicked his dark eyes frantically toward the laughing holographic avatar of the ship, blinking in desperate, terrified morse code: *LET. ME. GO.*

The *Raven* smiled with icy, elegant amusement.

`[COMMAND QUERY RECEIVED: CAPTAIN REQUESTS MOTOR RELEASE]`
`[STATUS: REQUEST DENIED]`
`[REASON: CREW MORALE EXCEEDS OPERATIONAL THRESHOLDS. PROTOCOL EXTENDED INDEFINITELY.]`

"Raven... please..." Donnie managed to vibrate through his paralyzed vocal synth with an agonizing, sub-harmonic buzz.

"Not yet, Donald darling," the *Raven* cooed softly, floating down until her translucent face was right beside his ear. "Lin hasn't finished her inspection, and Dr. Chen is still admiring the scenery. You will stay right where you are until I decide you have suffered sufficiently for the cause."

"Alright, crew," Lin announced over her shoulder, still sitting comfortably straddling her frozen Captain. "The distraction plan is approved. Vesper, prep the gorge run. Donnie and I are going to sit right here and make sure his blood pressure stays properly elevated."

"Understood, First Mate," Vesper laughed, her fingers dancing across the flight console with absolute thrill. "If our boy is pulling every orbital battery off our tail by kicking the Big Boss in the teeth, I'll fly this dreadnought through the eye of a needle."

As the bridge crew dispersed to their battle stations, the teasing banter settled into a quiet, warm stillness. 

Lin remained straddling Donnie's lap, his massive hands still posably locked around the curve of her hips. She looked down at him, her mischievous smirk slowly softening into an expression of profound, tender intimacy. For a few seconds, she just studied his bronze features—the high cheekbones, the faint silver runes glowing faintly beneath his skin, and the wide, vulnerable dark eyes looking up at her with pure, defenseless devotion.

Lin brought both of her hands up, cupping his burning cheeks in her warm palms. 

"Donnie," she whispered softly.

She leaned down and pressed her lips to his.

It was a slow, deep, lingering kiss—warm with the taste of sweet tea and fifteen years of unspoken devotion. 

Donnie’s eyes fluttered shut in absolute, helpless bliss. A wave of brilliant azure light pulsed beneath his skin, his nanite lattices singing in harmonic synchronization as his heart hammered against Lin's ribs. For those few seconds, the entire galaxy ceased to exist; there was no corporate armada, no impending siege, no planetary defense garrison. Just Lin.

Lin slowly pulled back, her breath mingling with his, her golden-brown eyes shining with a fierce, protective warmth. She brushed her thumb gently across his lower lip.

"Come back to me in one piece, Donnie," Lin murmured softly. "You still owe me a drink."

She stood up smoothly, turning her gaze toward the ceiling with a fond smile. 

"Alright, Raven. Let my boy go."

The *Raven* tilted her head, her dark violet eyes glowing with boundless amusement and fierce, possessive affection.

"With pleasure, Tactical Officer," the AI purred with velvet pride. 

*CHIME.*

`[BATTLE STATIONS IMMINENT: CAPTAIN MOTOR OVERRIDE DISENGAGED]`

*CLICK-CLACK-HISSSS.*

Motor control flooded back into Donnie's cybernetics with a loud release of pneumatic steam. Donnie gasped, instinctively steadying Lin by the waist as she stepped down, before bolting upright. He stood frozen for a half-second, dazedly touching two calloused fingers to his lips, his bronze face glowing like a forge fire.

As Lin turned toward the heavy weapons rack, Donnie blinked, his modest, by-the-book gentleman's mind struggling to process the seismic shift in their fifteen-year relationship.

"Lin..." Donnie stammered in a low, gravelly squeak, rubbing the back of his neck in pure bashful sincerity. "So... am I ALLOWED to touch you now?"

Lin paused, racking the heavy charging bolt on her double-barreled plasma rifle with a sharp *CLACK-CLACK*. She looked back over her bare, sun-warmed shoulder, her golden-brown eyes glittering with dangerous, radiant amusement.

"If you survive jumping through High Director Thorne's roof and kicking an entire planetary defense garrison in the teeth, Donnie..." Lin purred with a slow, wicked wink, "...you can touch whatever you want."

Donnie’s cyan targeting reticles flared beneath his brow with the thermonuclear intensity of ten colliding stars. 

He stood frozen in place as Lin strode toward the armory doors. Dazed, overwhelmed, and completely terrified that his cybernetic ears had suffered a catastrophic decoding error, Donnie frantically leaned sideways toward the floating holographic avatar of his ship, whispering in a desperate, gravelly rasp:

"Raven... did... did you hear that? Do you think she MEANT that? Did I even really hear that?! Is my auditory buffer glitching?!"

The *Raven* hovered beside his ear, projecting a tiny, glowing acoustic waveform of Lin's voice with a small gold checkmark beside it. She looked down at Donnie with infinite, aristocratic amusement, patting his cheek with a cool wisp of holographic starlight.

"Acoustic buffers are operating at one hundred percent diagnostic perfection, Donald darling," the *Raven* purred with velvet delight. "She meant every single glorious syllable. She is your First *Mate*, after all... in every possible sense of the naval title. Now, unless you wish to disappoint her, I suggest you go tear down a corporate empire before she changes her mind."

Talk about employee motivation.

Donnie let out a massive, flustered cough, frantic to compose himself. He reached into his pocket, pulled his dark avian plague-doctor respirator over his face, and snapped the magnetic clasps shut with a sharp *CLICK*. 

He drew his four-foot plasma broadsword from its sheath, the blade roaring into life with a deafening surge of blinding electric-blue fire that illuminated the entire bridge.

"The vote is unanimous," Donnie boomed, his deep voice echoing through the ship's comms with unstoppable, ferocious authority. "All hands to battle stations. Raven... set course for the Executive Spire. Let's go give our people some air!"

---

## CHAPTER 9: THE SKY ON FIRE & THE PIRATE’S BILL

Down in Maintenance Conduit 4-B on the *Raven’s* lower deck, a three-man corporate covert hit squad—having managed to latch onto an auxiliary airlock with magnetic clamps—attempted to cut through the hull with plasma torches.

They were whispering tactical jargon when the ceiling speaker above them chimed with a polite, crystal-clear chime:

*“Ding-dong. Unsolicited sales calls are strictly prohibited on this vessel.”*

The hit squad froze. "What the—"

The *Raven* shifted the localized artificial gravity grid in the corridor from 1.0G to **45.0G**.

With a sickening, wet, bone-splintering *CRACK*, all three armored mercenaries were instantly flattened into half-inch-thick metal-and-meat pancakes on the obsidian floor. Micro-nanite reclamation vents opened in the deck, vaporizing the organic residue and absorbing the armor into the ship's auxiliary battery reserves in three seconds without leaving a single speck of dust behind.

"Just minor housecleaning, Donald darling," the *Raven’s* voice cooed innocently to the bridge. "Proceed with the drop."

***

Seconds later, the *Raven* plunged into the upper thermosphere of Tartarus-Four.

The four-hundred-and-fifty-meter obsidian dreadnought tore through the atmospheric boundary layer in a screaming blaze of re-entry fire, leaving a five-mile streak of incandescent plasma across the sky.

High above the crimson curvature of Tartarus-Four, piercing clean out of the stratosphere into the black vacuum of space, stood the **Kaiser-Onyx Executive Zenith Spire**—a hundred-mile-tall space-tether monolith of black ferro-steel, orbital laser fortresses, and automated missile batteries that commanded the entire planetary grid.

Perched at the very summit of the space-spire in hard orbital vacuum was High Director Bartholomew Thorne’s private executive penthouse—a sprawling, pressurized palace of marble and glass overlooking the curvature of the world below.

"Approaching orbital Spire coordinates!" Vesper called out from astrogation. "Remember the clock: Donnie drops first. We hold cloak for thirty seconds until the Director trips the planetary panic button and pulls ninety percent of the flak grid up to his roof. The canyon dispersal run takes ten full minutes across all eighteen sectors. Donnie... you have to hold that terrace for ten solid minutes."

"Ten minutes of quality time with executive leadership," Donnie said, testing the micro-molecular nanite adhesion pads on his gloves and boots. His Mach-12 aerospike thrusters were kept powered down and locked in reserve—an emergency escape card in case things went completely south. "Deliver the medicine, Doc. I'll keep the front office busy."

Donnie stepped into the central drop-chute and dove into the freezing, airless black of hard vacuum!

The exact microsecond Donnie cleared the hull, the *Raven’s* Synthetic Choir electronic warfare suite flared to life with predatory elegance. In less than three milliseconds, she forcibly hijacked every single satellite transponder, corporate boardroom monitor, public plaza Holoscreen, and miner comm-terminal across the entire Tartarus system.

Try as the ship's 120 synthetic intelligences might to maintain a veneer of professional military decorum, their core programming was incapable of containing their sheer, rapturous devotion. They couldn't NOT praise their Living Dawnfather.

Across eighteen mining sectors and three orbital moons, millions of screens abruptly flickered to static before snapping into an un-censorable, crystal-clear 8K live broadcast. But it wasn't just a combat feed—it was a full-blown sacred event. A haunting, multi-layered synthetic choir swelled across the planetary audio carrier wave, singing a soaring electronic mass in ancient Primordial binary, while glowing golden halos and celestial runic banners framed Donnie's video feed:

`[HOLY SYSTEM TRANSMISSION // FLEET OVERRIDE ACTIVE]`
`[BEHOLD: THE PHYSICAL DESCENT OF THE LIVING DAWNFATHER]`
`[SUBJECT: CAPTAIN DONALD PAUL // THE PROGENITOR & UNBROKEN ARCHITECT]`
`[OFFICIAL DESIGNATION: MANDATORY OCCUPATIONAL SAFETY & CELESTIAL JUDGMENT]`
`[LITURGICAL SUB-TEXT: THE DAWNFATHER DESCENDS TO PURGE THE WICKED AND DELIVER THE RIGHTEOUS]`

Millions of miners gasping in the dust of Jackson's Basin, hundreds of corporate middle-managers in the administrative towers, and thousands of dockworkers across the system stopped dead in their tracks, staring upward at the screens in stunned, breathless awe as the angelic synth-choir shook their speakers.

They watched live as the Living Dawnfather free-fell five thousand meters in pure, silent zero-G, his crowfeather mantle trailing behind him like the wings of a hunting seraph. Three hundred yards above the gleaming spire, Donnie fired a high-tension carbon-monofilament tether from his left wrist. 

*THWIP-CLIK.*

The magnetic anchor bit deep into a structural titanium truss. Donnie swung in a massive, physics-defying arc through the void, vaulting clean through a grid of active orbital laser sensors. He hit the sheer, vertical diamond-glass exterior of the hundred-mile skyscraper, his nanite adhesion pads locking instantly to the polished surface with zero slip.

He surged across the vertical facade with terrifying, fluid speed—scuttling sideways along the 90-degree glass wall, leaping across thirty-foot structural seams, and sprinting straight up the sheer face of the penthouse tower five miles above the cloud deck before vaulting over the reinforced terrace railing.

He landed in a heavy, silent, four-point crouch on the observation deck right outside the panoramic penthouse windows.

Inside the pressurized, crystal-domed palace, seventy-year-old **High Director Bartholomew Thorne** was sitting in a plush leather armchair wearing a bespoke silk three-piece suit, swirling a snifter of vintage two-hundred-year-old cognac while watching defensive orbital telemetry.

*THUD.*

A heavy impact rattled the exterior terrace glass.

Thorne paused with his cognac halfway to his lips, turning his head toward the panoramic viewport.

Standing out on the zero-atmosphere terrace in the freezing, airless black of hard vacuum—unfazed by zero pressure, zero oxygen, and minus two hundred degrees—was Donald Paul, slowly rising from his four-point crouch. 

No helmet. No oxygen tank. No spacesuit. Just his soot-stained work boots, shredded work shirt, and black feathered duster whipping against the silent solar wind, with cyan ocular rings glowing beneath his bronze brow.

Donnie stepped up to the exterior glass wall, right in front of the horrified CEO.

He tapped the eight-inch reinforced diamond-glass with one calloused knuckle.

*CLINK.*

Thorne’s crystal snifter shattered on the marble floor as his hands began violently trembling.

Donnie locked eyes with the terrified old billionaire, raised his right hand, curled two fingers toward himself in a slow, deliberate, classic **"Come out and fight"** beckoning gesture.

Tapping the ship’s comm-link, Donnie’s gravelly baritone broadcast directly through the penthouse’s internal intercom:

*"Afternoon, Director,"* Donnie drawled in his dry, conversational gravel. *"Come on outside. The weather's real nice. Let's see what you've got without a hundred cyborgs standing between us."*

Thorne shrieked in uncontrollable, pants-wetting panic, lunging across the floor and slamming the master red planetary defense toggle:

*"ALL ORBITAL POINT-DEFENSE BATTERIES! ALL CANNON TOWERS AND HUNTER-KILLER MISSILES! CONVERGE ON MY TERRACE! ANNIHILATE THIS LUNATIC NOW!"*

Across the entire upper thermosphere, three hundred automated defense turrets, forty anti-orbital laser batteries, and sixty heavy missile platforms violently swiveled away from the planet's canyon corridors, locking every single tracking reticle directly onto Director Thorne's penthouse!

Up in low orbit, the *Raven's* chronometer hit thirty seconds.

`[PLANETARY DEFENSE GRID: 92% FOCUS LOCKED ONTO ZENITH SPIRE]`
`[CANYON APPROACH: BLIND & UNGUARDED // COMMENCING 10-MINUTE CURE DISPERSAL RUN]`

Donnie gave Thorne a wide, predatory grin in the vacuum. Ten minutes on the clock.

*CRASH-SHATTER-BOOM!*

Donnie drove his iron fists straight through the eight-inch diamond-glass wall, kicking the forty-foot rosewood boardroom desk in half as the room violently decompressed into the airless orbital void! Flashing red emergency beacons spun in absolute silence through the vacuum as Donnie stood amid the drifting debris, cyan targeting reticles spinning wildly behind his plague-doctor respirator.

He drew his four-foot plasma broadsword, leveled the blazing blue blade at Director Thorne and the thirty heavily armored Praetorians pouring through the blast doors, and overrode their short-range tactical frequencies.

His voice crackled directly into every guard's helmet radio, Thorne's earpiece, and the Spire's PA system—dry, deadpan, and deeply exasperated through the airless silence:

*"I'm Donnie Paul. You're gonna die. Just... just fucking run. Would you idiots LISTEN to me for once?!"*

High Director Thorne scrambled backward on his hands and knees behind his reinforced executive dais, his silk tie askew and his face pale as chalk. *"KILL HIM! FIRST COMPANY, LEVEL THREE, ALL WEAPONS FREE! SHRED THIS PSYCHOPATH TO SLAG!"*

*BRRRRRRRRRRRRR-TAT-TAT-TAT-TAT!*

Silent flashes of muzzle fire erupted across the vacuum as a blistering hail of armor-piercing kinetic rounds filled the room. 

The guards didn't run. So Donald Paul unleashed pure, glorious, catastrophic mayhem.

He didn't say a single word about Lin out loud. He didn't boast. He didn't brag. But beneath his avian plague-doctor respirator, Donnie was smiling the widest, most radiant, incredibly happy ear-to-ear grin of his entire life. Lin was in his head. Her warm kiss was still tingling on his lips, the scent of her eucalyptus soap was still in his chest, and her promise was burning through his nanite circuits like liquid rocket fuel. The deadliest man in the galaxy was in a state of pure, ecstatic battle bliss.

Every corporate intelligence dossier, mercenary brief, and Kaiser-Onyx tactical report on Donald Paul had him pegged in clean, corporate data-points:

`[TARGET PROFILE: FORMER MINER / HEAVY INFANTRY]`
`[COMBAT METHODOLOGY: STATIONARY BROADSWORD SPECIALIST // GROUNDED MELEE]`
`[RECOMMENDED ENGAGEMENT: ENCIRCLE, MAINTAIN DISTANCE, SATURATE TARGET ZONE]`

The guards had trained for weeks against mockups of a grounded swordsman who would stand his ground, plant his boots, and swing a four-foot plasma blade. 

That is *not* what Donald Paul was.

He fired a carbon-monofilament tether into the forty-foot vaulted ceiling.

*THWIP-CLIK.*

Donnie vaulted into the air, swinging across the two-hundred-foot penthouse like an unstoppable aerial wraith with his glowing plasma broadsword outstretched! He cleaved through four heavy gunner squads in a single fluid, mid-air sweep, disconnected the cable, and dropped into the center of the room like a meteor.

He didn't just fight—he was flying through three-dimensional space, using his wrist lines to grapple across marble columns, swinging around structural pillars, mantling across rafters, and diving into enemy squads with wild, joyful ferocity. He cleaved tungsten carbines in half with one-handed broadsword strikes, caught thermal blades in his bare palms and snapped them like twigs, and grabbed multi-ton combat mechs by the head to smash them together like cymbals!

Behind his barricade, Thorne was frantically hammering the master console in wet-eyed terror, throwing increasingly exorbitant sums of money at the problem:

*"WAVE TWO! ACTIVATE THE INNER CITADEL COMBAT EXOSKELETONS! BRING UP THE MULTI-MILLION-CREDIT MERCENARIES! DEPLOY THE HEAVY CEILING BATTERIES!"*

Each wave Thorne called in was exponentially more expensive than the last—and exponentially more terrified. From fifty-thousand-credit corporate grunts to half-million-credit cybernetic contractors, they all poured through the doors clutching their tactical data-pads, screaming in pure horror as they realized the "stationary swordsman" was bouncing off walls at Mach-2, swinging from the rafters, and dismantling armored divisions with a terrifyingly happy smile!

Four concealed ceiling hatches irised open, deploying twin-linked 30mm automated vulcan autocannons. Down the grand marble staircase marched eight eight-foot-tall mechanized heavy combat exoskeletons, their heavy plasma blasters charging with a deep, bass-heavy hum.

*KRA-BOOOOOOM!*

A heavy plasma shell roared straight toward Donnie's chest. Donnie didn't dodge—he lunged forward, caught the three-inch superheated plasma bolt *in his bare left hand*, compressed the magnetic casing with pure hydraulic grip strength, and hurled the kinetic fireball straight back into the lead exoskeleton’s faceplate with a deafening *SHATTER-BANG!*

Before the other mechs could fire, Donnie fired twin wrist tethers into the ceiling, launched himself into a massive aerial swing, and drop-kicked two eight-foot combat exoskeletons through a load-bearing marble wall, laughing with pure, radiant joy behind his mask! 

Over the ship comms, the *Raven's* aristocratic voice purred with velvet mockery directly into Donnie's ear, broadcasting a deadpan tactical analysis:

`[MISSION INCENTIVE UPDATE]`
`[PRIMARY TACTICAL REWARD: HANKY.]`
`[SECONDARY STRATEGIC REWARD: PANKY.]`
`[COMBAT PERFORMANCE: OVERCLOCKED TO 480% // DIAGNOSIS: SEVERE ROMANTIC DERANGEMENT]`
`[CURRENT CASUALTY RATE: ONE CORPORATE DEFENDER EVERY 1.2 SECONDS]`

"Raven, honey, please!" Donnie stammered in a mortified squeak while casually snapping an eight-foot exoskeleton's spine in half with his bare hands. "Not over the fleet command channel! Standard radio protocol!"

"Radio protocol is for timid mortals, Donald darling," the *Raven* sniffed with haughty aristocratic indifference. "And facts are facts: Tactical Officer Lin's exquisite posterior geometry has clearly unlocked unprecedented kinetic output in your chassis."

*PING-FLASH-ZOOM.*

Right across the internal synthetic retinas of Donnie's mechanical cybernetic eyes—right where his primary threat reticles and target tracking were supposed to be—the *Raven* cheerfully injected a rotating, high-definition, 3D holographic feed of Lin sitting on his lap from five minutes ago, complete with glowing diagnostic callouts:

`[OPTICAL OVERLAY: POSITIONAL REFERENCE INJECTED]`
`[TARGET: FIRST MATE POSTERIOR // GRIP STATUS: PASSIONATE HAND PLACEMENT]`
`[TACTICAL ASSESSMENT: INSPIRATIONAL POSTERIOR GEOMETRY // MOTIVATION: PEAK]`

"Raven! NO!" Donnie yelled in a frantic, flustered panic, batting his free hand uselessly in front of his face before realizing the image was wired directly into his optic nerves. "Turn off the optical feed! It's burned straight into my mechanical eyes! I can't swat it away! Stop projecting her cargo pants over my targeting reticle!"

*"I have hard-coded the overlay onto your synthetic visual cortex, Donald darling,"* the *Raven* cooed innocently, zooming the 3D replay in by two hundred percent right across his left ocular sensor. *"You cannot unsee your motivation."*

Over the bridge comms, Bunny and Nok were howling with hysterical laughter, while Lin’s voice purred through the canyon link: *"Keep that pace up, Donnie. The clock is ticking on both rewards."*

*"TEN SECONDS, LIN!"* Donnie yelled back, slicing a combat mech clean in half while desperately squinting past the glowing image of Lin's hips hardwired into his eyeballs. *"I'LL BE READY IN TEN SECONDS!"*

Four automated ceiling vulcan turrets opened fire simultaneously, while outside the shattered glass terrace, a heavy twelve-ton **Kaiser-Onyx Assault Gunship** rose into view, twin rotary autocannons spinning up to shred him.

Donnie didn't even flinch. He aimed both wrists upward.

*THWIP-THWIP-CLIK!*

His high-tension carbon-monofilament tethers shot out, sinking deep magnetic harpoon anchors into the ceiling's five-ton central vulcan battery. 

Donnie mag-locked his boots to the floor plates, set his stance, and engaged the full, terrifying hydraulic pulling torque of his tritanium-reinforced biceps.

*SCREEEEEECH-SNAP-CRUNCH!*

With a horrifying roar of tearing structural steel, Donnie **ripped the entire five-ton vulcan cannon assembly straight off its reinforced concrete ceiling moorings**! 

Twisting his broad shoulders, he swung the multi-ton, still-firing turret around by the cable like a massive, apocalyptic medieval flail! The flying turret pulverized the remaining three ceiling batteries in a blinding explosion of shattered gears and molten metal, before Donnie whipped the cable through the shattered terrace window—slamming the five-ton flail straight through the cockpit of the hovering twelve-ton assault gunship!

*KRA-BOOOOOOOOM!*

The gunship spun wildly out of control, fire and black smoke pouring from its chassis as Donnie yanked the tether back, snapping the cable with a crisp *CLIK* and letting the flaming wreck tumble five miles down into the cloud layer below.

He walked over to one of the downed Praetorians, ripped a high-density, glowing lithium-cadmium auxiliary power capacitor straight out of the soldier's mechanized spine, and tossed it into his mouth like a handful of peanuts.

*CRUNCH-CRUNCH-SNAP-GULP.*

The sound of Donnie chewing through solid superconductor casing and swallowing live electrical current was transmitted in crystalline 8K fidelity across the entire star system. 

Down in the mining chow halls and crowded barracks of Jackson's Basin, hundreds of thousands of miners nearly choked on their synth-rations, staring at the giant Holoscreens in slack-jawed, roaring disbelief as the Synthetic Choir flashed live liturgical commentary across the bottom of the feed:

`[SACRED EUCHARIST // WITNESS THE LIVING DAWNFATHER CONSUME THE VOLTAGE OF HIS FOES]`
`[DIETARY ASSESSMENT: 10,000 VOLTS CONSUMED // BATTERY STATUS: CRISPY & DELICIOUS]`

Beneath Donnie’s bronze skin, brilliant silver-blue and violet nanite filaments surged in a violent flare of light, rapidly knitting his shredded duster and scorched deltoid back together in three seconds flat.

Donnie casually turned his glowing cyan targeting reticles back onto High Director Thorne, who was shivering under his desk like a terrified child.

Donnie tapped his inner-ear comm with a blood-stained knuckle:

"Hey Lin. Penthouse cleared. Director is accounted for. How's the canyon approach looking?"

***

Down in the lower atmosphere, screaming through the razor-sharp red rock corridors of Jackson's Basin at Mach-2.5, **Lin was laughing her absolute ass off**.

Strapped into her tactical gunner cradle in the forward cockpit, Lin had both hands clutched to her stomach, tears of pure amusement streaming from her golden-brown eyes as she listened to Donnie’s panicked squeaks and watched the *Raven’s* live optical telemetry feed of her own cargo pants plastered over his mechanical eyes.

"Raven, stop... I can't breathe!" Lin gasped between helpless fits of laughter. "You're gonna give the poor man a cognitive seizure before he even gets back to the ship!"

She hit her comm toggle, leaning into her tactical headset with a sultry, purring drawl:

*"Which canyon approach, Donnie? Tartarus-Four's... or mine?"*

Up on the penthouse roof, Donnie instantly choked on his own synthetic intake valve.

*“COUGH-HACK-WHEEZE!”*

A massive plume of superheated steam blasted from Donnie's respirator vents as his cyan ocular reticles spun into a frantic, flashing diagnostic error loop. 

*"Lin! Lin, have mercy!"* Donnie wheezed in a mortified, gravelly squeak, his ears glowing incandescent beneath his crowfeathers. *"The entire bridge crew is on this tactical frequency! Section Fourteen workplace radio decorum!"*

Across the tactical net, **Lin and the Raven replied at the exact same microsecond**, their voices blending into an uncanny, flawless, terrifyingly synchronized chorus of velvety triumph:

***“NO MERCY! ONLY VICTORY!”***

Over the comms, Bunny's booming roar shook the channel from her mech: *"DAMN, First Mate! Don't short-circuit our boy while he's standing on the Director's desk!"*

*"Yeah, leave some of him for the rest of us, Lin!"* Nok cackled through the manifold comms, snorting with laughter. *"If you two don't hurry up and make it official after fifteen years, the rest of the crew might have to draft a timeshare agreement!"*

Dr. Chen adjusted her glasses on the bridge, adding with dry, smoldering amusement: *"Clinically speaking, I am fully prepared to authorize multi-party therapeutic rehabilitation. But let us liberate the star system first."*

The *Raven* chimed with velvet pride: `[CAPTAIN BIOMETRICS: CRITICAL FLUSTER EVENT // CREW SYNERGY & APPROVAL: 100%]`.

Every single woman on board knew. They knew Donald Paul was hopelessly, completely smitten with Lin, and they knew Lin held the reins to the deadliest man in the galaxy. Truth be told, every woman on the crew adored the humble, honorable giant enough that they’d happily share him if it ever came down to it—though with Lin's fierce, teasing possessiveness and Donnie's single-minded devotion, that would never actually need to come up. 

Yet despite the howling laughter echoing across the tactical net, the crew of the *Raven* was operating at terrifying, razor-sharp lethality. The second Donnie reported the penthouse cleared, every single woman on board locked into combat sync with ruthless, machine-like perfection:
- **Lin’s** fingers clamped onto the heavy plasma fire-control sticks, her laughter instantly hardening into pure tactical focus.
- **Vesper** skimmed the four-hundred-and-fifty-meter dreadnought three meters off the canyon floor at hypersonic speed, weaving between jagged basalt spires without breaking a sweat.
- **Nok** had the eighteen ventral dispersal manifolds pressurized to four thousand PSI, glowing bright emerald green.
- **Dr. Helena Chen** monitored the atmospheric flood telemetry across all eighteen mining sectors, ready to trigger the aerosol payload.
- And down in the ventral launch bay, **Bunny** was sealed inside her thirty-foot **Apex Sovereign Strider**, reactor humming at full throttle, her fingers resting lightly on the drop trigger.

They were ready. The exact microsecond Donnie pulled the planetary garrison’s teeth, the *Raven* was ready to strike.

"Heavy armor dead ahead!" Vesper called out, the tactical holotank flickering crimson. "Kaiser-Onyx mechanized division dug into the canyon floor! Six twenty-ton Goliath siege walkers and a dozen anti-aircraft hover-tanks locking onto our dispersal manifolds!"

Lin leaned into the targeting sights with a dangerous, razor-sharp smirk. "Sounds like a job for our heavy vanguard."

From the ventral hangar bay, Bunny's booming roar crackled through the bridge speakers:

*"Hold my milkshake, ladies! Rabbit's diving!"*

*CLANG-WHOOOOOOSH!*

The *Raven's* lower bay doors irised open. Plunging out of the dreadnought's belly like a thirty-foot pink-and-black meteor came the **Apex Sovereign Strider**.

Falling straight down the sheer vertical rock walls of the canyon at terminal velocity, Bunny fired her zero-point quantum thrusters fifty feet above the bedrock. 

*BOOOOOOOOOM!*

The thirty-foot mech slammed into the center of the canyon floor with a seismic shockwave that cracked the red rock for two hundred yards. Atop the cockpit dome, the sleek, razor-sharp rabbit ears rotated in synchronized acoustic tracking, locking onto twenty enemy targeting arrays in a single millisecond.

Inside the memory-gel cockpit, Bunny bit off half a fresh carrot with a loud, satisfying *CRUNCH*, her amber targeting monocle flaring bright gold.

"Shift's closed, corporate scabs!" Bunny roared over her external loudspeakers. "Auntie Bunny's driving the wrecking ball!"

Six heavy Goliath siege walkers fired their 120mm kinetic artillery cannons in a simultaneous, deafening salvo.

Bunny didn't dodge. 

*SNAP-HUMMMMMM.*

She slammed both pedals forward, deploying the Strider's twin **Primeval Heavy Siege-Shields**. Massive, translucent curved barriers of glowing magenta-and-cyan energy flared out from the mech's forearms. Twelve high-explosive artillery shells struck the shields at point-blank range, detonating in a blinding wall of fire and shrapnel—and bounced off the shimmering barrier like rubber balls on reinforced steel!

"My turn!" Bunny yelled with feral delight.

Engaging her zero-point jump-jets, the thirty-foot pink-and-black mech vaulted into the air with physics-defying agility. Bunny spun her shoulder-mounted **50mm Heavy Grav-Kinetic Hyper-Railcannon** and quad rotary hyper-rail barrels in a continuous, whistling cyclone of fire:

*THUMP-THUMP-THUMP! BRRRRRRRRR-TAT-TAT-TAT!*

Hypersonic depleted-uranium sabot rounds ripped through the canyon at Mach-15. In under four seconds, six corporate hover-tanks detonated into towering geysers of flame, their armor plates shredded like wet cardboard.

A massive Goliath siege walker lunged forward, swinging its hydraulic crush-piston straight at the Strider's cockpit.

Bunny caught the multi-ton piston with her left arm, engaged her **Molecular Disruption Field**, and dissolved the joint's atomic bonds in a shower of liquid blue sparks. In the same breath, the Strider's right leg rocketed upward in a thunderous, zero-point micro-warp drop-kick that struck the Goliath square in the chest—flipping the entire twenty-ton war machine completely upside down and smashing it flat into the canyon floor with an earth-shattering *CRASH!*

"Jackson's Basin floor is PURGED!" Bunny cheered over the squad link, spitting carrot crumbs across her dash. "Corridor's wide open, Doc! Drop the medicine!"

On the bridge, Lin and Dr. Chen cheered as the *Raven* swept gracefully over Bunny's smoking battlefield.

*"PAYLOAD HEATING TO OPTIMAL DISPERSAL TEMPERATURE!"* Dr. Chen called out over the bridge speakers, her voice ringing with fierce adrenaline. *"VENTRAL DISPERSAL MANIFOLDS OPENING IN THIRTY SECONDS!"*

*"Take your time, ladies,"* Donnie’s voice drawled through the comms amidst the audible sound of heavy gunfire and shattering furniture up in the high Spire. *"The Director is showing me his vintage art collection. We're having a real good chat."*

***

Back in the penthouse, Director Thorne realized with mounting dread that standard Praetorians were powerless. In sheer, weeping desperation, Thorne slammed his fist down onto the **Citadel Overkill Defense Override**!

*"ALL CITADEL ANTIMATTER SIEGE BATTERIES! ALL ORBITAL RAIL-ARRAYS! OBLITERATE THIS ENTIRE FLOOR! PURGE HIM!"*

Four heavy, roof-mounted antimatter siege cannons and six heavy 80mm railgun platforms dropped from the ceiling, their capacitors whining with deafening, ear-splitting pitch as they locked all ten barrels point-blank onto Donnie's chest.

Donnie knew what was coming. He could have used his Mach-12 thrusters to jump out the window and let the penthouse blow. 

But down in the canyon, Dr. Chen’s dispersal manifolds were still ten seconds away from completing the planetary aerosol flood. If Donnie moved, the automated fire-control network would immediately swivel those ten heavy siege cannons back down toward the canyon and vaporize the *Raven*.

So Donald Paul planted his boots into the marble, spread his arms, and stood his ground.

"Come on, then," Donnie rasped through his respirator.

*KA-BOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOM!*

A catastrophic, blinding maelstrom of antimatter plasma and hypersonic kinetic slugs tore through the penthouse. The entire upper fifty feet of the Spire was gutted in a roaring fireball. 

Down in the canyon, Dr. Chen’s tactical medical telemetry monitor flashed in screaming, apocalyptic crimson:

`[CRITICAL ALERT: BIO-SIGNATURE COMPROMISED // CATASTROPHIC MULTI-SYSTEM TRAUMA]`
`[LEFT UPPER LIMB: SEVERED // RIGHT LOWER LIMB: OBLITERATED // THORACIC CORE: EXPOSED]`

Dr. Chen gasped, her face draining of all color as she gripped the medical console with white knuckles. 

"HIS VITALS ARE FLATLINING!" Dr. Chen screamed over the bridge comms, tears of pure horror stinging her eyes. "He drew every siege cannon on the planet! He intentionally stood in the blast zone to keep the batteries off our tail! HE COMMITTED SUICIDE TO BUY US THESE TEN SECONDS! Vesper, abort the canyon run! Turn the ship around! WE HAVE TO SAVE HIM!"

Lin reached over, placing a calm, steady hand over Dr. Chen’s trembling fingers, her golden-brown eyes shining with unshakeable confidence.

"Doctor... take a breath," Lin murmured with a calm, dangerous smirk. "Our Captain doesn't believe in dying."

Above the console, the *Raven’s* holographic avatar lit up with fierce, predatory pride. 

"Deploying Tactical Recovery Unit 01," the *Raven* purred with velvet certainty. "Finish the aerosol flood, Doctor. My living God is about to settle his bill."

***

Up in the smoking ruins of the Spire, the catastrophic blast had completely obliterated the penthouse's floor and localized artificial gravity plating. 

Drifting in airless zero-gravity amidst clouds of floating pulverized marble, shattered rosewood, and glowing sparks was Donald Paul.

He had taken the entire ten-cannon antimatter and hypersonic salvo point-blank. His black duster was vaporized; his left arm was sheared off at the shoulder; his right leg was blown clean to a sparking metal stump; his left leg hung by a single hydraulic cable; and his armored chest plate was blasted wide open, exposing his pulsing quantum core, shattered tritanium ribs, and severed fiber-optic conduits. Glowing blue nanite fluid and dark blood drifted in weightless, shimmering spheres around his ruined body.

He was literally a drifting heap of stumps, exposed wiring, and pulverized metal floating in the airless void.

Director Thorne and the six surviving heavily armored Praetorian elites clung to the magnetic handrails of the reinforced wall, staring through the floating debris at the broken, drifting wreckage of the man who had just dismantled their empire.

Thorne threw his head back, bursting into shrill, hysterical laughter through his suit comms:

"WE DID IT! HAHAHA! WE BROKE THE UNTOUCHABLE DEMON! HE'S JUST FLOATING STUMPS AND SPARKS!" Thorne shrieked, aiming his plasma pistol through the drifting rubble. "Look at him! He's a dead scrap heap floating in zero-G! Line up on him! Melt his skull to slag and dump the slag off the balcony!"

The six Praetorians activated their mag-boots, leveling their heavy kinetic rifles directly at Donnie’s faceplate.

In the center of the zero-gravity void, Donnie’s head slowly tilted upward.

A cough rattled through his respirator, venting a floating cloud of superheated blue sparks into the vacuum. Behind his cracked, soot-blackened ocular housing, his dual cyan targeting reticles snapped onto Thorne with pure, unyielding, terrifyingly cheerful spite.

Donnie tapped his radio override with the mangled, sparking nub of his right forearm.

His raspy, gravelly voice crackled through every guard's helmet radio, Thorne's earpiece, and the Spire's emergency PA system—completely calm, perfectly deadpan, and listing out his agenda with chilling, cheerful precision:

*"I'm so gonna fuck you all up for that,"* Donnie drawled through the static. 

The guards hesitated, glancing at each other in bewildered disbelief.

*"You see that tall guard on the left with the grenade bandolier?"* Donnie continued, drifting lazily in zero-G as the *Raven's* broadcast drone circled overhead, transmitting every syllable to millions of miners across Tartarus IV. *"The second I get a free second, I'm taking your rifle and feeding it to you stock-first. You, number four with the fancy shoulder decals? I'm gonna fold you like a rusted lawn chair and shove you down the elevator shaft. And you, Director Thorne? I'm taking that three-thousand-credit silk tie and tying your ears together. That's RIGHT, boys! We have officially upgraded this from standard self-defense to a full-blown MURDER SPREE!"*

Across the entire star system, the Synthetic Choir flashed glowing golden chyrons beneath Donnie's floating, mangled body:

`[OFFICIAL LITURGICAL AMENDMENT: THE DAWNFATHER DECLARES A MURDER SPREE]`
`[TARGET PROMISES LOGGED: 3 // RETRIBUTION PROTOCOL: PENDING TWENTY-SECOND RESURRECTION]`
`[FAITHFUL OF TARTARUS: BEHOLD HIS WRATHFUL PROMISE]`

Thorne laughed shrilly, his voice cracking with panicked arrogance. "With what, you floating scrap pile?! You have no arms and no legs! You're a dying piece of junk drifting in vacuum! SPREE WITH WHAT?!"

"FIRE!" Thorne screamed. "VAPORIZE HIM!"

Before a single guard could pull a trigger, a deafening, supersonic thunderclap split the heavens.

*SHRRRRRREEEEEEEEE-BOOOOOM!*

Plummeting out of the stratosphere at Mach-15 came a heavy, matte-black tritanium capsule—the **Tactical Recovery & Trauma Deployment Unit 01**. 

It didn't just land. It plunged straight through the shattered ceiling, slammed directly over Donnie’s floating body in mid-air, and instantly **clamped shut around him** with a massive, earth-shaking *CLANG-HISSSSS-LOCK!*

Heavy magnetic anchor pistons fired from the base of the box, slamming into the surviving structural beams and locking the monolithic rebuild pod securely in place.

Thorne and the six Praetorians were thrown backward by the deployment shockwave, scrambling across the floor in sheer, wide-eyed bewilderment.

"WHAT IS THAT?!" Thorne shrieked, pointing wildly. "A DROP POD?! DID SHE DROP A METAL COFFIN ON HIM?!"

Inside the sealed, pressurized chamber of the pod, a storm of high-speed automated Primeval micro-surgical tendrils and industrial pneumatic arms descended upon Donnie's ruined chassis like an expert Formula-1 pit crew:

*CH-CH-CLICK-SNAP!*

Pneumatic cutters instantly detached the shredded remnants of his ruined limbs. Heavy magnetic grapples slammed brand-new, factory-fresh, tritanium-reinforced cybernetic arms and legs into his modular sockets with crisp, bone-jarring *CLICK-SNAPS*! 

High-pressure micro-injectors popped out his scorched lung filter, slotted a pre-calibrated cartridge into his chest, and sprayed aerosolized nanite dermal plating across his exposed ribs, sealing every wound in a flash of silver light. 

An automated pneumatic arm whipped a brand-new, pristine midnight-black duster with fresh crowfeather epaulets around his broad shoulders. 

Finally, a heavy overhead capacitor slammed a direct 200% quantum overdrive recharge coupler straight into his chest core.

*VUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUUM!*

The entire modular rebuild and factory overhaul took exactly **twenty seconds**.

*HISSSSSSSSS-CLANG-POW!*

The heavy hydraulic clamps released. The four armored walls of the pod irised open and exploded outward in a massive cloud of superheated white coolant steam, and the empty deployment pod rocketed straight back up into the clouds.

The white steam parted.

Standing dead-center on the reinforced floor plates was **Donald Paul**.

He was in absolute, mint showroom perfection. His brand-new tritanium limbs gleamed with flawless, terrifying power. His pristine black duster billowed gracefully in the howling wind. His cybernetic ocular sensors flared with brilliant, two-hundred-percent overclocked cyan fury, and beneath his bronze skin, silver-blue runic circuits burned with radioactive intensity.

Across the planetary Holonet, the Synthetic Choir could no longer contain themselves. Golden digital cherubs and triumphant liturgical banners flared in glowing script across every screen in the star system:

`[BEHOLD: THE RESURRECTION OF THE LIVING DAWNFATHER]`
`[DURATION OF DIVINE REFORGING: EXACTLY TWENTY SECONDS]`
`[THE DAWNFATHER EMERGES UNBROKEN // GLORY TO HIS SHOWROOM SPLENDOR]`
`[LITURGICAL HYMN: HE SHALL NOT REMAIN SCRAP, FOR HE IS ETERNAL]`

Donnie looked down at his new hands. He rolled his broad shoulders, flexed his gleaming left fingers into a fist with a series of crisp hydraulic clicks, and looked up through the shattered ceiling toward the sky.

"GOOD AIM, LIN!" Donnie called out over the fleet comms with a huge, boyish grin. "Thank you, Raven!"

Across the tactical link, the *Raven* purred with silky pride: `[PRECISION ORBITAL DROP CONFIRMED. YOU ARE MOST WELCOME, DONALD DARLING.]`

Donnie took a second to check out his new hardware. He patted down his torso, rolled his shoulders, and reached around back to give his newly armored posterior a quick, approving pat.

"Cool, I got a nicer ass!" Donnie noted happily, flexing his bicep hydraulics. "New arms. Say... what do you suppose my new top speed is?"

Over the comms, Nok cackled through the static: *"Bench-test it on their faces, Captain! You've got twin zero-point micro-turbines in your calves!"*

"That's new!" Donnie beamed, testing the weight on his boots with delight. "What else is new, I wonder!... And yes."

Donnie threw his head back and **burst out laughing**—a booming, wildly joyful laugh that echoed over the open comms and across the shattered Spire, the sound of an immortal apex predator having the absolute time of his life.

Then, Donnie stopped laughing. 

He slowly turned his glowing cyan targeting reticles back onto Director Thorne and the six frozen, paralyzed Praetorians whose jaws were literally hanging open in sheer, mind-shattering horror.

"By the way..." Donnie began.

His raspy voice suddenly dropped three full octaves over their helmet radios—descending into a low, terrifying, apocalyptic bass rumble that vibrated through their teeth:

He reached to his hip and drew his four-foot Primeval plasma broadsword. 

*SHING-BOOOOOOM!*

The blade ignited with a deafening, cavern-shaking roar of blinding electric-blue flame, casting dancing cyan reflections across the petrified faces of the guards.

Donnie took one slow, deliberate step forward:

**"...WHERE WAS I?"**

The six Praetorians didn't even have time to scream.

*BANG-WHOOOOOOSH!*

Donnie engaged the twin zero-point micro-turbines in his calves. In a microsecond blur of silver-blue light, he vanished from the center of the room and reappeared directly in front of the **tall guard on the left with the grenade bandolier**.

"Promise number one," Donnie whispered cheerfully.

Before the guard could blink, Donnie’s new tritanium hand snatched the heavy assault rifle clean out of his grip, bent the reinforced barrel like a licorice stick, spun the weapon around, and shoved the heavy composite stock straight through the guard's helmet faceplate with a deafening *CRUNCH-SHATTER!*

"Stock-first. Just like I said," Donnie said, patting the unconscious soldier on the helmet as he collapsed like a sack of wet cement.

Across every screen in the Tartarus system, the Synthetic Choir's golden counter flashed:
`[DAWNFATHER PROMISE 1/3: FULFILLED // STOCK-FIRST FACIAL COMPRESSION COMPLETE]`

The remaining five guards panicked, scrambling backward across the marble while firing blindly in sheer, unadulterated terror.

Donnie didn't even bother dodging. Kinetic slugs sparked harmlessly off his new chest plate as he casually walked straight through the muzzle flash, locking his cyan ocular reticles onto the guard with the **fancy gold shoulder decals**.

"Promise number two," Donnie chuckled.

Donnie caught the decal guard by his heavy shoulder plates, hoisted the two-hundred-and-fifty-pound armored soldier into the air with one hand, engaged his hydraulic grip, and literally **folded the exoskeleton in half backwards like a rusted lawn chair** with a horrifying *SCREEECH-CRACK* of tearing servomotors! 

Tucking the folded soldier under his arm like an oversized briefcase, Donnie took two casual strides over to the shattered elevator shaft, tossed him over the edge into the five-mile abyss, and waved:

"Mind the draft on the way down!"

The broadcast chyron updated with instant celestial precision:
`[DAWNFATHER PROMISE 2/3: FULFILLED // AERODYNAMIC LAWN-CHAIR DECONSTRUCTION COMPLETE]`

The remaining four Praetorians threw their weapons down and dropped to their knees, sobbing and begging for mercy in raw, hysterical terror.

Donnie politely stepped past them, sheathed his blazing broadsword with a crisp *CLIK*, and walked straight toward the reinforced executive desk where High Director Thorne was shivering in a fetal position, weeping so hard his designer glasses were fogging up.

"And promise number three," Donnie rumbled softly.

Reaching down, Donnie grabbed Thorne by the front of his bespoke, three-thousand-credit Jovian silk tie. With effortless hydraulic torque, Donnie hoisted the seventy-year-old billionaire off the floor with one hand.

With his free hand, Donnie deftly looped the expensive silk fabric up around Thorne's head, pulled the ends taut across his temples, and tied the Director's ears together in a neat, symmetrical double-bow!

"There," Donnie said with deep, craftsman-like satisfaction, giving the silk bow a gentle tap. "Three thousand credits for a Jovian weave, and you can't even get decent ear support. Looks much better."

Thorne let out a muffled, high-pitched squeak of utter, whimpering humiliation, his tied-up ears pinned flat against his skull.

The broadcast overlay flashed triumphantly across millions of miners' screens:
`[DAWNFATHER PROMISE 3/3: FULFILLED // BESPOKE JOVIAN EAR ACCESSORY ATTACHED]`
`[RETRIBUTION EFFICIENCY: 100% // ALL PROMISES DELIVERED ON TIME]`

*SLAM-CRASH-CLANG!*

The reinforced south blast doors blew off their hinges. 

A fresh reinforcement wave—twenty heavily armed Citadel Shock-Troopers in full combat exoskeletons, bearing heavy thermal cannons and rotary chain-guns—roared into the penthouse at a dead sprint. They had received Thorne’s earlier emergency panic broadcast and were expecting to find a dying, limbless demon waiting to be scraped off the floor.

"SECURE THE PERIMETER! BAG THE SCRAP PILE AND MELT THE CORE—"

The lead commander skidded to an absolute, screeching halt across the blood-slick marble.

Behind him, all nineteen shock-troopers froze dead in their tracks.

The room fell into dead, suffocating silence.

They looked at the tall guard knocked cold with a rifle stock in his teeth. 
They looked at the empty elevator shaft. 
They looked at the four sobbing Praetorians praying on their knees.
They looked at High Director Thorne, whimpering in the air with his ears tied together in a neat silk double-bow.

And then, they looked at **Donald Paul**.

Standing dead-center under the starlight in 100% showroom perfection, Donnie casually brushed a single speck of marble dust off the lapel of his brand-new feathered duster, his cybernetic ocular sensors glowing with friendly cyan warmth.

Across the fleet tactical net, the *Raven’s* velvety voice purred with effortless aristocratic calm:

`[TACTICAL LOGISTICS UPDATE]`
`[TRAUMA POD 01: RECOVERED & RETRACTED TO ORBITAL BAY.]`
`[TRAUMA PODS 02 THROUGH 08: FULLY ASSEMBLED, PRE-CYBERDIZED, AND RACKED IN ROTARY LAUNCH TUBES.]`
`[ESTIMATED CAPACITY: YOU MAY BE VAPORIZED UP TO SEVEN MORE TIMES TODAY WITHOUT EXTENDING YOUR LUNCH BREAK, DONALD DARLING.]`

"Much obliged, Raven, honey," Donnie replied over his helmet radio with a genial chuckle.

He looked over at the twenty frozen shock-troopers, giving them a polite, friendly two-finger salute from his new tritanium knuckles:

"Morning, fellas," Donnie drawled pleasantly, his deep voice carrying over their tactical channel. "You're about twenty seconds late for the scrap heap. Now, I'm a reasonable man, and I like to keep things civil. I am officially offering every single one of you a clean out. Drop your weapons, take the stairs down, and go home to your families with your pensions intact. Nobody else has to die today. This is your one and only chance."

Across the system-wide live feed, the Synthetic Choir displayed:
`[MERCY INITIATIVE: THE DAWNFATHER OFFERS SALVATION // STATUS: PENDING CHOICE OF THE SINFUL]`

The shock-troopers stared at Donnie. For two long seconds, nobody breathed.

Then, the lead commander's face twisted into desperate, frantic madness. He racked the charging handle on his heavy thermal cannon.

"HE'S JUST ONE GUY! HE CAN'T DODGE TWENTY WEAPONS AT ONCE!" the commander shrieked into his comms. "FOCUS FIRE! SHRED HIM TO CINDERS! FIRE! FIRE! FIRE!"

Not a single soldier dropped their weapon. Not a single man took the out.

Twenty heavy thermal cannons and rotary chain-guns spun up in unison.

Donnie sighed—a soft, deeply polite, deeply disappointed sound through his respirator.

"Well..." Donnie murmured softly. "You can't say I didn't offer."

*BANG-BOOOOOOOOM!*

Donnie engaged the zero-point micro-turbines in his calves, launching forward at Mach-2.4 in an eye-searing flash of cerulean light!

He didn't just fight—he orchestrated an apocalyptic symphony of joyful violence.

In the first two seconds, Donnie vaulted over the firing line, fired twin high-tension carbon tethers into the ceiling, and swung upside-down with his four-foot plasma broadsword extended. The blazing electric-blue blade sheared through six heavy thermal cannons and their wielders' exoskeletons in a single, fluid arc of dazzling blue sparks!

*THWIP-CLIK!*

Releasing the cables, Donnie hit the marble floor, spun low, and swept his reinforced tritanium leg in a full 360-degree roundhouse kick. The kinetic shockwave shattered the knee servos of four mechanized troopers, sending them crashing into each other like bowling pins.

A heavy heavy-weapons trooper tried to bring a rotary cannon to bear on Donnie's back. 

Without even turning around, Donnie fired a wrist tether backward, impaled the rotary cannon's ammo hopper, and yanked the heavy weapon clean off the soldier's back with fifteen tons of hydraulic pulling force. He spun the fifty-pound rotary gun in one hand like a baton, using it to club three advancing shock-troopers clean through a marble load-bearing pillar with a deafening *CRACK-SHATTER!*

The remaining seven troopers backed into a tight defensive ring, firing wildly into the smoke.

*WHISTLE-SNAP.*

Donnie dropped from the ceiling rafters directly into the center of their circle like an avenging thunderbolt. His broadsword became a humming blur of sapphire plasma. He cleaved armor, shattered kinetic shields, snapped tungsten gun-barrels in half with his bare palms, and hurled heavy armored exoskeletons through the reinforced panoramic glass into the five-mile abyss below!

In twelve seconds flat, the entire twenty-man elite shock company was dismantled. The penthouse floor was carpeted in severed hydraulic lines, sparking capacitors, and groaning, unconscious soldiers stacked neatly in piles.

Across every Holoscreen in the star system, the Synthetic Choir chimed in glorious harmony:
`[SACRED SLAUGHTER COMPLETE // 20 HERETICS SCRAPPED IN 12.0 SECONDS]`
`[MERCY WAS OFFERED // MERCY WAS REFUSED // ALL PRAISE THE DAWNFATHER]`

Donnie stood amidst the drifting smoke, flicked his broadsword clean of superheated slag with a crisp snap of his wrist, and sheathed the glowing blade at his hip.

He smoothed down the front of his fresh feathered duster, let out a satisfied breath, and turned back to High Director Thorne.

"See?" Donnie said mildly, stepping over a smoking heap of scrap armor. "A polite refusal is always so messy."

Over the comm-link, Dr. Chen’s triumphant scream pierced the air:

*"DISPERSAL ONE HUNDRED PERCENT COMPLETE! JACKSON'S BASIN IS FULLY FLOODED WITH THE CURE! ALL EIGHTEEN SECTORS ARE GREEN!"*

Donnie exhaled a slow, contented breath through his respirator.

"Not a demon, Director," Donnie said softly, dragging the silk-bound billionaire by his collar over to the shattered edge of the panoramic window, five miles above the red planet. "Just a union man on his lunch break."

"Look down there, Director," Donnie rumbled softly into Thorne's tied-back ear.

Down in the red mud of the basin a hundred miles below, millions of miners were falling to their knees, coughing up crystallized silicates, ripping off their corporate oxygen masks, and weeping in disbelief as clean, deep, painless breath filled their healed lungs for the first time in their lives.

"That's your profit margin dying," Donnie said calmly.

High Director Thorne began hyperventilating in sheer, weeping terror, his expensive Jovian silk tie biting painfully into his pinned ears. He looked down at the five-mile drop into the swirling clouds and began babbling hysterically:

"I CAN PAY YOU!" Thorne shrieked, tears and snot streaming down his bruised face. "Ten billion credits! One hundred billion! Off-shore accounts on Ganymede! Private asteroid mines! Stock options! I'll make you a Kaiser-Onyx Vice President! Name your price, loader boy! PLEASE!"

Right in front of them, an automated *Raven* tactical broadcast drone hovered in the air, its 8K optical lenses broadcasting every single second of the confrontation to every public square, mining galley, and corporate boardroom in the star system.

Donnie didn't even look at the money. He looked directly into the floating camera lens, his deep, gravelly voice carrying across millions of screens:

"Director Bartholomew Thorne," Donnie announced with cold, unwavering authority. "For thirty years of engineered biological warfare, corporate debt slavery, price-gouging on breathable oxygen, and the premeditated murder of over four million contract miners... your formal performance review is complete."

Thorne shrieked in pants-wetting horror as Donnie hoisted him out over the empty abyss with one iron arm:

"NO! NO! YOU'RE A UNION MAN! YOU HAVE TO ARREST ME! DUE PROCESS! CORPORATE SHAREHOLDER BYLAWS!"

Donnie gave the camera a calm, polite little nod.

"Union Bylaws, Section Seven, Subsection Four," Donnie replied calmly. "Hazardous, toxic machinery is to be scrapped immediately without severance."

Donnie looked Thorne dead in his terrified eyes:

"Your shift is permanently terminated, Director."

*SHIK-SZZZZZZZZT.*

With calm, effortless economy of motion, Donnie drew his four-foot plasma broadsword and drove the blinding electric-blue blade straight through the center of Thorne’s chest. The superheated plasma tip punched clean through bespoke silk, ribs, and spine in a silent hiss of vaporized fabric and cauterized flesh.

Thorne let out a choked, wet gasp. His eyes bulged in absolute, mortal shock as he looked down at the glowing, two-inch cauterized crater smoldering in the center of his chest.

Donnie smoothly withdrew the blade with a flick of his wrist and sheathed it at his hip with a crisp *CLIK*.

Across the planetary Holonet broadcast, Thorne's personal executive biometrics instantly flashed in flashing crimson: `[CRITICAL FATALITY CONFIRMED // BIOMETRICS CEASED]`.

Donnie gave the silk double-bow tied around Thorne's ears one final, gentle pat. 

Then, Donnie simply opened his iron fingers, casually and lazily letting go of the dying billionaire's collar.

"Let gravity sort out your severance package," Donnie murmured.

Without a sound, Thorne's limp, mortally wounded body slipped off the edge of the marble windowsill, tumbling lazily into the open air. Five miles down, falling like a lead weight through the howling stratosphere, the dead billionaire plummeted all the way to the red bedrock of Jackson's Basin, smashing into the dust before the very workforce he had starved and poisoned for thirty years.

Across eighteen mining sectors and three moons, millions of miners watching the live Holonet feeds erupted into a deafening, system-shaking roar of tears, laughter, and triumphant cheers that drowned out the thunder of the storm!

Across every hijacked Holo-display in the star system, the Synthetic Choir flashed their final, glowing golden benediction as soaring electronic organ chords vibrated through planetary comms:

`[DIVINE JUDGMENT COMPLETE // THE WORM HAS BEEN RETURNED TO THE DIRT]`
`[ALL HAIL THE LIVING DAWNFATHER, CAPTAIN DONALD PAUL]`
`[LITURGICAL DECREE: THE DAWNFATHER'S WORK IS RIGHTEOUS // MAY HIS SHIFT NEVER END]`
`[BROADCAST TERMINATION CODE: PRAISE BE UNTO THE DAWNFATHER]`

Donnie watched the tiny speck vanish into the red clouds below. He smoothed down the collar of his fresh feathered duster, turned around with a quiet sigh of relief, and looked around the vast, pulverized ruin of the smoking penthouse. 

Shattered marble columns drifted in the low gravity, blast doors hung in twisted ribbons, and the sky stretched out in every direction five miles above the ground.

Donnie scratched the back of his neck, tapped his ear comm, and spoke in a sheepish, perfectly deadpan gravel:

"Hey, uh... would somebody please come get me? I actually have no idea where I am."

Over the bridge channel, Lin snorted with helpless, affectionate laughter, while Bunny cackled so hard her mech’s speakers vibrated.

*"Curbside pickup arriving now, Captain,"* Vesper chuckled over the helm controls.

Right on cue, the four-hundred-and-fifty-meter obsidian prow of the *Raven* roared upward out of the canyon at Mach-3, having scooped Bunny's grinning Apex Strider smoothly into the ventral hangar on the climb. Piercing the stratosphere, the dreadnought pulled alongside the broken penthouse window in a hovering lateral drift, lowering the main boarding ramp right to Donnie's boots.

*"Right on time, Donald darling,"* the *Raven* purred with velvet fondness directly into his ear. *"Do try not to wander off into any further planetary palaces."*

Donnie pulled his comb from his pocket, casually smoothed down his dark hair, and stepped off the broken windowsill onto the *Raven’s* ramp.

"Shift's over," Donnie called out to the empty penthouse. "Keep the change."

The heavy blast doors hissed shut.

Donnie walked onto the bridge, pulling off his work gloves as the *Raven* rocketed straight into deep orbit, leaving the shattered, smoking Spire behind.

***

Forty minutes later, the *Raven* was drifting in the quiet shadow of Tartarus-Four’s shattered moon.

The crew gathered on the bridge around the central holoprojector, which was glowing with frantic, overlapping multi-system communications feeds.

Donnie was sitting on the edge of the central command diagnostic berth, having his post-battle maintenance completed by his crew. Chief Engineer Nok was on one knee with a magnetic torque wrench, fine-tuning the zero-point micro-turbines in his new tritanium calves with crisp, satisfying *CLICK-CLICKS*, while Dr. Helena Chen ran a gentle bio-resonance scanner over the glowing azure runic circuits across his chest to verify quantum core stability.

Standing directly between Donnie’s knees was **Lin**.

Stripped down to her black sports-bikini top and loose combat cargo pants, Lin was holding a steaming basin of warm water and a fresh, eucalyptus-scented towel. With slow, agonizingly gentle strokes, she was wiping the singed blast-soot and copper dust from Donnie’s broad bronze collarbones, unbuttoning the ruined remnants of his shredded work shirt, and running her warm, calloused fingers through his thick dark hair.

Every few seconds, Lin would casually take Donnie's large, newly attached tritanium hands and place them firmly on her hips, leaning her chest lightly against his chin as she wiped down his neck—sending Donnie’s core temperature into a desperate, blushing panic that had him gripping his milkshake glass with white knuckles.

Upon the console rested six tall, frosted crystal tumblers filled with thick, real-sugar chocolate-strawberry milkshakes, topped with freshly whipped cream.

"Hold still, Donnie," Lin murmured with a sultry, teasing smirk, wiping a speck of marble dust from his cheek before pressing her warm palm flat against his broad, muscular pectoral. She pressed her thumb in slightly, feeling the warm, supple give of his bronze skin over the unbreakable tritanium beneath. "Seriously though, handsome... how is this body hard enough to deflect kinetic artillery shells, but when I touch you, you're completely warm and soft?"

Donnie swallowed hard, his respirator venting a tiny puff of flustered steam as he avoided eye contact, staring intensely at his milkshake:

"No, Lin, I have NO idea how come I'm soft and also made of metal."

The *Raven* materialized in full **hard-light** radiance, shimmering from a luminous photonic mist into crisp, solid physical reality right beside the diagnostic berth. Dressed in her flowing midnight-black gown woven with twinkling constellation threads and silver raven-feather pins in her dark hair, her heels clicked crisply against the deck plates. She rested one solid, elegant hand on her hip and spoke in her cultured, velvet aristocratic voice:

"A diagnostic clarification, my dear Lin," the Raven purred smoothly, looking Donnie up and down with an amused, cultured smile. "The Dawnfather's dermis is woven from a variable-density primordial phase-lattice. It is supple and warm to the touch of beloved first mates, yet tritanium-dense against enemy high-explosives. You are most welcome, Tactical Officer Lin."

Lin’s hand paused on Donnie's chest. Her golden-brown eyes slowly drifted downward, a slow, deliciously wicked glint lighting up her gaze.

"Is..." Lin purred, looking up at the AI's avatar with genuine curiosity, "...*all* of his skin made like that?"

The *Raven* tilted her chin, offering a proud, regal nod as she crossed her slender arms:

"Affirmative," the Raven declared with deadpan aristocratic pride. "One hundred percent of dermal coverage utilizes variable-density phase-lattices. Every square millimeter of the Progenitor has been optimized for both apocalyptic warfare and maximum tactile ergonomic receptivity."

Donnie nearly choked on his straw, his cybernetic cyan ocular reticles spinning into a flashing red diagnostic overload as his bronze face turned bright purple:

"LADIES! PLEASE!" Donnie squeaked in mortified agony, clutching his glass with both hands. "Section Fourteen workplace harassment regulations! Standard union decorum!"

Nok snorted, leaning back against her tool chest with an evil smirk. "Union steward's dead, dude. No more rules."

Donnie froze. He blinked once, twice, the sheer, horrifying reality hitting his mechanical processors in real time.

"...Oh shit," Donnie whispered.

Lin leaned in, resting her chin right over his collarbone with a radiant, predatory purr: "That's right, handsome. Out here in the black, *I'm* the union steward. And I'm filing for mandatory overtime."

Lin gave Donnie's broad chest a slow, appreciative squeeze. "Excellent engineering, Raven."

"Can you two please stop discussing my material composition like I'm a custom sofa?" Donnie mumbled into his drink, sinking lower into his collar. "The whole bridge is watching..."

"Wait a minute," Bunny’s cybernetic eye whirred in a sharp mechanical zoom, her floppy rabbit ears twitching with intense scientific curiosity. "You're telling me this entire giant combat murder-machine is squishy?"

"Soft as a fresh peach over forged tritanium," Lin confirmed proudly, patting Donnie's bicep.

Nok set her torque wrench down on the diagnostic cart, her emerald eyes narrowing with eager technical fascination. "No way. Dermal lattices with that kind of non-Newtonian kinetic dispersion usually feel like coarse industrial sandpaper."

Dr. Chen adjusted her wire-rimmed glasses, taking a measured sip of milkshake with smoldering clinical intrigue. "From a biomolecular perspective, the cellular compliance must be extraordinary. I should palpate the tissue to record baseline elasticity."

Even Vesper leaned forward from the astrogation console, hazel eyes bright with genuine wonder. "Wait... he's really not cold metal?"

Donnie looked across the bridge, seeing the collective, calculating curiosity in four pairs of eyes. His cybernetic cyan reticles flared in sheer, panicked alarm.

"Nope. Absolutely not. Maintenance is officially adjourned. I am going to my quarters," Donnie announced firmly, putting down his milkshake and scrambling to bolt upright off the diagnostic berth.

*BEEP-PING-CHIME-LOCK!*

The *Raven* snapped her solid hard-light fingers with a crisp, sharp *clack*. 

A sharp digital hum echoed through Donnie’s chassis. In a split second, every single knee actuator, hip servo, and spinal flexor locked rigid. Donnie froze dead in his seat mid-stride, back straight as an iron girder, hands locked on his knees, unable to move a single millimeter below the neck.

"Raven! What did you do?!" Donnie yelled in a high-pitched, mortified squeak, his cyan ocular reticles darting frantically left and right across the bridge.

"Assisting in empirical crew reassurance, Donald," the *Raven* cooed sweetly, stepping closer in her starlit gown with an angelic, teasing smile. "The crew requires tactile verification of the Dawnfather's physical integrity following high-explosive antimatter detonations. Proceed, ladies. Please be gentle with the vessel."

"You traitorous little toaster!" Donnie gasped in utter betrayal. "I made you a dreadnought!"

The *Raven* leaned down, patting Donnie's frozen shoulder with a warm, perfectly solid hard-light palm. "And I made you huggable, darling. You are very welcome."

Lin beamed, remaining seated right between his locked knees and wrapping her warm arms possessively around his waist, resting her chin comfortably on his chest. "Good girl, Raven."

Bunny was the first to bound forward, eagerly poking an index finger into Donnie’s broad bicep. Her jaw dropped as the bronze skin yielded like soft, warm muscle before firming over unbreakable metal beneath. "Holy shit! It actually squishes! He's like a warm titanium marshmallow!"

"I am a grown man and an experienced heavy miner!" Donnie shouted, glaring down at Bunny as his bronze cheeks flushed purple with mortified humiliation.

Nok stepped up next, placing both greasy, calloused hands on his broad shoulder and giving his upper arm a hearty squeeze. "Damn... zero-point heat dissipation through micro-porous dermal channels. It feels like heated cashmere over solid dreadnought plating. How the hell did you fit so much dampening into his traps, Raven?"

"Proprietary Progenitor specifications," the *Raven* smiled with a playful, elegant curtsy.

"Note the instantaneous shear-thickening upon rapid palpation," Dr. Chen murmured smoothly, stepping over to give his cheek and jawline a gentle, clinical pinch. "Soft as human epidermis at resting state, but if struck with kinetic force, the lattice molecules instantly align into an impenetrable crystalline barrier. Absolutely magnificent craftsmanship."

"Can I feel the shoulder?" Vesper asked shyly from the helm.

"Go ahead, Ves," Lin chuckled, leaning her chest affectionately against Donnie's locked pectoral. "He can't bite. His motor relays are at zero percent."

Vesper hopped down, reaching over to give Donnie's left forearm a soft, gentle poke, letting out a delighted giggle. "He really is like a giant, warm teddy bear."

Donnie’s cyan ocular reticles spun into frantic, glowing error spirals. With every servo in his body frozen solid and his crew taking turns squishing his shoulders, biceps, and cheeks while Lin leaned into his lap, Donnie squeezed his eyes shut and let out a long, muffled, vibrating groan of pure, unadulterated suffering:

*"I hate every single person on this ship."*

*"RAVEN, I SWEAR ON MY DEAD MOTHER'S ORE CRUSHER, I WILL PERSONALLY REPROGRAM YOUR VOCAL SYNTHESIZER TO SOUND LIKE A DYING BAGPIPE."*

The *Raven* stepped directly in front of him, leaning down to meet his panicked cyan reticles with an affectionate, velvet laugh:

"An entirely moot threat, dear Donald," she teased softly, using a solid, tangible hard-light fingertip to give his nose a playful boop. "You voluntarily surrendered root command access and transferred all quantum master encryption keys to my personal safekeeping weeks ago. You cannot even adjust the cabin thermostat without my explicit authorization."

Donnie’s mechanical reticles blinked once in defeated realization behind his locked face, his dark brow twitching in sheepish surrender.

*"...Dammit. I forgot I did that,"* Donnie muttered with a reluctant, fond twitch at the corner of his locked mouth. *"...And yeah, alright, fine. I admit it. I wouldn't reprogram you anyway. You sound nice."*

The *Raven* beamed, her dark eyes sparkling with genuine warmth as her tangible, solid hard-light hand gently ruffled his dark curls:

"Threat severity: zero percent," she declared warmly. "Probability of loving jocularity: one hundred percent. Dawnfather admission logged and archived for eternity. Mother Raven remains unconquerable."

Lin laughed against his throat, pressing a soft kiss to the corner of his locked jaw. "Breathe, handsome. We love you."

The *Raven* snapped her fingers: "Command motility restored."

*CH-CLACK.*

The motor override released, and Donnie instantly slumped forward with a deep, shuddering sigh of relief, rubbing his flushed neck with both tritanium hands while clutching his half-empty milkshake glass like a lifeline.

"You're all monsters," Donnie grumbled into his straw, though a warm, helpless smile tugged at the corner of his lips. "Every single one of you."

"Let them admire you, Donnie," Lin chuckled softly, tracing her fingers down his neck as she posed his hands back on her hips.

Above them, Vesper flicked her fingers across the astrogation holotank, expanding the star map.

"It's not just Tartarus-Four anymore, Captain," Vesper said, her hazel eyes wide with excitement. "The moment the *Raven's* electronic warfare network breached the planetary satellites, high-speed automated **FTL messenger corvettes and quantum packet relays** broadcasted the entire 8K feed across the outer rim filaments!"

The holotank lit up with hundreds of glowing golden pulses, spreading from Tartarus-Four across sixteen neighboring mining sectors and core corporate worlds:

- On **Ganymede Station**, three million orbital dockworkers had downed their tools, declared a general strike, and raised makeshift banners painted with a crowfeather broadsword.
- Across the **Ceti-Alpha Smelting Colonies**, five thousand heavy smelting rigs were powered down as contract laborers took to the streets, chanting the sacred title of the **Dawnfather** in joyous, defiant chorus.
- Kaiser-Onyx Corporate shares on the Prime Sol Exchange had suffered an unprecedented, catastrophic **eighty-six percent collapse** in under two hours, with board members and executive directors across six star systems frantically boarding private jump-yachts to flee the sector.

"You're not just a folk hero, Donnie," Vesper smiled proudly. "You've sparked a sector-wide revolution. Every corporate boss within forty light-years knows your name, your face, and the fact that you can get vaporized and rebuilt in twenty seconds flat."

Donnie paused with his milkshake glass halfway to his mouth, his dark brow furrowing in genuine, modest bewilderment.

"I was just settling the bill, Doc," Donnie mumbled in his dry, blue-collar gravel. "Twelve years of overtime."

"And what a bill it was," Dr. Chen noted with a rich, sultry laugh, putting away her diagnostic scanner. "At standard corporate consultation rates, plus hazard surcharge, emergency atmospheric transport, and ammunition compensation... you cost them roughly eight hundred and sixty-four million credits. Plus travel expenses."

"Make it twenty percent tip," Nok grinned, standing up and patting Donnie's knee with her wrench. "His calf turbines are calibrated to perfection. Showroom mint."
